# FINDINGS

Append-only. One line per finding: date, one sentence, the commit or the file
that holds the detail. Newest at the bottom.

**Why this file exists.** In the run of 2026-08-18/19 the same
`figure-provenance` defect was found independently by **five** lanes, the same
three packets were derived from two directions by two lanes, and the
sound-arithmetic figure rotted in three places. Nothing made one lane's finding
visible to the next, because findings land in `docs/` prose and a lane reads the
routing table rather than everything anyone learned last night. Five lanes
finding one defect is four lanes spent on a rediscovery at area-lane cost.

**How to use it.** Every brief points here. Every lane reads it before starting
and appends to it when it finds something another lane could plausibly hit.
Append only, so there is no merge conflict worth the name: add your line at the
bottom, never edit or reorder somebody else's.

**What belongs here.** A defect class, a retracted number, an instrument that
measured the wrong thing, a dead end with its reason, a constraint that cost
time. Not progress reports, not "ported X", not anything already legible from
`git log`.

**What does not.** Detail. One sentence and a pointer. The mechanism goes in
`docs/` and the commit message, as it always has.

**Why the marker below.** Every lane appends here and nothing else about the
file changes, so two lanes appending is an add/add hunk at one anchor and git
conflicts on it. Measured 2026-09-05 over the 37 branches ahead of `main` that
touch this file: **659 of their 666 pairs conflict here**, on a file created
2026-08-19 — the day AFTER `.gitattributes`' coverage sweep concluded the
remaining surface was 19 pairs of 1035 and told the next person to reopen it
only with a fresh sweep above ~20. This is that sweep. Do not move or delete
the marker line: `ci/merge-docregions.py --check` runs in T0 and a lost marker
is a red, because the attribute would then promise a behaviour the file no
longer declares.

---

## 2026-08-18 / 19

* `108/108 falsifiable` counted **declarations**, not checks that can move: 61
  of 108 had never moved across 64 recordings, and a check with an empty
  history read identically to one that agreed every build.
* A **null result and an empty result must be different words**. The `frozen`
  count is the second defect from conflating them, and no string check catches
  this class, so it is a rule rather than a gate.
* `ci/unported-inventory.py` ranks by **where work has already been done**: a
  refusal marker needs a host line, so its gap counts correlate with lines
  written at r = 0.760, permutation p = 0.0001. Use `ci/surface-census.py` for
  the other direction.
* `git rev-list --count` counts commit **objects**, not changes. One question,
  four answers: 4,182 / 2,587 / 2,488 / 1,377. Size a backlog with
  `git cherry` plus `git patch-id --stable`.
* `union_N` and `innocent_N` are two nulls moving in **opposite** directions as
  N grows; scored consistently the headline went 14x to 3.53x.
* The nether's flagship window improved until it **stopped adjudicating**:
  2,455 residual against an ensemble floor of 1,709 to 6,133, registered
  `quotable = false` the day it was measured.
* All 41 published overworld figures come from **one map location** holding 18
  of 55 biomes; of 22 biome readings across three squares, exactly one cleared
  its floor.
* `551-attacking-from-out-of-range` is not a divergence: vanilla is near a coin
  flip (4 in 32 runs, 5 in 39) and we are pinned at five. **A single-run
  comparison cannot see "we are deterministic where vanilla is not."**
* The reference cache stored **four entries and hit approximately never**: its
  key hashes the scenario set and every branch edits scenarios. The 43.3 to
  21.6 min figure was measured re-gating the same tree.
* Batching measured **1.725x on the real queue** (35.0 h against 60.4 h), not
  the idealised 2.3x, and is still disabled.
* Corpus-lock exclusivity is **unexplained rather than justified**: concurrency
  measured at most 1.3% slowdown (95% CI 0.994 to 1.013, N=16/arm), integrity
  0/8 in every arm, and the founding incidents had per-tree ports already. It
  parked six recordings at once and cost 450 agent-minutes in one measurement.
* Hand-rolled waiters were the run's largest single waste: empty wakes at 615k
  and 361k tokens, repeatedly. `ci/run-detached.sh` is the fix and is **not on
  `main`** (`origin/docs-waiter-t2-blindspot`).
* `crates/rustmc-server/src/recipes.rs` loads 1,056 of 1,585 recipes, so
  `docs/SERVER.md`'s "read by nothing" line about `data/vanilla/recipes.json`
  is **stale**.
* ~~The worldgen dump places 8 of 20 structure sets and the running server
  places none of them.~~ **RETIRED 2026-09-05, refuted by measurement.** The
  running server places six overworld sets, because
  `World::generate_through` calls `worldgen_assembly::generate::generate`, the
  dump's own entry point. The dump and the server were already one
  configuration. `crates/rustmc-server/tests/served_structures.rs` decodes the
  chunks the packet writer is handed and finds, at seed 0: 341 `rail` + 199
  `cobweb` (mineshaft) and 15 `trial_spawner` + 19 `vault` (trial chamber) at
  centre (14,5) r=3; 729 `netherrack` + 1 `gold_block` (ruined portal) at
  (20,9) r=1; 647 `dirt_path` + 353 `farmland` + 276 `wheat` (savanna village)
  at (38,45) r=3; all zero in the `Through::Carvers` control. The world a
  player joins — `WORLD_SEED` 0, `WORLD_RADIUS` 10 — carries 45 `rail` and 14
  `cobweb`.
* **Every published floor in this repo is an unclamped floor.**
  `-Dmax.bg.threads=1` determinises a vanilla harvest without modifying
  vanilla; `ci/gen-vanilla-region.sh` has stamped `jvm_opts` in
  `HARVEST-ORDER` since 2026-08-18 and nothing has been measured with it set.
* SteelMC's parity reference **is** a modified vanilla (extractor patched to
  reset caches per chunk, fix generation order, order hash collections) **and
  they published that themselves**, so a launch line implying concealment does
  not survive the reader clicking through. `docs/ASSESSMENT-2026-08-19.md` 2.3.
* SteelMC's published benchmark is 10,201 chunks in 3.98 s on a 9950X, about
  75x our r=7 wall figure. Different radius, hardware and mask, so it is a flag
  and not a verdict, but our generator peaks at **1.00 cores**. Check it with
  their released binary before publishing any speed claim.
* 2026-09-04 `Mth.atan2`'s `COS_TAB[61]` built with glibc's `cos` is one ulp off the JVM's fdlibm value (4606922977173859056 vs 4606922977173859057), so a port that rebuilds the tables at runtime turns every mob's head a hair differently; the JVM's bits are embedded in `crates/rustmc-entity/src/mth_tables.rs` and `tests/golden_mob_math.rs` matches 2014 `atan2` inputs bit for bit. `ci/mob-math-golden.sh`, branch `mobs-goals`.
* 2026-09-04 The 26.2 server jar is signed per package: a golden placed in `net.minecraft.world.level.pathfinder` fails at load with `signer information does not match`; goldens that subclass jar classes go in their own package (`rustmc.golden`), protected access still works. `tools/goldengen/vanilla/MobPathGolden.java`.
* 2026-09-04 `PathFinder` can be driven from the jar with no server: `findPath(region, mob, ...)` hands both only to `NodeEvaluator.prepare`, so a grid `NodeEvaluator` subclass exercises the real A*, `BinaryHeap`, `Node`, `Target` and `Path`. 15 grids incl. 15-target hash-walk ties, a 200-node heap replay with changeCost and remove, and `createHash` match bit for bit (g, h, f, walkedDistance). `ci/mob-path-golden.sh`, `tests/golden_mob_path.rs`, branch `mobs-goals`.
* 2026-09-04 `PathfinderMob.getWalkTargetValue` is `fconst_0` in the base class and `RandomPos.generateRandomPos` keeps the best of ten with a strict `>`, so for every mob that does not override it the stroll target is the FIRST stable candidate of ten, not a random one; the ten attempts still draw 3 ints each. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/random_pos.rs`, branch `mobs-goals`.
* 2026-09-04 `PathNavigation.moveTo(x, y, z, speed)` paths with accuracy 1, so a mob told to walk to a block stops one node short of it; `moveTo(Entity, speed)` the same. A corpus scenario that expects the mob on the block will diverge by one. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/navigation.rs`, branch `mobs-goals`.
* 2026-09-04 `LookAtPlayerGoal.canUse` (26.2) assigns `lookAt = mob.getTarget()` and then unconditionally overwrites it with the nearest-player / nearest-entity search (offsets 22..42 then 51..179), so a mob's combat target never influences whom it idly looks at; transcribed as written. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/goals/look_at_player_goal.rs`.
* 2026-09-04 `TargetingConditions.test` asks `Sensing.hasLineOfSight` LAST, after the selector, the combat refusals and the range test, so a target refused earlier never costs a ray cast and never enters the per-tick seen/unseen memo; an implementation that casts first changes which ids the memo holds. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/targeting.rs`.
* 2026-09-04 `ci/readiness-falsify.sh` reds T0 on a race of its own: it snapshots the booting server's log every 0.1 s and calls the last pre-`listening on` copy "still starting", so when the bind lands inside one poll the snapshot already carries the announcement and the script prints `the still-starting capture already contains the announcement` (state 2 unmeasured). Seen on `mobs-goals` at `rustmc-mobs-goals` T0 run 2 (log `~/grit/work-mobs-t0-2.log:212`), a branch that touches no server code; run 1 on the same tree did not hit it. A branch-independent flake, not a verdict.
* 2026-09-05 `CatLieOnBedGoal` sets `verticalSearchStart = -2` and `MoveToBlockGoal.findNearestBlock` steps `k` as `k > 0 ? -k : 1 - k`, so from -2 the walk is `-2, 3, -3, 4, -4, 5, -5, 6, -6` and the y offsets (`k - 1`) are `-3, 2, -4, 3, ...`: a bed level with the cat's feet (offsets -1, 0, 1) is never a candidate; the first ring searched is two blocks up. `MoveToBlockGoal.class` offsets 185..200, `CatLieOnBedGoal.<init>` offset 16. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/goals/move_to_block_goal.rs`, test `cat_lie_on_bed_skips_its_own_level`, branch `mobs-goals-2`.
* 2026-09-05 `StrollThroughVillageGoal.canContinueToUse` requires `navigation.getTargetPos().equals(wantedPos)` while `tick` only ever paths to a point ten blocks along the line (offsets 44..146) or a random one, so the goal ends one tick after it starts and only its `nextInt(interval)` draw re-arms it. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/goals/stroll_through_village_goal.rs`, branch `mobs-goals-2`.
* 2026-09-05 `NonTameRandomTargetGoal.canContinueToUse` branches on `targetConditions != null` (offsets 1..4) but the superclass constructor always assigns it, so the `super.canContinueToUse()` arm is dead: a wild cat, ocelot or wolf keeps its target exactly as long as the conditions hold against the goal's own `target` field, with no unseen-memory ticks. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/goals/non_tame_random_target_goal.rs`, branch `mobs-goals-2`.
* 2026-09-05 `DefendVillageTargetGoal.potentialTarget` is a field written by the villager/player double loop and never cleared (offsets 136..149, no store on the miss path), so a player once hated at reputation <= -100 stays the golem's answer on every later `canUse` until another overwrites it; the last match in iteration order wins. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/goals/defend_village_target_goal.rs`, branch `mobs-goals-2`.
* 2026-09-05 `NearestHealableRaiderTargetGoal` is built with `randomInterval = 500` but overrides `canUse` without calling `super.canUse()`, so the `nextInt(250)` gate never fires; its gate is one `nextBoolean` per tick (offset 14) while `cooldown <= 0`, and `start` sets `cooldown = reducedTickDelay(200) = 100`. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/goals/nearest_healable_raider_target_goal.rs`, branch `mobs-goals-2`.
* 2026-09-05 `RemoveBlockGoal.canUse` sets `nextStartTick = reducedTickDelay(20)` (10, not `WAIT_AFTER_BLOCK_FOUND`'s 20) after a find, and `stop` writes `fallDistance = 1.0`; the egg-breaking timeline is a 0.3 upward kick with three `nextFloat` particle offsets each reached tick, a -0.3 kick every second tick, the progress sound every sixth, removal after the sixty-first with twenty poof particles at three `nextGaussian` each. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/goals/move_to_block_goal.rs`, branch `mobs-goals-2`.
* 2026-09-05 `TemptGoal.ForNonPathfinders.navigateTowards` draws `random.nextDouble()` (offset 21) on every navigating tick to scale the step toward the player's eye position, so a squid being tempted consumes one draw per tick that a pathfinding mob does not; `stopNavigation` is `moveControl.setWait()`. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/goals/tempt_goal.rs`, branch `mobs-goals-2`.
* 2026-09-05 `BlockPos.betweenClosed` iterates x fastest, then y, then z (`BlockPos$4.computeNext`: `index % width`, `index / width % height`, `index / width / height`), and `BlockPos.neighborColumn` iterates offset-major over `(0,0,0), NORTH, EAST, SOUTH, WEST` with all y steps of one offset before the next (`BlockPos$5`); `TryFindWaterGoal` and `BreathAirGoal` take the first match in those orders. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/goals/try_find_water_goal.rs`, `breath_air_goal.rs`, branch `mobs-goals-2`.
* 2026-09-05 `HoverRandomPos.getPos(mob, 8, 7, dx, dz, angle, 3, 1)` passes its last two ints as `(yMin, yMax)` in that order and the lambda computes `nextInt(yMax - yMin + 1) + yMin` with the locals swapped, i.e. `nextInt(3) + 1`: the parrot's flying stroll lifts a land candidate one to three blocks above the solid, with the draw AFTER the three direction draws and only when the land candidate survived. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/ai/random_pos.rs::hover_get_pos`, branch `mobs-goals-2`.
* 2026-09-05 The 26.2 jar registers 463 `addGoal` calls from 54 `registerGoals()` bodies naming 168 distinct goal classes: 58 from `net.minecraft.world.entity.ai.goal` (all transcribed) and 110 inner classes of mobs (`Wolf$WolfAvoidEntityGoal`, the illager spells, ...) that this lane did not touch. The count comes from `ci/gen-mob-goal-tables.py` over `javap -p -c -constants` and is pinned by `tests/mob_goal_tables.rs`. `crates/rustmc-entity/golden/mob_goal_tables.json`, branch `mobs-goals-2`.
* 2026-09-05 The supervisor parks a lane's worktree mid-run (`git stash` as `supervisor: parked ...`, then `checkout --detach`), which silently reverted seven uncommitted goal files and three tracked edits in `~/grit/work-mobs` at 02:24 while the lane was between a failed test and its fix; `git stash pop` recovered all of it. A lane that reads `git status` clean after a test run should check `git stash list` before concluding its edits never happened. Branch `mobs-goals-2`.
* 2026-09-05 26.2 has no `Schedule` class: `Brain.schedule` is an `EnvironmentAttribute<Activity>` (`gameplay/villager_activity`, `gameplay/baby_villager_activity`, default `IDLE`) and the villager day is **data** — `data/minecraft/timeline/villager_schedule.json` (10 idle, 2000 work, 9000 meet, 11000 idle, 12000 rest; baby 10 idle, 3000 play, 6000 idle, 10000 play, 12000 rest; period 24000) sampled by `KeyframeTrackSampler` under `LerpFunction.ofStep(1.0f)`, which is the legacy "last keyframe at or before `dayTime % 24000`, wrapping" rule. Golden `crates/rustmc-entity/golden/villager_schedule.json`; `crates/rustmc-entity/src/brain/schedule.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `Brain.availableBehaviorsByPriority` is `TreeMap<Integer, HashMap<Activity, LinkedHashSet<BehaviorControl>>>` (`Brain.<init>` offsets 18..22, `lambda$addActivity$0/$1`), and `Activity.hashCode()` is `name.hashCode()`: at one priority the walk over active activities is the Java `HashMap` bucket order of the String hashes (core 0x2EAF9F → bucket 1, idle 0x313FD4 → bucket 5 on a table of 16), reproduced by `brain::activity::java_hash_map_order`; `Brain.memories` is identity-hashed but nothing observable walks it in order (`brain/memory.rs`).
* 2026-09-05 `Behavior.tryStart` draws `endTimestamp = time + minDuration + level.getRandom().nextInt(maxDuration + 1 - minDuration)` from the **level** random on every successful start (offsets 24..48), so a fixed-duration behaviour still consumes one `nextInt(1)`; `DoNothing.tryStart` does the same and always starts; `MoveToTargetSink.stop` draws `nextInt(40)` from the level random only when stuck. `Swim.tick` and `ShufflingList.shuffle` draw from the mob's and the list's own random respectively. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/brain/behavior.rs:178`.
* 2026-09-05 `Sensor.<clinit>` builds six static `TargetingConditions` and `Sensor.tick` mutates all six to the ticking mob's `FOLLOW_RANGE` (`updateTargetingConditionRanges`, offsets 0..56) before `doTick`; `isEntityTargetable`/`isEntityAttackable` read them from behaviours outside any sensor tick, so the range used there is whichever mob's sensor ticked last on the server. Kept as one process-wide cell, `brain::sensor::SENSOR_RANGE`, starting at 16.0.
* 2026-09-05 `Sensor.tick` is `if (--timeToTick <= 0)`, so a delay draw of `d` (mob random, `nextInt(scanRate)` in `Brain.<init>`) first fires on brain tick `d` (tick 1 for `d = 0`) and every `scanRate` after; `crates/rustmc-entity/src/brain/sensor.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `ShufflingList` seeds its own `RandomSource.create()` (`RandomSupport.generateUniqueSeed`), so every `SHUFFLED` gate (`RunOne`, `TriggerGate.triggerOneShuffled`) in vanilla is unreproducible from game state; the port takes the seed as a constructor argument. `shuffle` is one `nextFloat` per entry in list order, key `-Math.pow((double) f, (double) (1.0f / (float) weight))`, stable ascending sort.
* 2026-09-05 `DOORS_TO_CLOSE` is a `HashSet<GlobalPos>` and `GlobalPos`'s record hash mixes `ResourceKey.hashCode()`, which is identity-based, so the order `InteractWithDoor.closeDoorsThatIHaveOpenedOrPassedThrough` walks pending doors varies between JVM runs; ported in insertion order. Only that loop reads the set.
* 2026-09-05 `Brain.setMemory` clears the slot when the value is an **empty `Collection`** (`isEmptyCollection`, offsets 12..21 of `setMemoryInternal`), so `NearestLivingEntitySensor`/`PlayerSensor` leave `NEAREST_LIVING_ENTITIES`/`NEAREST_PLAYERS` absent with nobody around while an empty `NearestVisibleLivingEntities` (not a `Collection`) is stored; `BehaviorBuilder` conditions read `getMemoryInternal`, which is `null` for an unregistered type, so even `absent(X)` fails when `X` is not registered in that brain.
* 2026-09-05 `mobs-goals-2`'s `Cargo.lock` lacked the `rustmc-worldgen` and `serde_json` entries its `rustmc-entity/Cargo.toml` declares (and `rustmc-server`'s `rustmc-data`); committed on `mobs-brains` as `0dd70a232`.
* 2026-09-05 26.2 builds brains through `Brain$Provider` + `Brain$ActivitySupplier` lambdas, not per-mob `makeBrain` bodies: `Villager.<clinit>` calls the two-argument `Brain.provider(List.of(9 SensorTypes), Villager::lambda$static$0)` with **no memory-type list** (memories come from the sensors' `requires()` and each behaviour's `getRequiredMemories()`), while `Piglin` uses the three-argument form with six memories and five sensors. 20 classes call `Brain.provider`; `crates/rustmc-entity/golden/mob_brain_tables.json`, `ci/gen-brain-tables.py`.
* 2026-09-05 `TriggerGate.triggerGate`'s trigger (`lambda$triggerGate$2`, offsets 0..69) returns `true` whether or not any child fired, so a `triggerOneShuffled` one-shot always counts as started; `SetWalkTargetFromAttackTargetIfTargetOutOfReach`, `StopAttackingIfTargetInvalid`, `GoToTargetLocation`, `StopBeingAngryIfTargetDead` and `SetWalkTargetAwayFrom` (once past its gates) likewise always answer `true`. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/brain/gate.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `SetWalkTargetFromBlockMemory` (offsets 19..27) tests `gp.dimension() == level.dimension()` only on its give-up branch; a `GlobalPos` from another dimension still falls through to the Manhattan-distance branches against this level's coordinates. `AnimalPanic.lookForWater` uses `squareOutSouthEast(pos).allMatch(water)` for a 2-wide mob and `findClosestMatch(pos, 5, 1)`, unlike `PanicGoal`'s single-block test. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/brain/behaviors/set_walk_target_from_block_memory.rs`, `animal_panic.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `crates/rustmc-loot`'s headline figure rested on two fixtures no committed step could make: `data/vanilla/loot_tables.json` (1,355 table documents) and `data/vanilla/item_tags.json` (224 flattened tags) existed in exactly one worktree on this box and `ci/loot-golden.sh` wrote neither, so from a clean checkout two of the crate's three test targets panic on a missing file. The generator is in `ci/loot-golden.sh` now and reproduces both hand-made files exactly (key sets and values, both directions), and the crate's three targets run in T0 in 1.26 s. `ci/checks/test-targets.toml` bucket `loot-interpreter`.
* 2026-09-05 `ClientboundCommandsPacket.enumerateNodes` deduplicates by Brigadier's **structural** equality, not identity, and nothing names it: it collects into an `Object2IntOpenHashMap`, and `CommandNode.equals` compares only the children map and the `Command` object — not the requirement, not the redirect, not the suggestion provider. At `OWNERS`, where the permission filter drops nothing, vanilla's 2,085-node dispatcher becomes a **2,017-entry** packet; all 68 collapsed nodes are in `/execute store {result,success} {block,entity,storage} <path> {byte,short,int,long,float,double} [scale]`. An encoder deduplicating by identity emits a packet 694 bytes too long. `crates/rustmc-server/src/commandtree.rs`, `Graph::class_of`.
* 2026-09-05 `ArgumentType.equals` is by value for only four of Brigadier's six primitives — `integer`, `long`, `float`, `double` — and identity for `bool`, `string` and every `net.minecraft.commands.arguments` type, so only those four can make two argument nodes merge. A non-capturing `c -> 0` in a golden registration is one cached lambda per call site and would merge 94 boolean nodes into one; vanilla's are `ctx -> setRule(ctx, rule)` and capture. `tools/goldengen/vanilla/CommandsGolden.java`.
* 2026-09-05 **Vanilla's own `ClientboundCommandsPacket` is not byte-stable for `/gamerule`.** Sixteen consecutive JVM runs of one dumper against the pinned jar: fifteen produced one document and one produced another, differing in 160 of 2,085 nodes, every one a `/gamerule` child, permuted — packet lengths identical at all five levels. `GameRuleCommand.register` walks `new GameRules(ctx.enabledFeatures())` with `visitGameRuleTypes` and that iteration is not stably ordered. So there is no byte-exact golden of that subtree to take, and a port that reproduced one run's order would be gated against a coin flip. `ci/command-tree-compare.py`.
* 2026-09-05 `ci/command-tree-extract.sh` reported that instability as "the dump is not deterministic", a claim about vanilla, and its `run()` swallowed java's exit status and its stderr — so a JVM that died mid-document produced the same message. Both fixed; the comparison now names what moved and refuses anything that is not a permutation under a named root.
* 2026-09-05 `data/vanilla/update_shape.json`'s per-reason block lists were capped at 8 by `ShapeGolden` (`if (blocks.size() < 8)`), so six of its eleven refusal reasons reported exactly 8 blocks and the union read as 37 distinct blocks. Uncapped it is **258**, a 7x correction, and the 8-cap made every block figure derived from that file a truncation. Pair counts were never affected. `tools/goldengen/vanilla/ShapeGolden.java`.
* 2026-09-05 `docs/EXTRACTABILITY.md` §2.3's `update_shape` figure of 48,405 pairs does not reproduce: the current extract says **48,993** for the dominant refusal (`updateShape` read `0,1,0`), of **79,111** refused of 103,318 sensitive pairs over 11 reasons, with 12,716 answered and 11,491 connective. `docs/SERVER.md` and `docs/WORKLIST.md` already carried 48,993; §2.3 was the stale copy.
* 2026-09-05 The unblocked-root count in `docs/EXTRACTABILITY.md` §2.1 does not reproduce as 38. Its other two figures do exactly — 36 of 90 roots take an `EntityArgument` somewhere, and the registry groups are 5 players / 4 blocks / 4 nbt / 4 scoreboard — but sweeping the same subtrees for any subsystem-bearing parser gives **35** free, and **30** once redirects are followed (`tp`, `tm`, `w` and `xp` are pure redirects into entity-bearing subtrees and are only free if the walk stops at the redirect). Method and command in `docs/cold-start/stack-extract.md`.
* 2026-09-05 **A falsifier's injection can stop injecting, and only one of `ci/command-surface-falsify.py`'s nine mutators would have said so.** `surface-edited-by-hand` perturbed `docs/generated/command-surface.tsv` with `replace("\tno\t", "\tyes\t", 1)` on the row starting `gamerule\t`; this stack's `/gamerule` port flipped that row's `ported` cell to `yes`, the row then held no `\tno\t`, the file was rewritten byte-identical and `--check` correctly found no drift. It announced itself only because its expected rc (1) differed from the unperturbed one (0) — a case expecting rc=0, or one whose refusal fires for another reason, would have gone inert in silence. The mutator is now addressed by column, and `main` snapshots the fixture by content around every mutator and FAILs a case whose injection changed nothing. `ci/command-surface-falsify.py`.
* 2026-09-05 **`Tree::resolve` was a model of Brigadier, not a transcription, and the model forecloses whole command classes rather than approximating them.** It split on whitespace and gave each argument node one token; `minecraft:block_pos` is three tokens in one node, `minecraft:message` and greedy `brigadier:string` are every remaining token in one node, and `stone[facing=north]` is one node whose argument ends after `stone`. `/setblock`, `/say`, `/me`, `/msg`, `/tellraw`, `/give` and `/help` were unreachable independently of their executors. Replaced by `crates/rustmc-server/src/cmdreader.rs`, a transcription of brigadier-1.3.10's `StringReader`, and a `parse_nodes` transcription of `CommandDispatcher.parseNodes`.
* 2026-09-05 **Brigadier does not tolerate extra whitespace, and `commandtree::tests::resolution_matches_whole_tokens` asserted it did** — with the comment "Brigadier tolerates it the same way". `parseNodes` skips exactly one separator character (one `skip()`, offset 219) and `Commands.trimOptionalPrefix` removes a leading `/` and nothing else; there is no `trim` anywhere between the packet and the dispatcher. A doubled, leading or trailing space is a syntax error on a real 26.2 server. The old assertion was a property of the tokeniser under test written down as a property of vanilla.
* 2026-09-05 **The three command dumpers in `ci/` all asked about the tree a client is SHOWN; none constrained what happens when a player types a command INTO it**, so the whole of `Parser::parse` shipped ungated. `ci/command-parse-golden.sh` records `(accepted, StringReader.getCursor())` for 27 parser configurations x 107 inputs from vanilla's own `ArgumentType.parse`. The cursor is the quantity that matters: a parser one character over leaves the NEXT node reading the wrong input, and the answer is "unknown command" for a command that is present, correctly spelled and whose executor works. 2,645 agreed / 0 disagreed / 244 unread; the positive control requires a deliberately one-character-wrong parser to be caught on every row that has a character left to take.
* 2026-09-05 `WorldCoordinates.parseInt`'s separator rule needs **more input AND a space**, not "a space if there is any input" (`10: canRead / 14: ifeq 26 / 23: if_icmpeq 39 / 26: setCursor(start); throw` — `!canRead` jumps to the same error branch a non-space does). The permissive reading passes accept/reject on all 186 coordinate rows and disagrees with vanilla's CURSOR on 29 of them: `minecraft:block_pos` on `"1 2"` leaves vanilla at 0 and the permissive port at 3. Caught by the golden, not by reading.
* 2026-09-05 A **relative** coordinate reads a `double` even under `WorldCoordinates.parseInt` (offset 60 branches on `isRelative` and takes `readInt` only on the absolute side), so `/setblock ~1.5 ~ ~ stone` parses. A `^` inside a `WorldCoordinate` is `ERROR_MIXED_TYPE`, not a local coordinate. The failure rewind in both `WorldCoordinates` and `LocalCoordinates` goes to the start of the **triple**, not of the component.
* 2026-09-05 There are **six** entity selector types, not five: `parseSelector`'s `tableswitch` (97..115) has live arms at a, e, **n**, p, r, s, and `@n` is `SELECTOR_NEAREST_ENTITY`. `parseNameOrUUID` tries `UUID.fromString` FIRST and the player-name branch is its `IllegalArgumentException` handler, so Java's UUID grammar is part of `minecraft:entity`'s accept set: at most 36 characters, exactly four dashes with a fifth forbidden, each component hex with **no width requirement** — `a-b-c-d-e` is valid and `1-2-3-4-5-6` is not.
* 2026-09-05 `MessageArgument` forgives exactly two selector errors — a trailing `@` and an unknown type — setting the cursor to `start + 1` and continuing; every other exception fails the whole message. That is what makes `hello@world` plain text and `@a[bad` a syntax error. `TimeArgument.UNITS` has **four** entries, not three: `""` is in the map mapped to 1, and `getOrDefault(s, 0)` returning 0 is the only route to `argument.time.invalid_unit`, so a three-entry map with `default 1` accepts `/time set 5x` as five ticks.
* 2026-09-05 `Identifier.read` accepts an **empty** run — the empty identifier is a valid parse — so `minecraft:resource_location` rejected nothing at all on a 91-input corpus and `ci/command-parse-golden.sh`'s own "every configuration must reject something" check refused the dump. `isAllowedInIdentifier` also differs from `isAllowedInUnquotedString` in both directions (adds `:` and `/`, drops `A-Z` and `+`), and a **leading** colon is not an empty namespace: `bySeparator` tests `i != 0` before taking one, so `:stone` is `minecraft:stone`.
* 2026-09-05 **`ListTag.write` wraps mismatched elements and this project had been applying the rule by hand at three call sites without naming it.** `identifyRawElementType()` scans every element; any disagreement makes the list type 10, and `wrapIfNeeded` then replaces each non-compound with `{"": tag}` — and **re-wraps a compound that is itself a wrapper** (`size()==1 && contains("")`). So `/list`'s `{"": 2}` is an `IntTag` the writer wrapped because a Component sat beside it, while `/version`'s `commands.version.data` is a bare `TAG_Int` because nothing disagrees with it in that list. A port that always wraps gets the first right and the second wrong. `rustmc_world::nbt`'s writer takes the FIRST element's tag and errors on a heterogeneous list, which is a narrower domain rather than a wrong answer — but it is why every caller hand-wraps. `crates/rustmc-server/src/commands.rs::translation_args`.
* 2026-09-05 `/version`'s reply is **ten** `system_chat` packets and `VersionCommand.dumpVersion` emits only nine: the executor sends `commands.version.header` first and then calls `dumpVersion`. `ci/commands-golden.sh` had dumped `dumpVersion` alone since it was written, so a port gated against it would have sent nine of vanilla's ten packets and passed. The header is in the golden now and is asserted to carry no `with` list.
* 2026-09-05 **`ci/readiness-falsify.sh` carries a timing race that reds a clean branch.** Its state-2 arm snapshots a still-starting server and asserts the announcement is absent; on an idle Threadripper the snapshot loses that race and the run reports `T0 RED — a readiness proof's fixtures are not what the server prints` with three RED lines naming `ci/t2-gameplay.sh`, `ci/t2-gameplay-subset.sh` and `ci/block-entity-subset.sh`. Measured on `commands` at 411605f1: 1 of 4 runs red, the other 3 green with 0 RED lines, on an unchanged tree that touches no `ci/*.sh` wait loop. Not this branch's; it belongs to whoever owns `ci/`.
* 2026-09-05 **The `minecraft:chat_type` registry the client receives is KEY-SORTED, not in `ChatType.bootstrap`'s registration order.** `bootstrap` registers chat, say_command, msg_command_incoming, msg_command_outgoing, team_msg_command_incoming, team_msg_command_outgoing, emote_command; `data/vanilla/registries.bin` carries them sorted, so `say_command` is id **4** and `emote_command` is **1**, not 1 and 6. A wrong id is a well-formed packet that renders as the wrong kind of message and nothing else in the packet constrains it. `minecraft:chat` is first under both orders, which is why the existing `player_chat` work never had to find this out. Written in bootstrap order and refused by the capture on the first run of `chat::tests::the_chat_type_registry_is_the_one_the_client_receives`.
* 2026-09-05 `MessageArgument.resolveComponent` passes `permissions.hasPermission(Permissions.COMMANDS_ENTITY_SELECTORS)` as `allowSelectors`, and `LevelBasedPermissionSet.hasPermission` answers that one `Permission$Atom` specially: **`level >= GAMEMASTERS`**. So a `/me` from a level-0 player does **not** resolve `@a` — vanilla sends the raw text with the selector still in it — while `/say`, which requires GAMEMASTERS, always resolves. This also refines `chat.rs`'s standing note that no class outside `Permissions` references those atoms: it named the four `CHAT_*` ones, and `COMMANDS_ENTITY_SELECTORS` is a fifth that IS referenced, in `MessageArgument` and in `LevelBasedPermissionSet` itself.
* 2026-09-05 **`ci/command-surface.py::ours()` silently dropped two roots through two independent holes, and only the Rust-side backstop caught it.** (a) its call pattern was `\b<name>\(\)` — a builder taking arguments matched neither alternative, its parentheses balanced under the bracket counter, and the root vanished; (b) its builder resolution searched `src[body:]`, unbounded to the end of the file, so a builder with no literal `Node::leaf("x")` of its own adopted the next one further down and two roots both resolved to `gamerule`, which `sorted(set(...))` then deduplicated away. Both are now refusals. Comments are stripped before the scan too: the block's own prose names vanilla registrations like `` `literal("say")` ``, which was enough to trip the new refusal.
* 2026-09-05 **A redirect restarts the resolved path at its target, and that is `parseNodes` rather than a convenience.** Following one builds `new CommandContextBuilder(dispatcher, source, node.getRedirect(), cursor)` — a fresh context rooted at the target — and attaches it with `withChild`; `execute` runs the last child context's command. So `/tell oracle hi` dispatches from a context rooted at `msg` holding `targets` and `message`, and `tell` lives only in the parent. A port that kept the typed literal at the head of the path needs one executor arm per redirect root for the same command, which is what `redirect` exists to avoid. `tell` alone is `execute`'s `context.getCommand() == null` arm, which is `Refusal::Incomplete` here and not `Unknown`.
* 2026-09-05 `MsgCommand.sendMessage` puts **two** `disguised_chat` packets on the wire per target, with two different chat types: the SENDER gets `msg_command_outgoing` bound `withTargetName(target.getDisplayName())`, the target gets `msg_command_incoming` with no target name, in that order inside the loop. `ChatType.bind` leaves the target name empty and only `withTargetName` fills it, so the same message produces two different `ChatType$Bound`s. `EntityArgument.getPlayers` throws `NO_PLAYERS_FOUND` on an empty result, which makes `MsgCommand`'s own `!collection.isEmpty()` guard unreachable.
* 2026-09-05 `ci/command-surface.py::ours()` lost two MORE roots on the next commit, through a third shape: `Node::redirect_to("tell", &["msg"])` matched neither `Node::leaf(...)` nor either call alternative, because the lookbehind excludes an identifier after `::`. Three silent drops in one function in one day is the argument for the backstop that landed with it — any `Node::<something>(` at depth one in `roots: vec![]` that this parser does not recognise is now a refusal, so the next shape stops the generator instead of shrinking its answer.
* 2026-09-05 **`javap` strips trailing whitespace from a `Utf8` constant, and Brigadier's usage generator has two constants that end in a space.** `CommandDispatcher.USAGE_REDIRECT` renders as `->` and `ARGUMENT_SEPARATOR` renders as nothing at all — confirmed with `od -c` on both `-c -constants` and `-v` output, where the whole value of the separator is invisible. A redirect usage transcribed from the listing produces `tell ->msg`; vanilla's own `getSmartUsage` over the same tree produces `tell -> msg`. Only a golden taken from the running generator shows it, and this project's clean-room rule means the listing is the only other source. Anything transcribed from a `javap` string constant that could plausibly end in a space needs a vector.
* 2026-09-05 `/difficulty` is REFUSED, and the reason is the dead-field class rather than the work: this server has no difficulty state, no `server.properties` reader, and no `change_difficulty` in `crates/rustmc-proto/src/ids.rs` at all. A difficulty cell would be read by nothing — no mob spawning, no damage scaling, no login field — and its only observable would be a packet whose id is not in the table. Adding that id belongs to the `packets` lane; the state belongs to whoever ports the first mechanic that reads it.
* 2026-09-05 **The reply to a command that does not run is one shape whose reach is the whole unported surface, and it is ported now.** `Commands.finishParsing` sends `ComponentUtils.fromMessage(e.getRawMessage())` and then a context component; `crates/rustmc-server/src/commands.rs` had answered all of it with silence, which on the wire is indistinguishable from a command that ran and printed nothing. 41 of the golden's 55 rows are byte-exact, 8 are silent where vanilla is silent, 6 are the argument-level arm and are ratcheted. Three things a reading gets wrong: the red is `sendFailure`'s wrapper (`Component.empty().append(c).withStyle(RED)`) and neither inner component is red itself; `MutableComponent.append(String)` returns early on an empty string, so a cursor-0 failure has NO prefix element rather than `{"": ""}`; and the context elements are wrapped by `ListTag.write`'s heterogeneous rule, so the ellipsis and the prefix are wrapped bare strings while the styled parts are not.
* 2026-09-05 `Commands.getParseException` has three arms and they are three different messages: reader exhausted is no exception at all (and then `ContextChain.tryFlatten` fails with `command.unknown.command` at the END of the input), `exceptions.size() == 1` returns that candidate's own exception, an empty context range is `command.unknown.command` at cursor 0, and anything else is `command.unknown.argument`. `Tree::resolve` had collapsed all of them into one `Refusal::Unknown`, which is why the padded-whitespace forms `list ` and `list  uuids` are `unknown.argument` while ` list` is `unknown.command`.
* 2026-09-05 **`BrigadierExceptions` SWAPS the two arguments of every bound message.** `IntegerArgumentType.parse` raises `integerTooLow().createWithContext(reader, result, minimum)` — found, then bound — and `BrigadierExceptions`' `lambda$static$4(a, b)` then builds `translatable("argument.integer.low", b, a)`. So the wire order is **bound first, value second**: `/gamerule random_tick_speed -1` produces `with: [0, -1]` and `/gamerule max_snow_accumulation_height 99` produces `with: [8, 99]`. A port that passed them in Brigadier's order renders "must not be less than -1, found 0". Every one of the eight `argument.<type>.low|big` messages has the same lambda shape.
* 2026-09-05 Vanilla replaces Brigadier's `BuiltInExceptions` wholesale: `net.minecraft.commands.BrigadierExceptions` supplies `Component.translatable` for all twenty-six built-in exception types, so the first failure line a client sees is a translation key and never Brigadier's English `LiteralMessage`. `ComponentUtils.fromMessage` then returns that component rather than wrapping a string. A port that used Brigadier's own messages would send readable English that no client would translate.
* 2026-09-05 With the argument-level table in, the whole failure surface of this server's own tree is byte-exact: **80 golden rows (16 failing inputs x 5 permission levels), 72 rendered, 8 silent where vanilla is silent, 0 unrendered, 0 disagreements**, and the unrendered ratchet is pinned at zero so that the first `net.minecraft.commands.arguments` exception this tree can raise is a reviewed change rather than a silent regression.
* 2026-09-05 **`minecraft:block_state` and `minecraft:item_stack` cannot be transcribed the way the other eight parsers were: for these two the registry decides the ACCEPT, not just the value.** `ci/command-parse-golden.sh` shows the two differing on one string — `diamond_sword` is rejected by `block_state` and accepted by `item_stack`, `hello` is rejected by both, `stone` accepted by both — so a port with no registry in hand accepts `hello` as a block. `stone[facing=north]` is rejected because `stone` has no `facing` property, so the property parse is registry-dependent too. `Parser` is a pure static description of a node and `Parser::parse` has nothing else in hand; threading a block and item registry into it is a design change and not a transcription, and this lane stopped in front of it rather than guessing. The data exists (`data/vanilla/block_props.json`, `data/vanilla/items.json`).
* 2026-09-05 `ItemArgument.item(ctx)` is built with `allowNbt = false` and `BlockStateArgument.block(ctx)` with `true`: `minecraft:block_state` on `stone{Foo:1b}` ends at cursor **13**, having consumed the compound, and `minecraft:item_stack` on the same string ends at **5**. In 26.2 item components are `[...]` and a trailing `{...}` is the next node's problem.
* 2026-09-05 26.2 declares **49** block-entity types and `main` modelled **14** (chest, furnace, sign, hanging_sign, banner, beacon, brushable_block, campfire, conduit, copper_golem_statue, creaking_heart, decorated_pot, shelf, skull), not 3 of 46 — `docs/SERVER.md`'s "Three block-entity types" bullet was a commit behind the eleven added 2026-08-12. The gap was 35. `crates/rustmc-server/src/blockentity.rs`, branch `block-entities-2`.
* 2026-09-05 `minecraft:bed` is **not** a block-entity type in 26.2 — the registry has no such entry and `BedBlock` is not an `EntityBlock`. Any worklist ordering that puts a bed among the block entities is porting nothing. `oracle-work/vanilla-26.2/generated/reports/registries.json`.
* 2026-09-05 The eight copper chests are `BlockEntityTypes.CHEST` — its registration is `Util.copyAndAdd(Blocks.COPPER_CHEST.asList(), Blocks.CHEST)`, nine blocks — and `blockentity::for_state` matched the literal `"minecraft:chest"`, so every copper chest had a container, a menu path and nowhere to keep an item. 132 of 186 valid blocks were mapped before this. `ci/block-entity-golden.sh`, commit 178f1e3db.
* 2026-09-05 `containers::trigger_event` ignored `b0` and answered `true` for any event at a chest. `ChestBlockEntity.triggerEvent`'s `if_icmpne` at offset 2 jumps to the `super` call at 23, and `RandomizableContainerBlockEntity` does not override it, so the chain reaches `BlockEntity.triggerEvent`'s `iconst_0; ireturn`: a chest is **false** for every `b0` but 1. The doc comment above the function asserted the opposite ("accepts unconditionally") — a comment in our own source refuted. Seven types override `triggerEvent` and all seven gate on `b0 == 1`; only `DecoratedPotBlockEntity` reads `b1`, bounded by `WobbleStyle.values().length` = 2. `crates/rustmc-server/src/containers.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `containers::has_openers_counter` was keyed on the block *name* and so said no for nine of the ten `ChestBlockEntity`-family blocks (trapped chest + eight copper chests). Three classes hold a `ContainerOpenersCounter` and the barrel's is **not** the same mechanism: `BarrelBlockEntity$1` calls `updateBlockState` (a `setBlock` on `open`) where `ChestBlockEntity$1` and `EnderChestBlockEntity$1` call `signalOpenCount`/`Level.blockEvent`, so a barrel opening sends no `block_event` at all. Inert until `menu::menu_for_state` covers more than `minecraft:chest`.
* 2026-09-05 `BlockEntity.getUpdatePacket()` cannot be probed on an instance without a level — `ClientboundBlockEntityDataPacket.create` goes through `getUpdateTag(this.level.registryAccess())` — so a golden column reading `getUpdatePacket() != null` came back **false for all 49 types**, a column with one value on its first run. Walking the class chain for a *declared* no-arg `getUpdatePacket` needs no level and gives 19 yes, 29 no, 1 unknown (`minecraft:vault` does not instantiate either: `VaultConfig.DEFAULT`'s class initialiser). `tools/goldengen/vanilla/BlockEntityGolden.java`.
* 2026-09-05 `ShulkerBoxBlockEntity` is the registry's only `iconst_0` into `ContainerHelper.saveAllItems(_, _, boolean)`, so an empty shulker box reaches `ValueOutput.discard("Items")` at offset 79 and saves **no** `Items` key, where every other container saves `{Items: []}`. Three types with a slot do not use `Items` at all: `RecordItem` (jukebox), `Book` (lectern), `item` (decorated pot), each behind an `isEmpty()` guard. `crates/rustmc-server/src/blockentity.rs::items_key`.
* 2026-09-05 A freshly placed hopper saves `TransferCooldown: -1` and a fresh chiseled bookshelf `last_interacted_slot: -1` (`iconst_m1; putfield` in each constructor), and `BrewTime`/`Fuel` are `putShort`/`putByte` six instructions apart while the four furnace counters are all `putShort`. A round-trip test that starts from the value the code wrote sees none of those three classes of error. `Kind::extra_keys`.
* 2026-09-05 `Page` and `ticks_since_song_started` are the only two block-entity scalar keys inside a guard — `if (!getBook().isEmpty())` at `LecternBlockEntity.saveAdditional` offset 12 and `if (getSong() != null)` at `JukeboxBlockEntity.saveAdditional` offset 37 — so neither is a key its type always writes and a table of unconditional keys must exclude both.
* 2026-09-05 **Every block entity in every saved chunk carries a `components` key and this server wrote it for none of them.** `BlockEntity.saveWithoutMetadata` ends with an unconditional `ValueOutput.store("components", DataComponentMap.CODEC, this.components)` — a `store`, not a `storeNullable` — so all 49 types write it, as an empty compound when nothing has set one. Found by dumping `saveWithFullMetadata` on a freshly constructed instance of every type; 47 of 49 dump (piston and vault throw in a bootstrapped-only JVM). `tools/goldengen/vanilla/BlockEntityGolden.java`, branch `block-entities-2`.
* 2026-09-05 The defaults a *freshly placed* block entity saves are not zeroes and several are one plausible guess away: `StructureBlockEntity.posY` is **1** where `posX`/`posZ` are 0, `ignoreEntities` and `showboundingbox` are **true** where `powered`/`showair`/`strict` are false, `integrity` is a float 1.0 and `seed` a long; `CommandBlockEntity.TrackOutput` and `UpdateLastExecution` are **true**; a `JigsawBlockEntity`'s `name`/`pool`/`target` are `minecraft:empty` and `final_state` is `minecraft:air`, not ""; a fresh `SpawnerBlockEntity` saves `Delay: 20s` with seven other shorts; `PotentSulfurBlockEntity.countdown` is -1. `CrafterBlockEntity.triggered` is an **int** where every other boolean-shaped field in the registry is a byte. All 19 structure-block keys, all 7 command-block keys and all 7 jigsaw keys are now transcribed and compared to vanilla's dump name, tag id **and** rendered value.
* 2026-09-05 **`ci/gen-gameplay-tables.sh` derives its table list from a quoted `"data/vanilla/x.json"` literal, so a `generator_for()` arm whose load site spells the path any other way is unreachable and its table is never generated in any gate dir.** `crates/rustmc-server/src/blockentity.rs`'s `.join("../../data/vanilla/block_entities.json")` begins `"../../` and could not match the deriving grep; the arm existed, stage 3 reported 21 tables current, `ci/block-entity-golden.sh` never ran, and three tests panicked `missing fixture` — T0 exit 101 on `block-entities-2` while the lane's own tree, which had the table by hand, was green. * 2026-09-05 The three orphaned provenance shas (`48d1efb`, `00ab0bde`, `917ebc43`) are red on a fourth branch. * 2026-09-05 `ci/liveness-proofs.py --discover` counted its own formatting, not the proofs: `PROBE`'s `-q\b` cannot match `grep -qa` (`\b` after `q` needs a non-word character), and `ENDS_WAIT` wanted the `break` on the probe's own physical line, so a backslash continuation and a multi-line `if … then`/`break`/`fi` were both invisible. **6 discovered -> 10** after folding logical lines and following a `then` block; the four missed are `ci/t2-gameplay-survival.sh:202` (the survival pass's ONLY readiness proof), `ci/readiness-falsify.sh:163` (inside the falsifier that took every fixture in the registry) and `ci/run-detached-falsify.sh:43,45`. The tenth, `a-daylight-detector-inverts-when-right-clicked`, is the whole difference between stack-extract's 99/108 and their 98/108 — it is `match` only on `origin/stack-extract`, which is not merged, and locking it from anywhere else would ratchet main to a verdict main's server cannot produce.
* 2026-09-05 `ci/corpus-grades.py`'s frozen ratchet counted `frozen` at **history_n = 1** as an agreement. One recording is one comparison and one comparison has zero opportunities to differ — the same defect that made `unseen` (N=0) a separate state on 2026-08-18, one N later, and `ci/corpus-history.py`'s report had been printing it as a caveat ever since ("surfaced rather than acted on"). New state `unresolved` (N < 2), excluded from `HISTORY_FROZEN_CEILING`, bounded by its own ceiling of 14, and required to carry tier 2 in the falsifiable ledger. `--check` re-derives frozen/unresolved from `history_n` in both directions, so an old ledger reclassifies with no re-take. Redstone's ledger goes `69 frozen (RED)` -> `60 frozen + 9 unresolved (PASS)`; main is unchanged at 41/6/61/0. **The tier-2 exemption was refused**: 108 of 108 checks are tier 2, so counting only frozen-and-below-tier-2 counts zero forever — the ratchet deleted rather than corrected.
* 2026-09-05 `ci/readiness-falsify.sh` printed `ok` on four refusal arms for a readiness line that never ran: an `eval` of an unparseable fragment leaves `RUSTMC_READY` at 0, which is indistinguishable from a predicate that read the log and said "not ready". Exposed by my widened `--discover` reporting the `if … then` shape as the probe line alone (`eval: line 126: syntax error: unexpected end of file`, ×5, two T0 reds). Discovery now reports the smallest span around a probe that parses under `bash -n` and never grows one that parses alone (growing it annexed a neighbouring loop's `break` and reported a post-loop `grep … || exit 1` as a proof); `--check` refuses an UNPARSEABLE source line at registration; `run_readiness_line` hard-fails instead of reading a syntax error as a refusal; and the cross-examined population is selected on a declared `contract = "rustmc-ready"` rather than on `"rustmc.log" in source_line`, with a proof naming the log and declaring neither field refused so it cannot leave that population by omission.
* 2026-09-05 `origin/worldgen-land`'s tip publishes `docs/WORKLIST.md` "All 99 are now placed and nothing else moved" for the village `feature_pool_element`s while its own `tools/worldgen-assembly/src/generate.rs` refuses every one of them — `features.get(feature` is 0 occurrences on that tip and 1 on `origin/bastion-nether`. The refusal is deliberate (`5fa6d01ec`, "village-pool-elements lands its ports and its census, NOT its placement path"); the WORKLIST row is the half that did not follow, and it is a doc claim its own branch's code does not implement.
* 2026-09-05 Nobody's branch implements `BlockPileWorld` or `BlockEntityRecorder` for `feature::tree::UndoWorld`, and no branch alone can: worldgen-land makes `BlockEntityRecorder` a supertrait of `MonsterRoomWorld` and pale-census/bastion-nether add `BlockPileWorld` to `FeatureWorld`, so `dyn FeatureWorld` only stops being implementable for the wrapper once both land. Measured on all six branches: 0 impls each.
* 2026-09-05 `UndoWorld`'s all-or-nothing was untrue of block entities before it was ever exercised: `Canvas::record_block_entity` appends to a per-chunk list with no remove, so `rollback` restores the block and leaves the record. Fixed by buffering records in the wrapper and flushing only on the Ok arm of `tree::place`.
* 2026-09-05 `worldgen-parallelism` and `worldgen-land` are two independent implementations of the same divided carve — trait `CarveBlocks` against trait `Blocks`, same design — and a hunk-level union puts two complete drivers in `generate_resuming` with two sets of counters. Not unionable; one has to be chosen and the other's §104 numbers re-taken on it.
* 2026-09-05 `docs/DETERMINISM.md:151` carried a bare `||||||| base` with the base text twice and no `=======`/`>>>>>>>` on both `floors-n-campaign` and `end-biome-census` — a committed conflict marker that no `<<<<<<<` grep would have found, and the whole of what turned both branches T0 red.
* 2026-09-05 `ci/figure-provenance.py --check` compares an abbreviated sha by `in`, so it breaks whenever git's `%h` width changes; on a tree 369 commits larger than main the width is 9 and every 8-char provenance stops matching — 56 of 70 red for a reason unrelated to any figure. Already the queue's tier-0 item on `boxcopy`; the gate directory never sees it because it has no `.git` and the check skips there.
* 2026-09-05 Twelve of the nineteen raw fixture skips the worldgen backlog carries are dead text: `load_tags`, `state_spec::extract_or_skip`, `pale_oak_config` and `worldgen_assembly::have_extract` had all adopted `rustmc_data::fixture::available`, and their callers kept the old `eprintln!("skipping: …")` in the `else` arm — a message the helper's own decision had already made unreachable.
* 2026-09-05 `clippy::if_same_then_else` on transcribed parity code is a lint about vanilla, not about the port: `feature/huge_mushroom.rs`'s red `getTreeRadiusForHeight` has two arms returning `radius` because the jar's two arms do (`if (y < height && y >= height - 3) return radius; if (y == height) return radius;`, `height` bound to the literal `-1` at the only call site). Merging them is arithmetically identical and erases which of vanilla's two tests a `y` fell through. `#[allow]` with the bytecode beside it; the same rule applies to every dead branch this tree reproduces rather than corrects.
* 2026-09-05 The two corpus stacks' grade ledgers have incomparable denominators and neither file says so: `stack-worldgen`'s `oracle-runner` reads all 65 recordings on the box, `stack-gameplay`'s refuses 38 of them with `unknown variant 'probe'` and reads 27. `history_recordings` in `ci/checks/gameplay-grades.toml` is the only place the difference is visible, and `moved`/`frozen` counts taken over different subsets are being compared against one pair of constants (`HISTORY_MOVED_FLOOR`, `HISTORY_FROZEN_CEILING`) in `ci/corpus-grades.py`.
* 2026-09-05 `ci/mob-tick-census.py`'s "quoted in prose" rule was green on a substring coincidence: `mob_subtypes = 114` is declared as quoted in `docs/SERVER.md` and **has never been written there** — the rule passed because that file carries `11491 connective`, which contains `114`. It surfaced only when `stack-worldgen` rewrote those counts and the coincidence went away, so the rule reddened for the right figure at a moment that had nothing to do with it. Measured across all three landing stacks and all six declared figures before narrowing: that pair is the ONLY one where a substring test and a digit-bounded test disagree, so the fix costs one sentence of prose per stack. `ci/mob-tick-census-falsify.sh` case 9b is the evidence — it buries `3,870` inside `13,8705` and **FAILS against the old rule** (verified on a scratch copy) — because case 9, which edits the figure out entirely, is refused by a substring test too and could never have caught this.
* 2026-09-05 `ci/figure-provenance.py --check` is red on MAIN and no gate can see it: 56 of 56 re-derived figures disagree with their recorded provenance, measured by swapping `origin/main:ci/figures.toml` into a worktree and running main's own copy of the script (`figure-provenance: 56 figure(s) re-derived, 56 whose derived commit is not in their provenance`). It passes in every gate directory because `ci/t0-remote.sh` rsyncs with `--exclude .git`, so there is no history to re-derive against and the check has nothing to disagree with. The diagnosis and the repair are already written on branch `boxcopy` (`cac40208a`, "an abbreviation is not an identity, and main is red because it was treated as one"), which is not an ancestor of main; `stack-ci`'s own copy passes at 59 re-derived / 0 mismatched. **A check that needs `.git` is inert in the one tree the gate runs in** — that class is worth a sweep of its own.
* 2026-09-05 **A test that pins a field empty and says "update this alongside" is a T1 red waiting for whoever builds that field, and this stack built it three folds later.** `crates/rustmc-server/tests/protocol_ids_reachable.rs::world_chunk_runs_end_to_end_beside_the_block_entity_store_and_its_ids` (`:115`) asserted `chunk.block_entities.is_empty()`; `94fe1efbb` made `World::chunk` build that field from `BlockEntities::shared().in_chunk`, and the T1 gate could not even match the panic to a signature because an `assert!` carries no `N of M blocks differ` header. The repair is to assert the entry's content — packed_xz, y, registry id, and the single END byte a null `getUpdateTag()` encodes to — not merely that one exists: "non-empty" goes green on an entry at the wrong position, of the wrong type or with the wrong tag. Shown red on `chunk_payload_entry(pos, &be, type_id + 1)`.

* 2026-09-05 **`time set` was confirmed by grepping for `Set the time to <n>`, which 26.2 does not print, so the harness refused a vanilla server that had done exactly what it was asked.** It cost `stack-worldgen` a whole T2 slot: 36 minutes queued for the corpus lock, vanilla booted, and the refusal fired before one scenario ran, with vanilla's own log two lines below reading `Set minecraft:overworld to 6000 tick(s)`. `commands.time.set` = `Set the time to %s` is **still in 26.2's lang file** while being referenced nowhere in the code — `javap -p -c -constants net/minecraft/server/commands/TimeCommand.class` references `commands.time.set.absolute` (`Set %s to %s tick(s)`) twice and `commands.time.set` never — so a lang grep for the text would have found the wrong one and confirmed the mistake. * 2026-09-05 **`gamerule_or_die` had only the negative half — it refused vanilla's error texts and never confirmed the rule was applied** — in the same file whose `time_set_or_die` comment argues at length that a command accepted-and-ignored leaves the substrate wherever it was. The 2026-08-03 incident that helper exists for was caught by the *refusal* text; a rule silently retired to a no-op prints no refusal and would have passed. Now confirmed positively against `commands.gamerule.set` = `Gamerule %s is now set to: %s`, the only set-message key `net/minecraft/server/commands/GameRuleCommand.class` references, derived from the pinned jar rather than written down.
