* 2026-09-05 `RingBell.BELL_RING_CHANCE = 0.95f` is compared as `level.getRandom().nextFloat() > 0.95f` (`lambda$create$2`, offsets 0..16), so a villager at the bell rings it on 5 % of triggers, not 95 %; `crates/rustmc-entity/src/brain/behaviors/ring_bell.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `SpearAttack$SpearStatus` is `APPROACH, CHARGING, RETREAT` (`$values`, 0..21); `mobs-brains` had named the middle variant `Attack`, renamed to `Charging` in `brain/memory.rs` before anything read it.
* 2026-09-05 `Behavior.tryStart` computes `maxDuration + 1 - minDuration` in Java int arithmetic, so `LookAndFollowTradingPlayerSink`'s `Integer.MAX_VALUE` duration wraps to `nextInt(1)`; the port's `i32` overflowed there and now wraps (`brain/behavior.rs`, `try_start`).
* 2026-09-05 `NearestBedSensor.doTick` (offsets 0..8) returns unless `mob.isBaby()`: adult villagers never sense `NEAREST_BED`; the sensor exists for `JumpOnBed`, and `SleepInBed` finds beds through `AcquirePoi(HOME)` instead.
* 2026-09-05 `CrossbowAttack.crossbowAttack`'s `CHARGING` branch (offsets 51..125) sets `UNCHARGED` when the mob stopped using the item and then still tests `getTicksUsingItem() >= getChargeDuration` in the same tick, so a fully charged crossbow whose use ended fires `CHARGED` regardless.
* 2026-09-05 `InsideBrownianWalk`'s third filter (`lambda$create$5`) ignores its candidate and calls `level.noCollision(mob)` on the mob's own box, so it is a constant per trigger; and `Collections.shuffle(list)` uses an unseeded `java.util.Random` (unverifiable order, a caller-seeded source stands in).
* 2026-09-05 `VillagerCalmDown` erases `HURT_BY` and `NEAREST_HOSTILE` but not `HURT_BY_ENTITY` (offsets 55..62), which is left to its own 200-tick expiry from `HurtBySensor`.
* 2026-09-05 `SleepInBed.nextOkStartTime` gates only the effects of `start` (offsets 0..6): a start within 40 ticks of being woken runs, does nothing, and reports `Running` until `canStillUse` fails.
* 2026-09-05 `AcquirePoi`'s retry predicate (`markAttempt`, one level `nextInt(40)` each) runs on every in-range record before `findAllClosestFirstWithType`'s `sorted`, so the level random's draw count depends on the manager's chunk order, not the closest five; the post-failure cache fill walks a `HashSet<Pair<Holder, BlockPos>>` (identity hash) and is unverifiable by construction.
* 2026-09-05 `PlayTagWithOtherKids.findSomeoneBeingChased` sorts a `HashMap<LivingEntity, Integer>`'s entries by count: ties between equally chased kids resolve in identity-hash order, unverifiable; the port breaks them by first appearance.
* 2026-09-05 `SetHiddenState.ticksHidden` (a captured `MutableInt`) is reset only by the timeout branch, never by a new bell, so a second raid alarm inside the hide window inherits the count from the first.
* 2026-09-05 `LocateHidingPlace` draws the mob random (`Util.shuffle`, `size - 1` `nextInt`s) only when no home within `closeEnoughDist + 1` passes the centre test (`Optional.or` is lazy); the walk target uses `closeEnoughDist`, not `maxDistance`.
* 2026-09-05 `PoiCompetitorScan.selectWinner` keeps the higher `getVillagerXp()` and on a tie the *second* argument (`if_icmple`, offset 8), so in the `reduce(villager, ...)` fold an equally experienced newcomer takes the site from the current holder.
* 2026-09-05 `PoiManager.getRandom` (`LocateHidingPlace`) is `Util.toShuffledList(getInRange(...), random)` then `filter(posPred).findFirst()`: the type predicate is applied before the shuffle and the position predicate after it.
* 2026-09-05 The supervisor parks a dirty worktree into `git stash` and detaches HEAD (`reflog`: `checkout: moving from mobs-brains-2 to HEAD`); commits made after that land on a detached HEAD and `git push origin <branch>` reports `Everything up-to-date` while pushing nothing. Push `HEAD:refs/heads/<branch>` and check `git ls-remote` every time.
* 2026-09-05 `tests/brain_tables.rs`'s "every class the piglin package names" was true of its **scope**, not of the package: six of the piglin's behaviours (`StartAdmiringItemIfSeen`, `StopHoldingItemIfNoLongerAdmiring`, `StopAdmiringIfItemTooFarAway`, `StopAdmiringIfTiredOfTryingToReachItem`, `RememberIfHoglinWasKilled`, `StartHuntingHoglin`) live in `monster/piglin/`, not `ai/behavior/`, and the old walker only matched the latter prefix.
* 2026-09-05 A behaviour class is what the jar's descriptors say it is: a `new` of any `world/entity/` class outside `ai/behavior/declarative`, or a `call` whose owner is under `ai/behavior/` or whose descriptor returns `BehaviorControl`/`OneShot`/`Behavior`/`Trigger`. Over the twenty brain packages that names 131 classes and 87 sensor slots over 23 distinct `SensorType`s.
* 2026-09-05 `LongJumpUtil.calculateJumpVectorForAngle` computes the angle in **float** (`i2f; ldc 3.1415927f; fmul; ldc 180.0f; fdiv`) and only then widens, so `65.0 * PI / 180.0` in `f64` is the wrong number; its result is scaled by `0.949999988079071`, the widened `0.95f`, and `EntityDimensions.makeBoundingBox` halves the width in float too.
* 2026-09-05 `LongJumpToRandomPos.pickCandidate` accepts a landing spot when the navigation **cannot** reach it (`path == null || !path.canReach()`), and writes `LOOK_TARGET` before the path test, so a rejected candidate still leaves its look target behind.
* 2026-09-05 The long-jump cooldown after a completed jump is **twice** the one after a refused start: both `LongJumpToRandomPos` writes are `timeBetweenLongJumps.sample(level.random) / 2` (an integer halving) and `LongJumpMidJump.stop`'s is the full sample.
* 2026-09-05 `LongJumpToPreferredBlock.getJumpCandidate` falls back in **draw order**, not by weight: it drains the whole weighted list, parks every non-preferred draw in `notPrefferedJumpCandidates` (vanilla's spelling) and returns `remove(0)` from the front of that list.
* 2026-09-05 `TemptingSensor`'s range is `(double)(float) getAttributeValue(TEMPT_RANGE)` (`d2f; f2d` at 18..19), and its `Stream.sorted` is a **stable** merge sort, so two players holding food at equal `distanceToSqr` are separated by `level.players()` order alone.
* 2026-09-05 `AdultSensor.doTick` is `getMemory(NEAREST_VISIBLE_LIVING_ENTITIES).ifPresent(...)` with **no else**: an absent visible list leaves `NEAREST_VISIBLE_ADULT` untouched, while a present one with no passing adult **erases** it (`setMemory(type, Optional)`).
* 2026-09-05 The two attackable sensors use different distance tests: `AxolotlAttackablesSensor.isClose` is `distanceToSqr <= 64.0` (inclusive, so exactly 8 blocks passes) and `FrogAttackablesSensor` ends in `closerThan(mob, 10.0)`, a strict `<` whose literal is the `double`, not the `float TARGET_DETECTION_DISTANCE`.
* 2026-09-05 `SensorType.ARMADILLO_SCARE_DETECTED` is `new MobSensor<>(5, Armadillo::isScaredBy, Armadillo::canStayRolledUp, DANGER_DETECTED_RECENTLY, 80)` — **scan rate 5, not the default 20** — and `MobSensor.checkForMobsNearby` returns without touching the memory when `NEAREST_LIVING_ENTITIES` is absent, so only the not-ready path clears it.
* 2026-09-05 `WardenEntitySensor` walks the whole distance-ordered list for a **player** before it will look at any non-player (`Optional.or` is lazy), so a player at 4 blocks outranks a mob at 1 and the second pass re-runs `canTargetEntity` on everything the first rejected.
* 2026-09-05 None of the warden's four durations is an integer literal: `WardenAi`'s static initialiser is `Mth.ceil` of `100.0f`, `133.59999f`, `84.0f` and `83.2f` — so `EMERGE_DURATION` is **134** and `SNIFFING_DURATION` is **84**.
* 2026-09-05 `warden.Digging.start` calls its own `stop` in the not-on-ground branch, discarding the warden on the same tick it plays `WARDEN_AGITATED`; the duration's own `stop` then finds `getRemovalReason() != null` and does nothing.
* 2026-09-05 `warden.Roar` plays its sound from `tick`, not `start`: `start` arms a 25-tick `ROAR_SOUND_DELAY` and the firing tick arms `ROAR_DURATION - 25 = 59` of `ROAR_SOUND_COOLDOWN`, which **outlives the 84-tick roar**, so an immediately following roar is silent.
* 2026-09-05 `warden.SonicBoom` has three cooldowns doing three jobs — `SONIC_BOOM_SOUND_DELAY` (34) delays the beam inside one use, `SONIC_BOOM_SOUND_COOLDOWN` (60-34) stops a second inside it, `SONIC_BOOM_COOLDOWN` (40) is the gap between uses — and the sound cooldown is armed **before** the two range filters, so an out-of-range target still consumes it.
* 2026-09-05 `StopAdmiringIfItemTooFarAway` reads backwards from its name: both refusals are the keep-admiring case (a **full** off hand means the item is already held, a **near** item means the walk is worth making), and it gives up only when the hand is empty and the item absent or far.
* 2026-09-05 `StartHuntingHoglin` calls `dontKillAnyMoreHoglinsForAWhile` twice on the hunter — once directly, once through the `NEAREST_VISIBLE_ADULT_PIGLINS` forEach that may contain it — so its `HUNTED_RECENTLY` takes two separate `TIME_BETWEEN_HUNTS` draws; its refusal is any visible adult piglin being angry at **anything**.
* 2026-09-05 `TryFindWater`'s first delay is `20 + 2` and its refresh is `40`; `TryFindLand`/`TryFindLandNearWater` use one number twice (60, 40). All three keep the cooldown in a `MutableLong` captured by `create`, not a memory, so it is per-`BehaviorControl` rather than per-mob.
* 2026-09-05 `TryLaySpawnOnFluidNearLand`'s guard is `!isInWater() && onGround()` (1..15: `isInWater; ifne 14` where 14 is `iconst_0; ireturn`), and it returns `true` whether or not it laid anything, so an unsuccessful frog stays pregnant.
* 2026-09-05 `Croak`'s 100-tick `TIME_OUT_DURATION` is unreachable — `canStillUse` is `croakCounter < 60` — and `start` sets `Pose.CROAKING` only out of liquid while `stop` restores `STANDING` unconditionally.
* 2026-09-05 `Vec3.directionFromRotation` is **not** `Entity.calculateViewVector`: the yaw term carries an extra `- 3.1415927f` and the pitch cosine is negated, so `(0, 0)` gives `(0, 0, -1)` against the view vector's `(0, 0, 1)`. `RandomLookAround` uses the former.
* 2026-09-05 `StayCloseToTarget` refuses when the mob is **already** within `tooFar` and walks only when it is further out, and puts the identical `PositionTracker` object into both `LOOK_TARGET` and the `WalkTarget`.
* 2026-09-05 Refuted from our own tree: `Sensor.isEntityAttackable` must not throw on an unregistered `ATTACK_TARGET`. It reaches the memory through `Brain.isMemoryValue`, which goes through `checkMemory` and answers `false` for an unregistered slot, never reaching the `getMemory` that throws; a port that used `getMemory` reddened `player_sensor`'s test. `brain::sensor::attack_target_id` carries the guard.
* 2026-09-05 `axolotl.PlayDead` has no `tick` and no `stop`; what ends the play-dead activity is `ValidatePlayDead`'s `<= 0` branch calling `Brain.useDefaultActivity()`, and the memory erase alone would not.
* 2026-09-05 An ABSENT `DANGER_DETECTED_RECENTLY` reads as MAXIMAL danger in `ArmadilloAi$ArmadilloBallUp.tick`. `danger = brain.getTimeUntilExpiry(...) > 75L`, `Brain.getTimeUntilExpiry` is `getMemorySlot(type).timeToLive()`, and `MemorySlot.clear()` (0..12) puts `timeToLive` back to `NEVER_EXPIRE = Long.MAX_VALUE` — so a memory that expired or was never set answers `Long.MAX_VALUE`, `danger` is true, and the armadillo stays balled up. The obvious reading is the opposite one.
* 2026-09-05 `RamTarget.tick`'s `closerThan(ramTarget, 0.25)` disjunct CAN NEVER FIRE. `start` writes `new WalkTarget(ramTarget, speed, 0)`, and `WalkTarget(Vec3, float, int)` is `BlockPosTracker(BlockPos.containing(v))` — the block constructor, `centerPosition = atCenterOf(pos)`, y = blockY + 0.5 — while every `RAM_TARGET` is `PrepareRamNearestTarget.getEdgeOfBlock` = `atBottomCenterOf(to).add(x, 0, z)`, y = blockY. The gap is exactly 0.5 on y alone. `BlockPosTracker(Vec3)` does keep its argument verbatim, but `WalkTarget` never calls that one.
* 2026-09-05 `ChargeAttack`'s constructor assigns its two doubles in the OPPOSITE order to their field declarations: slot 5-6 goes to `maxChargeDistance` (offset 40) and slot 7-8 to `maxTargetDetectionDistance` (offset 46), while the fields read the other way. Both are used squared, so transcribing from the field list swaps them silently.
* 2026-09-05 `breeze.LongJump`'s angle shuffle IS seeded — `Util.shuffledCopy(ALLOWED_ANGLES, breeze.getRandom())`, and `Util.shuffle` (0..56) is Fisher-Yates from the end down, four `nextInt` draws for five angles — unlike `LongJumpToRandomPos`'s unseeded `Collections.shuffle`, which the fifth lane recorded as unverifiable by construction.
* 2026-09-05 `breeze.Shoot.checkExtraStartConditions` has THREE refusals with three different side effects: out of range erases `BREEZE_SHOOT` through `lambda$checkExtraStartConditions$1` (a side effect wearing a `Function`), the wrong pose takes the early `return false` at offset 10 and erases nothing, and an absent attack target reaches `orElse(false)` and runs neither lambda.
* 2026-09-05 Four classes are one-shots written as `Behavior`s, with `canStillUse` a bare `iconst_0` or no override at all, so their `DEFAULT_DURATION` is never reached and their only cost is the `nextInt(1)` `tryStart` draws: `breeze.ShootWhenStuck`, `breeze.Slide`, `CamelAi$RandomSitting`, and `GoAndGiveItemsToTarget`'s duration is the exception that really is used.
* 2026-09-05 `SnifferAi$Digging.stop` sets the 9600-tick `SNIFF_COOLDOWN` only on the timed-out path; a dig cut short by `canStillUse` calls `SnifferAi.resetSniffing` instead and leaves no cooldown, so the sniffer may start again on the next tick. `FinishedDigging.stop` runs the same three statements on both paths and only the `onDiggingComplete` boolean differs.
* 2026-09-05 `SnifferAi$Searching.canStillUse` is the only `canStillUse` in the sniffer's five that WRITES: on `!canSniff()` it calls `transitionTo(IDLING)` at offset 7 before returning false at 15, and `stop` never sets `IDLING` at all.
* 2026-09-05 `debugString()` of `SnifferAi$1/$2/$3`, `ArmadilloAi$1` and `CreakingAi$1` is the EMPTY STRING — `Behavior.debugString()` is `getClass().getSimpleName()` and `getSimpleName()` of an anonymous class is `""`. They have no source name, only the binary name the ledger uses.
* 2026-09-05 `BreezeUtil.hasLineOfSight` casts from the breeze's FEET (`new Vec3(getX(), getY(), getZ())`), not its eyes, and its `MAX_ANGLE` local (`bipush 90` at offset 0) is dead — the `90.0f` used is a separate `ldc` at offset 17. `randomPointBehindTarget` draws gaussian then float from the mob's own random.
* 2026-09-05 `breeze.Slide`'s inner-circle retreat has a third guard that decides it: the `DefaultRandomPos.getPosAway` position is taken only if `target.distanceToSqr(away) > target.distanceToSqr(breeze)` (offsets 71 and 93, strictly greater), so a retreat that would not retreat falls through to the coin flip. Its `Mth.lerp(nextDouble(), 8.0, 4.0)` runs 8 DOWN to 4 and is subtracted.
* 2026-09-05 `GoAndGiveItemsToTarget`'s `throwVelocity` is three `double` literals `0.20000000298023224` / `0.30000001192092896` — the exact widenings of `0.2f` and `0.3f` — and its `removeItem(0, 1)` runs BEFORE the emptiness check, so the container is mutated on every tick inside 3 blocks whether or not anything is thrown. Its `ITEM_PICKUP_COOLDOWN_TICKS` is a `setMemory` value, not an expiry.
* 2026-09-05 `frog.ShootTongue.checkExtraStartConditions` has a side effect only on the pathfinding half: `!canPathfindToTarget` erases `ATTACK_TARGET` and remembers the UUID for 100 ticks, while the pose and `Frog.canEat` tests that follow have none. Its catch state lasts 6 ticks (`++timer >= 6`) and its eat state 5 more (`timer >= 10` tested before the increment), not 6 and 10.
* 2026-09-05 `ChargeAttack.tick` calls `stop` DIRECTLY rather than `doStop`, so the status stays RUNNING after a hit and the next `tickOrStop` runs `stop` a second time; both statements are idempotent, so nothing is observable. `start` likewise sets `startPosition` and `chargeVelocityVector` BEFORE asking `canStillUse` whether to play the charge sound, so a tame animal charges silently for one tick.
* 2026-09-05 `TransportItemsBetweenContainers.hasValidTravellingPath` treats `path == null && !within` as a VALID travelling path (offsets 58..73), so "no path at all" does NOT mark a target unreachable — the opposite of the obvious reading, and the guard that stops `hasValidTarget` blacklisting every target on the tick it was chosen. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/brain/behaviors/transport_items_between_containers.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `TransportItemsBetweenContainers.tick` runs THREE independent `if`s over the state, not an `if/else` chain, so one tick can carry a mob queuing -> travelling -> interacting -> its first `ticksSinceReachingTarget++`. `timedOut(long)` is a bare `iconst_0`, so its `Behavior(Map)` durations of 60/60 never end it and the `tryStart` `nextInt(1)` draw is consumed for nothing.
* 2026-09-05 `TransportItemsBetweenContainers.addItemsToContainer` adds `moved = min(room, hand.getCount())` to the slot but subtracts the whole `room` from the hand (offsets 92..124), driving the hand's count NEGATIVE when it holds less than the room; `ItemStack.isEmpty()` is `count <= 0` so items are conserved, but `putDownItem` has already called `setItemSlot(MAINHAND, left)` with that stack before testing it.
* 2026-09-05 `ChunkAccess.blockEntities` is a fastutil `Object2ObjectOpenHashMap`, so `LevelChunk.getBlockEntities().values()` walks hash order — not insertion order, not sorted. `TransportItemsBetweenContainers.getTransportTarget` keeps a candidate on a strict `distToCenterSqr < best`, so two equidistant containers in one chunk are tie-broken by that map's internal order and by nothing recoverable from the positions.
* 2026-09-05 The visited/unreachable `GlobalPos` memories are `java.util.HashSet`s that NOTHING iterates — `getVisitedPositions` reads the memory or `Set.of()`, both writers copy into `new HashSet<>(...)`, and every operation is `add`/`remove`/`size`/`contains`. Their hash order is therefore unobservable and a `Vec<GlobalPos>` in first-added order is behaviourally exact.
* 2026-09-05 `TransportItemsBetweenContainers`'s two cooldown trips DISCARD the write that provoked them: `setVisitedBlockPos` at size > 10 and `markVisitedBlockPosAsUnreachable` at size > 50 both call `enterCooldownAfterNoMatchingTargetFound`, which ERASES both memories, so the enlarged set is never stored and the second case loses the removal from the visited set as well.
* 2026-09-05 `TransportItemsBetweenContainers`'s nearest-target scan seeds `bestDist` with `3.4028234663852886E38d` — `Float.MAX_VALUE` widened, not `Double.MAX_VALUE` — and tests `d >= bestDist` BEFORE `isTargetValidToPick`, so a nearer invalid container never raises the bar; `bestDist` moves only on an accepted candidate.
* 2026-09-05 `TransportItemsBetweenContainers`'s chunk scan filters `instanceof ChestBlockEntity`, so `TransportItemTarget.getBlockEntityContainer`'s `instanceof Container` fallback is unreachable for barrels, shulker boxes and hoppers through this behaviour; its only other caller is `getConnectedTargets`, which reads whatever a chest's own `getConnectedDirection` points at.
* 2026-09-05 `TransportItemsBetweenContainers.isWithinTargetDistance` inflates the target's collision bounds by `(d, 0.5d, d)` — the vertical reach is the literal 0.5 whichever of 3.0/2.0/1.0/0.5 the caller passed. `getTargetSearchArea` is `new AABB(mob.blockPosition()).inflate(h, v, h)` and `AABB.contains` is `min <= c < max`, so the accepted x are exactly `bx-h ..= bx+h` despite the half-open test.
* 2026-09-05 `ChunkPos.rangeClosed(centre, r)`'s spliterator (`ChunkPos$2.tryAdvance`, 0..114) walks x as the INNER axis and z as the outer, restarting x at `from.x` on each row; `Math.floorDiv(horizontalSearchDistance, 16) + 1` is the radius `getTransportTarget` passes it.
* 2026-09-05 `Aabb::inflate` in `crates/rustmc-entity/src/phys.rs` was writing the six coordinates straight into the struct, where the jar's `AABB.inflate` (0..69) hands them to the `AABB(DDDDDD)` constructor and so min/maxes them; a deflation large enough to invert a box differed. Fixed on `mobs-brains-5`; no caller in the tree passes one, so nothing moved.
* 2026-09-05 `EntityType$Builder.<init>` defaults `canSpawnFarFromPlayer` to **`category == CREATURE || category == MISC`** (offsets 94-114), not to false. Measured over the whole registry with `ci/dump-entity-spawn-facts.sh`: **105 of 158 types have it and only two — `pillager` and `shulker` — get it from an explicit `.canSpawnFarFromPlayer()` call**; the other 103 get it from that constructor line. A port reading the flag as "false unless the builder says otherwise" applies `NaturalSpawner.isValidSpawnPostitionForType`'s 128*128 gate to every animal in the game. The same constructor defaults `summon=true` (16), `allowedInPeaceful=true` (79), `spawnDimensionsScale=1.0f` (43) and `dimensions=scalable(0.6f, 1.8f)` (31-39), so an absent builder call is never an absent value.
* 2026-09-05 A biome may list a mob under a `MobCategory` that is not the mob's own, and 26.2 does: **`minecraft:ocelot` (CREATURE) is in the `monster` spawner list of `jungle` (weight 2, 1..3) and `bamboo_jungle` (weight 2, 1..1)**. Everything downstream of the weighted draw reads the *type's* category, not the pass's — `isValidSpawnPostitionForType`'s despawn-distance gate and `SpawnState.afterSpawn`'s cap and `LocalMobCapCalculator.addMob` — so an ocelot drawn on the MONSTER pass is charged to the CREATURE cap. Only reachable through the MONSTER pass, which `spawn-monsters=false` removes. `data/vanilla/biome_spawners.json`, `ci/dump-biome-spawners.sh`.
* 2026-09-05 `jungle`, `bamboo_jungle` and `sparse_jungle` each list **`minecraft:chicken 4..4` twice, at weight 10 each**, in their `creature` list. `MobSpawnSettings$SpawnerData` is a record of `(type, minCount, maxCount)` with **no weight field**, so the two entries are `equals` and a port that de-duplicated the list on record equality — which is exactly what `WeightedList.contains` compares in `NaturalSpawner.canSpawnMobAt` — would halve the chicken's share from 20/total to 10/total in three biomes.
* 2026-09-05 A zero-total-weight `WeightedList` **consumes no RNG**: `WeightedList.<init>` leaves `selector` null and `getRandom` returns `Optional.empty()` before reaching `nextInt(totalWeight)`. Most biome/category pairs are empty (66 biomes x 8 categories, 795 rows in total), so a port that drew first and checked after would desync `ServerLevel.random` on nearly every chunk of every tick.
* 2026-09-05 `NaturalSpawner.getFilteredSpawningCategories`' two booleans do not do what their names say. `spawnEnemies` (the `spawn-monsters` server property, a `ServerChunkCache` field and not a game rule) gates every **unfriendly** category, and `MONSTER` is the only one of the eight. `spawnFriendlies` — `gameTime % 400 == 0` — gates every **persistent** category, which among the seven spawning categories is `CREATURE` alone; `AMBIENT`, `AXOLOTLS` and the three water categories are all `isFriendly()` and run every tick, not every 400.
* 2026-09-05 `SpawnPlacementTypes.NO_RESTRICTIONS` is `iconst_1; ireturn` — **no world-border test**, where `IN_WATER`, `IN_LAVA` and `ON_GROUND` all open with `type != null && level.getWorldBorder().isWithinBounds(pos)`. Ten of the 83 registered types are `NO_RESTRICTIONS`, and so is every unregistered type, because `getPlacementType` defaults to it. Hoisting the shared prefix out of the other three would be adding a check vanilla does not make.
* 2026-09-05 `ServerChunkCache.tickChunks` calls `Util.shuffle(this.spawningChunks, level.getRandom())` **every tick, on the same shared stream every spawn draw comes from**, before any spawning happens — one `nextInt` per element from the end down. So the number of loaded spawning chunks shifts the stream position for everything that tick, and a port that iterated chunks in a fixed order diverges on tick one even with a byte-exact spawner. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/spawn/chunk_cache.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 The `spawnPotentials` density check is five numbers. Across all 66 biome files in the 26.2 jar there are **5 `spawn_costs` entries in 2 biomes** — `soul_sand_valley` (enderman/ghast/skeleton/strider, charge 0.7, budget 0.15) and `warped_forest` (enderman, 1.0/0.12). A server with no `PotentialCalculator` at all is wrong in two biomes and right in the other 64, which is the cost of deferring it stated as a number.

## 2026-09-05 — the corpus harness

* 2026-09-05 **`ci/t2-gameplay.sh` booted its two servers on different difficulties and nothing read either value back** — `difficulty=peaceful` in vanilla's `server.properties`, no difficulty argument to this server, so the subject ran at its own default. At PEACEFUL `SpawnEggItem.useOn` returns `InteractionResult.FAIL` at offsets 21-33, before it reads the clicked position, because `EntityType.canSpawn` (offsets 13-31) is `isAllowedInPeaceful() || getDifficulty() != PEACEFUL`; `Mob.checkDespawn` offsets 0-27 then `discard()`s anything that got in another way, from `ServerLevel.lambda$tick$0` offsets 17-30, every entity every tick. So a monster scenario was graded against vanilla silence while the subject spawned the mob. `ci/gameplay-difficulty.sh` is now the one place either side chooses one; `ci/gameplay-difficulty-falsify.sh` in T0.
* 2026-09-05 **The corpus has no monster coverage and the harness is why.** `mob-in-world`, `mob-combat` and `mobs-in-world-2` add ten mob scenarios between them and not one uses a monster — pig, bat, cow, sheep, chicken, iron golem, snow golem, every one either `allowedInPeaceful` or a golem. Three lanes independently wrote around the difficulty mismatch rather than through it.
* 2026-09-05 A flag this server does not recognise is silently ignored, not rejected — `crates/rustmc-server/src/main.rs` says so in its own comment — so **a harness that passes `--difficulty` to a tree without the flag has configured nothing while its launch line claims a symmetry**. Support is derived from the server source (`set_difficulty` for "has one", `"--difficulty"` for "can be told one") and the unmatched state refuses.
* 2026-09-05 At PEACEFUL this server would still spawn a creeper from an egg and then discard it a tick later: the peaceful arm of `Mob.checkDespawn` is ported (`crates/rustmc-server/src/mob/mod.rs`, on the mob branches) but `EntityType.canSpawn`'s guard in the spawn-egg path is not, so ours emits an `add_entity`/removal pair where vanilla emits nothing at all. Not yet measured on the wire — the capture is queued.
* 2026-09-05 **`docs/FINDINGS.md` is the merge queue's largest collision: 588 of the 595 pairs of the 37 branches ahead of main that touch it (98.8%).** It was created 2026-08-19, the day after `.gitattributes`' coverage sweep concluded the surface was closed at 19 pairs of 1035, so it could not appear in that ranking. `merge=docregions to-eof check=unique-bullet-line` takes it to 37 of 595 — 551 pairs freed.
* 2026-09-05 `ci/checks/test-targets.toml` is the second: 193 of 630 pairs over 36 branches. It falls to 18 of 630 under the driver, 175 pairs freed. Its shape is `ci/t0.sh`'s rather than `ci/figures.toml`'s — a new `[[bucket]]` lands beside the one it is topically closest to, not at a tail (`light-engine-2`'s six hunks start at 182, 250, 274, 287, 628, 709) — so the region is the whole body with `check=toml-buckets`, which refuses a duplicated id and a target listed in two buckets.
* 2026-09-05 **`tools/oracle-runner/src/trace.rs` does NOT collide between the three landing branches.** `light-engine-2` (10 hunks) and `advancements` (4) auto-merge on it, and `gate-instruments` does not touch the file at all — its projection-source work is 41 lines of `compare.rs`. A 421-commit rebase of `light-engine-2` onto main conflicts at commit 287 on `ci/checks/test-targets.toml`, never on `trace.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 **`gate-instruments` does not move the projection id**: its `compare.rs` change is entirely comment and test text, which `projection_body` strips, so main and `gate-instruments` are both `proj:1277e43a7e157183`. main's own `ci/checks/gameplay-floor.toml` pins `proj:7cefde0fb56d9f3e` and is therefore **STALE PROJECTION on main right now**; `gate-instruments`' floor re-derivation is the repair and `advancements` and `light-engine-2` both still carry the stale pin too. `advancements` alone is `proj:2538a57f06c76644`, `light-engine-2` alone `proj:9823c4267825d586` (measured from its built binary in `~/rustmc-light2`), and the union of all three `proj:fc5dab50ab0b3eae`. Re-derive the floor once, after the last of them, not once per landing.
* 2026-09-05 `trace::digest` is FNV-1a **with a mistyped prime** — `0x1000_0000_01b3`, one hex zero more than FNV-64's `0x100000001b3`. Harmless for "did these bytes change", but any projection id re-derived off-tree from "FNV-1a 64" comes out wrong in the high 32 bits and right in the low 32, which reads like a near-collision rather than a wrong constant.
* 2026-09-05 The `light_update` subset capture died at `SUBSET-EXIT=255` six minutes into the corpus-lock queue because **255 is ssh's own exit code, not the script's**: it was `ssh -n threadripper '...t2-gameplay-subset.sh...'` and the connection went with the caller. The script's own exits are 1, 2 and 4, so a caller-written `SUBSET-EXIT=` sentinel could not distinguish "the run failed" from "the transport died" — the sentinel was unfalsifiable in exactly the sense `ci/run-detached.sh`'s header describes. Re-run under `ci/run-detached.sh`, whose sentinel is written by a `setsid nohup` wrapper on the box: past 22 minutes in the same queue at the time of writing.
* 2026-09-05 **`ci/t2-gameplay-subset.sh` quieted vanilla and not the subject, for its whole life.** The gate sends four substrate game rules to both servers — `ci/t2-gameplay.sh`'s own comment says an un-quieted subject random-ticks every rig's grass at speed 3, this server's `random_tick_speed` default being vanilla's 3 — and the subset sent them to vanilla only, passing no `--console` to `rustmc-server` so it could not have sent them at all. Every subset observation this project has taken was of a subject that random-ticked, advanced its clock and cycled its weather against a reference that did none of those things, in the one instrument whose purpose is to make a red seen here evidence about a red seen in the gate. A scenario declaring a `[[gamerule]]` window could not run in the subset at all, for the same missing flag.
* 2026-09-05 **Moving a substrate constant out of `ci/t2-gameplay.sh` silently widens the reference cache.** Its `harness` key component hashes that file, and the comment beside `SUBSTRATE_GAMERULES` said "change a rule, change the substrate, miss the cache, boot vanilla" — true only while the array lived there. `ci/gameplay-substrate.sh` and `ci/gameplay-difficulty.sh` are in the key now, and so is the **value** `GAMEPLAY_DIFFICULTY`, which is an environment variable and therefore invisible to any file hash: without it a `GAMEPLAY_DIFFICULTY=easy` run is served a reference recorded at `peaceful`, where vanilla creates no monster at all.
* 2026-09-05 **`REQUIRED_FLOOR` in `ci/check-checks-present.py` has to be raised when a check is ADDED, not only lowered when one is removed**, and nothing says so until the falsifier fires. It was 14 against a manifest of 14; two checks were added and the floor left alone, and `--check` then counted 16 against a floor of 14 and passed with a row of slack. The only thing that catches the one removal a tree stays self-consistent after — check, `ci/t0.sh` invocation and manifest row all deleted together — is that floor, so at 14 of 16 the removal lands on 15, clears it, and `ci/check-checks-present-falsify.sh` case 4 goes red with "a removed check went unrefused". Reads exactly like a defect in the guard and is a defect in the caller. If you add a required check tonight, bump the constant in the same commit.
* 2026-09-06 **A substrate reader that parses one file goes blind the moment the substrate is split across three, and `ci/mob-substrate.py` did.** Moving `SUBSTRATE_GAMERULES` into `ci/gameplay-substrate.sh` and the difficulty into `ci/gameplay-difficulty.sh` left it reading `0 server properties` and `0 game rules` from `ci/t2-gameplay.sh` — a T0 red that reads like the corpus lost its suppressions when the suppressions had only moved. Its `control()` refusal is what stopped it reporting a world with no suppressions, which is the arrangement working. The reader now derives its file list from the gate's own `. "$ROOT/ci/…"` lines rather than naming a second file, and refuses a setting whose value it cannot resolve (`difficulty=$GAMEPLAY_DIFFICULTY` with no default anywhere is a claim about a world nobody booted). `ci/mob-substrate-falsify.sh` gains 3b and 3c for both.
* 2026-09-06 **`ci/t2-gameplay-subset.sh`'s day-time confirmation can never match, on all six branches that carry it.** It greps `Set the time to $SUBSTRATE_DAY_TIME`; 26.2's `TimeCommand` references `commands.time.set.absolute` and never `commands.time.set`, so vanilla prints `Set minecraft:overworld to 6000 tick(s)` — read out of the failed run's own log. The `time set` command WORKED; only the confirmation was fiction, and it killed a subset run **after 109 minutes in the corpus-lock queue**. `ci/t2-gameplay.sh` already derives the string from the pinned jar with `lang_re`, and left the subset on hardcoded English — the same "the correction landed in one of the two files" shape as the difficulty and the game rules. `lang_re` is defined inside `ci/t2-gameplay.sh`, which is why the subset could not reuse it; the fix is to hoist it into a sourceable file. Neither the day-time pin nor `ci/lang-message-re.py` is on main, so this belongs to whoever owns `SUBSTRATE_DAY_TIME` (`rec-sky`, `sky-light-model`, `light-engine`, `light-engine-2`, `stack-worldgen`, `stackwg-work`).
* 2026-09-05 **Vanilla emits 98 distinct sound events across the 32 corpus recordings on the box, and 20 of them this tree cannot produce at all.** The gap, by observation count: `block.piston.extend` 120, `entity.pig.ambient` 61, `block.wooden_door.open` 60, `block.note_block.harp` 60, `entity.pig_big.ambient` 40, `item.flintandsteel.use` 30, `entity.pig_mini.ambient` 26, `entity.player.swim` 21, `entity.player.splash` 10, `block.tripwire.attach` 8, `item.bucket.empty` 7, then `block.shulker_box.open`, `entity.copper_golem.spawn`, `block.piston.contract`, `block.bell.use`, `entity.bat.ambient`, `entity.pig_big.hurt`, `entity.player.attack.weak`, `item.bucket.fill`, `item.bucket.empty_lava`. `./ci/sound-emission-gap.py`, 4,751 sound observations; the figure is a **lower bound** because the `.step` arm over-credits `entity.pig.step`, which is a mob's sound and not a block sound type.

* 2026-09-05 **`ServerLevel` has three broadcast radii and this server was using none of them.** `playSeededSound` passes `SoundEvent.getRange(volume)` = `fixedRange.orElse(volume > 1.0f ? 16.0f * volume : 16.0f)`; `levelEvent` passes a constant `64.0` at the block's **raw** coordinates, not its centre; `sendParticles` uses `blockPosition().closerToCenterThan(vec, longDistance ? 512 : 32)`, which measures from the player's block **centre** rather than their position and so disagrees with `PlayerList.broadcast` by up to half a block on each axis. All three comparisons are `dcmpg; ifge` — exclusive. Ported in `crates/rustmc-server/src/playerlist.rs`, branch `sounds`.
* 2026-09-05 **No built-in sound event in 26.2 carries a fixed range: `SoundEvent.fixedRange()` is empty for all 1,968, so every sound this server or vanilla plays broadcasts at `volume > 1.0f ? 16.0f * volume : 16.0f` and nothing else.** Three agreeing derivations: a bootstrapped walk of `BuiltInRegistries.SOUND_EVENT` reports 0; `SoundEvents.<clinit>` makes 1,905 registrations (1,665 `register(String)`, 236 `registerForHolder(String)`, four by `Identifier`) and **none** through the private `register(Identifier, Identifier, float)` that would call `createFixedRangeEvent`; and across all 7,434 classes in the jar the string `createFixedRangeEvent` appears in exactly two constant pools, `SoundEvent`'s own and that unreachable helper. The field exists for **datapack** sounds, via `SoundEvent.DIRECT_CODEC`. Found by a generator guard written the other way round — "zero means a broken read" — which failed on its first run; the guard is now `!= 0` plus a positive control that builds a fixed-range event by hand, because "no event has one" and "the dumper cannot read the field" are the same number. `ci/sound-particle-golden.sh`, branch `sounds`.
* 2026-09-05 **A comment in our own source was refuted by the recordings: `crates/rustmc-server/src/itemuse.rs` said of `item.flintandsteel.use` that "`ctx.getPlayer()` is excluded from it either way, so no scenario in this corpus can hear it".** The exclusion is real and it excludes the *actor*; the corpus reads both streams, and `flint-and-steel-lights-a-fire` carries the event on the **observer's** stream 30 times across the 32 recordings on this box, at `[2.5, 1.5, 0.5]` — `relPos`'s centre, not the clicked block's. The sound is emitted now, at volume 1.0 with pitch `nextFloat() * 0.4F + 0.8F`; the other stated reason for dropping it, that the pitch is an unmodelled draw, stopped applying when `crate::sounds` began producing drawn pitches for the lever, the chest and the piston and `tools/oracle-runner` stopped comparing the field.

Nothing needs to carry `location`. `ci/sound-particle-golden.sh`'s own output line.

* 2026-09-06 **`430-a-note-block-retunes-when-right-clicked` diverges at observation 0 on the note block's INSTRUMENT, not on its sound: vanilla writes `instrument=harp` and this server writes `instrument=basedrum` for the same placement on superflat.** `NoteBlock.setInstrument` reads `getBlockState(pos.above()).instrument()` first and falls back to `getBlockState(pos.below()).instrument()`, mapping any `worksAboveNoteBlock` result there to `HARP` — so the instrument is a `BlockBehaviour.Properties` field of the *neighbour*, and this server has no table for it. The note itself is right (`note=1` on both sides), so the sound port behind it is untested by this check until the placement is fixed; the divergence is upstream of every kind `430` observes. * 2026-09-06 **`placement.json` classifies `minecraft:note_block` as `yawOnly: true, neighbourSensitive: false`, and it is neither: `NoteBlock.getStateForPlacement` is one line — `setInstrument(ctx.getLevel(), ctx.getClickedPos(), defaultBlockState())`, offsets 0-16 — which reads `getBlockState(pos.above()).instrument()` and falls back to `getBlockState(pos.below()).instrument()`, and touches no yaw at all.** Its row in the table carries **two** states, `instrument=harp` and `instrument=basedrum`, so the probe did see the variation and the classifier attributed it to yaw; our server then picks one deterministically by look direction and writes `basedrum` where the world says `harp`. That is the whole of `430-a-note-block-retunes-when-right-clicked`'s divergence, at observation 0, upstream of every kind it observes — the `NOTE` cycle behind it is right on both sides (`note=1`). A truly yaw-only block cannot produce two different instruments; the two-state row is the evidence the classification is wrong. `tools/goldengen/vanilla/PlacementGolden.java` and `crates/rustmc-server/src/placement.rs`; not fixed on branch `sounds`, which owns the sound and not the placement.

* 2026-09-06 `ServerLevel.tickChunk`'s ice-and-snow arm **is** `randomTickSpeed`-dependent, refuting the brief that asked for the weather port: offsets 32-76 are `for (i = 0; i < <param 2>; i++) if (random.nextInt(48) == 0) tickPrecipitation(getBlockRandomPos(minX, 0, minZ, 15))`, and `ServerChunkCache.lambda$tickChunks$0` passes the `GameRules.RANDOM_TICK_SPEED` it read as that second parameter. At the default speed of 3 a chunk attempts precipitation 3x/tick at p=1/48. `crates/rustmc-server/src/weather.rs::PRECIPITATION_DRAWS_PER_CHUNK_TICK`.
* 2026-09-06 `Level.isRaining()` crosses on tick **20** of a rain ramp, not the 21 exact decimal predicts, and on **81** ramping down from 1.0, not 80. The level is an `f32` accumulated `0.01f` at a time; twenty of those sum to 0.20000002980232239, and the `> 0.2` compare happens in `double` after an `f2d` that preserves the overshoot. A port carrying the level as `f64` or as a tick count is one tick wrong on every storm. Pinned with a double-arithmetic control in `weather::tests::is_raining_crosses_where_f32_accumulation_puts_it`.
* 2026-09-06 A rain transition emits the **rain level twice in one tick** — the guarded send at `advanceWeatherCycle` offset 372 fires because the level moved, and the unconditional one at 509 fires because it is inside the `wasRaining != isRaining()` arm — and emits **four** packets rather than five in a world that is not thundering, because the guarded thunder send at 412 sees `oThunderLevel == thunderLevel == 0` while the arm send at 533 fires anyway. The two guarded sends also use `broadcastAll(Packet, ResourceKey<Level>)` and the three arm sends use `broadcastAll(Packet)`: dimension-scoped against server-wide, invisible on a one-dimension server and carried as `weather::Audience` so a second cannot inherit the wrong one.
* 2026-09-06 **Ice and snow are unreachable in the gameplay corpus at any settle length**, not slow: `Biome.shouldFreeze` returns false on `warmEnoughToRain` and `Biome.shouldSnow` reduces to `!warmEnoughToRain` through `getPrecipitationAt`/`coldEnoughToSnow`, both of which are `getTemperature(pos, seaLevel) >= 0.15f` (`ldc float 0.15f`, offset 6) — and the superflat biome is `minecraft:plains` at temperature **0.8** (the jar's `data/minecraft/worldgen/biome/plains.json`, and `crate::flat`'s default). False at every position, for all 110 scenarios, by construction. The third effect, `LayeredCauldronBlock.handlePrecipitation`, survives the biome and dies on rate: at `random_tick_speed = 1000` it is 1000/48 attempts per chunk-tick x 1/256 columns x 0.05 (`CauldronBlock.shouldHandlePrecipitation`'s `nextFloat() < 0.05f`) = 0.00407/tick, so **1129 ticks = 56.5 s of settle for 99% confidence**, against the corpus's usual 1.5-4 s. `docs/PARITY-ROADMAP.md` §107.
* 2026-09-06 26.2 does **not** keep weather in `level.dat`: `ServerLevel.getWeatherData()` is `this.server.getWeatherData()`, one server-wide `WeatherData` `SavedData` with type id `"weather"` and codec keys `clear_weather_time`, `rain_time`, `thunder_time`, `raining`, `thundering`. The four `float` levels (`rainLevel`, `oRainLevel`, `thunderLevel`, `oThunderLevel`) are per-`Level` and are not saved at all — they are rebuilt by `ServerLevel.prepareWeather`, which snaps to 1.0 rather than ramping. Recorded for persistence-2, which owns the save side.

* 2026-09-06 §26.3's `advance_weather` NULL (reference projection empty in all six `probe-advance-weather` runs; §21.5 work-order row 8) is true of the route it measured and **not of the subsystem**. Turning the game rule on waits for `RAIN_DELAY`'s 12,000-tick floor and then draws from the unseeded `level.random`; `/weather rain 300` does neither — `setWeatherParameters` writes the flags directly and the level ramp is outside the rule, so `isRaining()` crosses on tick 20 (1.0 s), and `WeatherCommand.getDuration` samples its provider **only** when the duration argument is absent, so an explicit duration touches no generator and the recording is reproducible by construction. Row 8 is OBSERVABLE-blocked-on-`Step::Console`, not NULL. `docs/PARITY-ROADMAP.md` §107.1.

So 60 is a *ceiling* ended early by `minecraft:player_loaded` (44), and a fixed 60-tick immunity keeps a loaded, moving player immune for up to three seconds after vanilla stopped.

* 2026-09-05 `MinecraftServer.AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL = 6000` is the CONSTRUCTOR's initial value and nothing reads it again; every later interval is `computeNextAutosaveInterval()` = `max(MIMINUM_AUTOSAVE_TICKS = 100, (int)(tickrate * 300.0f))`, which is 6000 only at the default rate of 20.0. `onTickRateChanged` clamps in ONE direction — a shorter computed interval pulls the pending save in, a longer one does not. * 2026-09-05 A trade set's RNG is `RandomSequences.get`, a `computeIfAbsent` over a **saved** map, so `minecraft:trade_set/farmer/level_1` is created once per world and continues across every farmer that ever levels in it — two farmers do not get the same trades. * 2026-09-05 `MerchantOffer.getModifiedPrice` does not exist in 26.2; it is `getModifiedCostCount(ItemCost)`, and its `Math.max(0, ..)` wraps the **demand term only** — `clamp(base + max(0, floor((base*demand)*mult)) + specialPriceDiff, 1, cost.itemStack().getMaxStackSize())` — so a negative `specialPriceDiff` still discounts while negative demand cannot, and the floor of 1 means no trade is ever free. `updateDemand` is `demand += uses - (maxUses - uses)`, signed and unbounded below. `crates/rustmc-entity/src/trading/offer.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 Five of the six per-offer integers in `merchant_offers` (cb 52) are `writeInt` — fixed 32-bit BE — while the packet's own three fields are varints; `specialPriceDiff` and `demand` are routinely negative, and a varint there desynchronises the rest of the packet rather than giving a wrong number. * 2026-09-05 `Villager.shouldRestock` is **not a predicate**: it writes `lastRestockCheckDay` on every call and, on `now > last+12000` or a day roll, rewinds `lastRestockGameTime` and runs `resetNumberOfRestocks` -> `catchUpDemand`, which resets uses and then runs `updateDemand` once per restock the villager did NOT do today (each round is `-maxUses` with uses at 0). That is the whole "an unbought trade gets cheaper over days" mechanism; treating the method as pure freezes `numberOfRestocksToday` at 2 and the villager restocks twice and never again. * 2026-09-05 `VillagerData.getMaxXpPerLevel(5)` is **0**, not 250: `NEXT_LEVEL_XP_THRESHOLDS` is `{0,10,70,150,250}` and the getter is guarded by `canLevelUp(level)` = `level >= 1 && level < 5`. DOWN : UP`. Reading it the other way put every button on its ceiling: 8,784 disagreeing cells.

Nothing anywhere plays a sound — the break sound is the client's own response to the entity event.

Routing every crop through the `CropBlock` version takes one draw too many and desynchronises every later draw on that level's random source — a divergence with no local symptom. `BeetrootBlock`'s is `super / 3` on that same single draw, so it is 0 for a roll of 2: **a beetroot fails one bone meal in four and still consumes it**, because `growCrop`'s `stack.shrink(1)` is at offset 86, inside the `isValidBonemealTarget` branch and below `isBonemealSuccess`. `Mth.nextInt(rs, lo, hi)` is `nextInt(hi - lo + 1) + lo`, so the bound is 4 and not 3.
* 2026-09-05 **`BonemealableBlock` is 45 classes over 63 blocks and nothing in this repo had ever said which.** `ci/bonemeal-golden.sh` is the census plus `isValidBonemealTarget` for all 721 states (303 valid, 0 refused). Five are `CropBlock` and are ported; the other 58 answer `Unported`, kept distinct from `Pass` because a gap that reports as a pass is a gap nobody counts. Most of the 58 place a **configured feature** in `performBonemeal` — a sapling grows a tree — which is worldgen reached through an item, and a wrong tree is a worse answer than no tree.

A decoder written from the wrong packet consumes 22 bytes of a 10-byte body. `handleUseItem`'s `ackBlockChangesUpTo` at offset 25 is **unconditional and above** the empty-hand return at 58-78, so a right-click into the air with an empty hand still corrects a client that mispredicted a block; that is the packet's whole observable answer on this server and it is now sent.
* 2026-09-05 **Every stack this server has ever put on the wire has claimed to be its item's prototype.** `crate::menu::write_item` writes `0, 0` for the `DataComponentPatch`, and `0, 0` does not mean "no components" — it means "exactly the prototype's", so a damaged pickaxe, a renamed sign and a full bundle are all indistinguishable from a fresh one. `DataComponentPatch$3.encode` is two varint counts, then each present entry as `type id + the component's own codec`, then each removed entry as a bare type id — **added first, removals last**, and there is no length prefix around a payload, so a component whose codec a port has not read must not be written at all. `crate::patch` writes the framing and the three payloads it has read; the type ids are `item_props.json`'s new `componentTypeIds`, all 111 of them rather than the 59 with a default, because a patch names components no prototype has (`minecraft:custom_name` is id 6).
* 2026-09-05 **`ci/command-parse-golden.sh` could not succeed on any tree, ever, and one broken generator makes every table after it unregenerable.** `CommandParseGolden` recorded `String.valueOf(e.getType())` for its `error` column; `SimpleCommandExceptionType` and `DynamicCommandExceptionType` override neither `toString` nor `hashCode`, so the value was `…DynamicCommandExceptionType@<identity hash>` — a different string on every JVM run. The script dumps twice and `cmp`s, so it failed on every run on every tree, and `ci/gen-gameplay-tables.sh` **exits on the first failing generator**, so no table listed after `command_parse.json` was regenerated anywhere on the box. That is how eight `block_props` tests came back red on a branch whose only relation to them was adding a column. The comment above the line said "the type's simple name, not the message" and the code did something else; it is `e.getType().getClass().getSimpleName()` now, two runs byte-identical, and `command_parse_vanilla`'s four tests are unaffected because nothing under `crates/` reads that column.
* 2026-09-05 **`StandingAndWallBlockItem.getPlacementState` is a walk over the player's LOOK, not a test of the clicked face**, and `crates/rustmc-server/src/world_send.rs` had approximated it as "a side face gets the wall variant" with the divergence named in a comment. The walk is `Direction.orderedByNearest(player)` reordered by `BlockPlaceContext.getNearestLookingDirections` — the clicked face's *opposite* moved to the front, and only when its index is `> 0` — then the first direction that is not `attachmentDirection.getOpposite()` and whose block survives. So a torch clicked on a floor while looking at a wall can come out standing, and one clicked on a wall that cannot hold it walks on to the next direction. `orderedByNearest` is pure arithmetic on the two rotations with `0.017453292f` as the degree constant, and **its y term is inverted relative to the other two** (`up = sinX < 0`, magnitude `-sinX`) because `xRot` is positive downwards; `makeDirectionArray(p, q, r)` is `{p, q, r, ~r, ~q, ~p}`, which is why the result is always a permutation. `canPlace` inside that walk is `state.canSurvive` alone — a different method from `BlockItem.canPlace`, with a different signature, no `mustSurvive` and no `isUnobstructed`; the obstruction test runs once, on the winner.
* 2026-09-05 **`ci/t0-remote.sh` rsyncs with `--exclude data/vanilla`, so a table regenerated in the working tree never reaches the gate directory** — and the gate then reads whatever `cp -rn` seeded that directory with. A chained run of `ci/gen-gameplay-tables.sh` **before** `ci/t0-remote.sh` therefore does nothing for the gate: it must run *inside* `$RUSTMC_REMOTE_DIR` on the box, between the rsync and T0, which is the order `ci/handoff.sh` uses and the reason it uses it. Nine `block_props` tests reddened on this branch that way, against a copy of the table that predated the column the branch added, and the panic said only ``loads: "no `placeTagOrder`"`` — the missing field and nothing about where or why. `crates/rustmc-behavior/src/block_props.rs:499` now names the file, the generator and that exclusion; a skip is not the alternative, because the early return that `fixture::available` replaced made every test below it **pass** when the extract was absent.
- landing (23 Sep): `items-2`'s three `useOn` sounds (shovel flatten, axe strip, shears) did not compile on main 1049a12d3 because main's `Sound` gained `pos` (the doubles `PlayerList.broadcast` compares) after the fork, E0063 x3; filled with `sounds::block_centre`. `BlockPropsGolden.java` conflicted with main's `lightOcclusionFaces` column; both columns kept, the golden output is generated and untracked.
- landing (24 Sep): main's `Tiles` does not delegate `Blocks::place_props`, so items-2's `BlockItem.canPlace` canSurvive gate answers None (no gate) on the running server and is only exercised against `World` in tests; `ci/check-blocks-delegation.sh` refused at 4c64b06c5, fixed by reading the shared `WorldTables` off `Tiles`.
* 2026-09-06 **An enchantment is a datapack registry entry in 26.2, not a Java subclass, and there are 43 of them.** `net/minecraft/world/item/enchantment/` holds one `final Enchantment` plus `EnchantmentDefinition` and `Cost`; every member ships as JSON in the jar under `data/minecraft/enchantment/` with `weight`, `max_level`, `min_cost`, `max_cost`, `anvil_cost`, `slots`, `supported_items`, optional `primary_items`/`exclusive_set` and an `effects` map. `ci/enchantments-golden.sh` -> `data/vanilla/enchantments.json`: **43 in the code registry, 43 bundled in the jar, 43 written by the data generator, 0 mismatches** after key-canonicalisation, 22 tags, 36 in `#minecraft:in_enchanting_table`, enchantability on 77 items.
* 2026-09-06 **`VanillaRegistries.createLookup()`'s iteration order is NOT the ENCHANTMENT registry's order and using it would have put a wrong id on the wire for all 43.** It hands back `thorns, unbreaking, swift_sneak, fire_protection, ...` (the bootstrap's registration order); the `registry_data` packet for `minecraft:enchantment` decoded out of `data/vanilla/registries.bin` — a real server's bytes — is `aqua_affinity, bane_of_arthropods, binding_curse, ...`, strictly sorted by identifier, because a datapack registry is loaded by `RegistryDataLoader` walking a sorted resource listing. `EnchantmentMenu.slotsChanged` writes `asHolderIdMap().getId(holder)` into `enchantClue`, a `DataSlot`, so the id is observable. `crates/rustmc-server/tests/enchantment_registry_order.rs` compares the two position by position. **Any lane emitting a datapack-registry table from `createLookup()` has the same hazard.**
* 2026-09-06 **The enchanting table's bookshelf clamp is inside `EnchantmentHelper.getEnchantmentCost` (offset 19), not in `slotsChanged`, and the raw count it clamps reaches 32, not 15.** `EnchantingTableBlock.BOOKSHELF_OFFSETS` is `BlockPos.betweenClosedStream(-2,0,-2,2,1,2)` filtered by `abs(x)==2 || abs(z)==2` — the perimeter of a 5x5 at two heights. A port that clamped at the counting site agrees with vanilla on every observable number, so nothing would catch it.
* 2026-09-06 **`slotsChanged` makes seven `RandomSource` calls, not three.** One `setSeed(enchantmentSeed)`, then **two** `nextInt` per slot inside `getEnchantmentCost` — `nextInt(8)` first, then `nextInt(bookshelves + 1)`, with `(bookshelves >> 1)` between them as arithmetic and not a draw — over a first loop that does all three costs, then a **second** loop for the clues in which `getEnchantmentList` reseeds to `enchantmentSeed + slot` before each. Fusing the two loops, or reordering the two cost draws, changes every number after the first and is invisible at 0 bookshelves because `nextInt(1)` still consumes a draw.
* 2026-09-06 **`EnchantmentHelper.getDamageBonus` and `getMobLooting` do not exist in 26.2** and a brief that names them is a version behind. Damage and looting are effect components on the registry entry (`minecraft:damage`, `minecraft:mob_experience`), reached through `modifyDamage` / `processMobExperience` and ~40 other component-driven entry points. Not ported here: they need the combat path and the item-damage path.

Whoever wants one should add the `generator_for` arm in the same commit as the first reader.

- recipes: `Ingredient` is a `HolderSet<Item>`, so a JSON **array** of ids is legal wherever a `"#tag"` or a bare id is. Our parser read `as_str()` and `filter_map`ped the failures away, which for a shapeless recipe **shortened the ingredient list instead of dropping the recipe**: `dye_black_bed` loaded with one ingredient, so a lone `minecraft:black_dye` in a crafting grid assembled a black bed. 65 of 323 shapeless recipes loaded corrupted this way and 13 of 733 shaped were dropped whole — `main` served **1,043** of the 1,056 it is credited with, and 65 of those 1,043 were wrong. Fixed by `Ingredient::Items`; `crates/rustmc-server/tests/recipes_table.rs::a_list_ingredient_is_a_set_and_not_a_dropped_field`. (recipes)
- recipes: the `getRecipeFor` tie-break is **first match in sorted recipe-id order**, not registry order — `RecipeManager.prepare` offset 0 is `new java.util.TreeMap()`, walked by `SortedMap.forEach` into the list `RecipeMap.create` feeds to an `ImmutableMultimap` (insertion-order-preserving per key), and `getRecipeFor` is `findFirst` over `byType`. The sort is `Identifier.compareTo`, which compares **path first, namespace second**. Our `serde_json::Map` (a `BTreeMap`, no `preserve_order`) over `ci/dump-recipes.sh`'s bare-path keys is byte-for-byte that order for a vanilla-only table; `recipes.rs`'s claim that "the two are not the same" was wrong. Gated with a positive control at `tests/recipes_table.rs::the_load_order_is_vanillas_sorted_path_order`. (recipes)
- recipes: 26.2 has **no** `ArmorDyeRecipe`, `MapCloningRecipe`, `TippedArrowRecipe` or `ShulkerBoxColoring` class; they are replaced by data-driven `crafting_dye` (6), `crafting_imbue` (1), `crafting_transmute` (33) and `crafting_decorated_pot` (1) serializers. The 529 unserved recipes are: stonecutting 319, smelting 73, crafting_transmute 33, blasting 25, smithing_trim 18, crafting_special_bannerduplicate 16, smithing_transform 12, campfire_cooking 9, smoking 9, crafting_dye 6, and nine singletons. `python3 -c` over `data/vanilla/recipes.json`, 1,585 entries. (recipes)
- recipes: `ItemTags` did not follow a `#other_tag` entry, and 50 of the 224 item tags in the 26.2 extract carry one — 24 of them have **no** plain entries at all. `#minecraft:trimmable_armor` is `["#foot_armor", "#leg_armor", "#chest_armor", "#head_armor"]` and resolved to the empty set, so 29 recipes had an ingredient that matched nothing (18 `smithing_trim`, 10 `crafting_shaped`, 1 `smelting`) and one more resolved short. `TagLoader.build` feeds every tag to a `DependencySorter` and calls `orderByDependencies`, so nested references are flattened in dependency order and a cycle is refused; ported as an explicit walk with a `visiting` stack. Tests: `recipes::tests::a_nested_tag_reference_is_flattened` (two levels, declared out of order) and `a_cyclic_tag_reference_terminates`. (recipes)
- recipes: `TransmuteRecipe`'s `material_count` defaults to `MinMaxBounds.Ints.exactly(1)` (`DEFAULT_MATERIAL_COUNT`, `iconst_1; invokestatic exactly`), **not** to an open range. The `orElse(1)` / `orElse(8)` in `minMaterialCount`/`maxMaterialCount` are what an explicitly open bound means, and conflating the two lets a bundle be dyed with up to eight dyes. All 33 26.2 transmute documents omit the field. (recipes)
- recipes: `Ingredient.testOptionalIngredient` is `opt.map(i -> i.test(stack)).orElseGet(stack::isEmpty)` — an **absent** smithing template or addition requires an *empty* slot, not "anything". Both smithing serializers reach it through `SmithingRecipe`'s `default matches`, which is three ingredient tests and nothing else. (recipes)
- recipes: REFUSED — `SmithingTrimRecipe.assemble`, `map_cloning`, and the 31 remaining `crafting_*` documents (6 `crafting_dye`, 16 `crafting_special_bannerduplicate`, 3 firework, and 6 singletons). Every one computes its result out of data components (`applyTrim` reads `PROVIDES_TRIM_MATERIAL` and returns `ItemStack.EMPTY` at offsets 48 and 73; `computeResult` copies `getComponentsPatch`), and `crate::item::ItemStack` is `{item, count}`. A transcription would hand out a plausible wrong item and make both `EMPTY` branches unreachable, so no test of them could go red. The 18 trims still **match**. (recipes)
- recipes: `ShapedRecipePattern.shrink` trims a pattern to its bounding box before storing it, and we did not — so **seven shaped recipes could not be crafted on this server at all**: `creaking_heart`, `mace`, `spyglass` and the four `waxed_*_chiseled_copper`. Each is written with blank border columns (`spyglass` is `[" # ", " X ", " X "]`, a 1x3 recipe); we kept the 3x3 while `CraftingInput.of` trims the player's grid to 1x3, so the sizes could never match. Nothing said so — a recipe that never matches is indistinguishable from one nobody tried. Found by the golden, not by reading. `crates/rustmc-server/tests/recipes_table.rs::the_seven_loosely_written_patterns_craft`. (recipes)
- recipes: `ci/recipe-match-golden.sh` + `tools/goldengen/vanilla/RecipeMatchGolden.java` — the jar's own `RecipeMap.getRecipesFor(...).findFirst()` over grids built from `Recipe.placementInfo()`, so neither side of the comparison is our reading of the recipe documents. 1,585 recipes decoded, 224 item tags bound through vanilla's `TagLoader.build`, 24 `CustomRecipe`s reported unplaceable, **3,122 vectors** (1,561 positive + 1,561 bedrock-poisoned negatives). Our matcher agrees with **3,094 of 3,122**; the 28 disagreements are the declared refusals and the test asserts that set exactly, in both directions. **256 of the 1,561 positive grids are won by a recipe other than the one they were built from** — a table in the wrong sort order fails 256 vectors, which is the tie-break no unit test written from the same reading can catch. (recipes)
- recipes: two `RecipeMatchGolden` steps that would each have produced a silent oracle: `VanillaRegistries.createLookup()` leaves item tags unbound (every `#tag` ingredient resolves empty, every case reports no winner) and `MappedRegistry.bindTags` is refused after `Bootstrap.bootStrap()` with "Registry is already frozen" — the route that works is `prepareTagReload(new TagLoader.LoadResult<>(...)).apply()`. Data components must be bound separately (`DATA_COMPONENT_INITIALIZERS.build(registries)`), or every `new ItemStack(...)` throws "Components not bound yet". The script refuses a file with fewer than 90% of positives matching, and refuses one where every grid is won by its own recipe. (recipes)

The recipe table reaches the client through `recipe_book_add` displays instead. `forSingleInput` tests `getType()==type && instanceof SingleItemRecipe`, so **stonecutting contributes to no property set** despite being a `SingleItemRecipe`. Two boots of unmodified vanilla send identical content in different bytes, so such a gate would pin one JVM's hash salt. The content is gated instead: 7 sets, **303 item entries, all agreeing with vanilla's own reflected `IngredientExtractor`s** (`tests/recipe_match_golden.rs::the_update_recipes_property_sets_match_vanillas`). (recipes)
- recipes: the stonecutter half of `update_recipes` **is** order-deterministic where the property-set map is not, and the difference is structural: `finalizeRecipeLoading` builds the map with `Collectors.toUnmodifiableMap` (hash-probed, per-run salt) but builds the `SelectableRecipe.SingleInputSet` by walking `RecipeMap.values()` — the `byKey` ImmutableMap, i.e. sorted recipe id — and appending to a `List`. All **319 entries agree with vanilla in order**, ingredient sets included (`tests/recipe_match_golden.rs::the_stonecutter_set_matches_vanillas_in_order`). (recipes)
- recipes: **`getRecipeFor`'s `lastRecipe` argument is a behaviour, not a cache.** Offsets 0..25 of the four-argument overload are `if (last != null && last.value().matches(input, level)) return Optional.of(last)`, **before** the sorted walk — so a crafting menu holding a last recipe gets that one even when a lower-sorting recipe also matches, and `CraftingMenu.slotChangedCraftingGrid` always passes one. Measured: 256 of the 1,561 probe grids are won by a recipe other than the one they were built from, and passing the built-from recipe as `last` flips **all 256**. Ported as `Recipes::find_with_last`/`find_single_with_last`; falsified by stubbing the argument out, which reds **254** of the 3,378 vectors (2 coincide with declared refusals). `crates/rustmc-server/src/click.rs` still calls the no-last path — that wiring belongs to whichever lane owns the crafting menu. (recipes)

* 2026-09-05 **`light_golden`'s 3744/3744 could not see the decrease queue at all, and five injections prove it rather than argue it.** Every case in that suite reaches the engine through `propagateLightSources`, which enqueues nothing but increases, so `LightEngine.propagateDecreases` drains an empty queue and **a port with no decrease queue passes all of it**. Measured on the tree: swapping the decrease and increase passes, replacing `repeatFirstLayer` with a dark layer, deleting the skip-one-direction bit, reading emptiness off values instead of write history, and keeping a stale `ChunkSkyLightSources` are all `static=pass`. `ci/light-incremental-falsify.sh` asserts BOTH verdicts per injection, so the falsifier is also the standing evidence for the blind spot. Same shape as `slab_roof`'s 6,762 states: a count of what a check COULD be wrong about is not a reading.
* 2026-09-05 **`tools/goldengen/vanilla/LightGolden.java`'s mutator recorded that an 8x8 stone lid darkens nothing, because `ProtoChunk.setBlockState` runs its whole light branch only when `status.isOrAfter(INITIALIZE_LIGHT)` AND `lightEngine != null`** — and the golden's grid is built `new ProtoChunk(pos, UpgradeData.EMPTY, heights, containers, null)` at the default status. So neither `LevelLightEngine.updateSectionStatus` nor `ChunkSkyLightSources.update` ran, and `SkyLightEngine.checkNode` read the **pre-edit** `getLowestSourceY` and decided the sealed position was still a sky source. The oracle was wrong, not the port: 86 disagreeing sections became 50 with `updateSectionStatus`, then 9 with the sources update. **A golden that drives a `ProtoChunk` is not driving a `LevelChunk`, and every side effect the real path has must be done by hand or it is silently absent.**
* 2026-09-05 **A from-scratch relight and vanilla's incremental repair are different answers for the same final blocks, and the difference is not confined to the registered `relit` case.** `light_golden.rs` lights the golden's dumped world from scratch, and the dumped world is the world AFTER a mutation — so adding `roof_opened` and `sky_sealed` reddened it on cases where neither answer is defective. Mutated cases are now skipped there and named in its output; the mutation test reproduces the edit instead. The general rule: **a golden that carries edit history cannot be graded by a consumer whose input is only the final state.**
* 2026-09-05 `oracle-work/vanilla-r7` carries **362 chunks at `minecraft:full`, every one with `isLightOn: 1` and both `BlockLight`/`SkyLight` arrays saved** — a vanilla-authored light oracle that has been on the box unread since the harvest was taken. 289 of them have all eight neighbours loaded. `crates/rustmc-world/src/anvil.rs` discarded all of it, and the light arrays live disproportionately on the **sections with no `block_states`** (the padding above and below the terrain), which the section loop `continue`s past — so a reader that decoded light after that `continue` would silently drop the brightest sections in the file.
* 2026-09-05 **`SectionPos.aroundAndAtBlockPos` is one to eight sections, never 27.** It takes `blockToSectionCoord` of each coordinate minus and plus one and iterates the inclusive box: one section for a write strictly inside one, two across a face, four across an edge, eight at a corner. `setStoredLevel` feeds `sectionsAffectedByLightUpdates` through it, so that is the mask a partial `light_update` carries — a port that reused `updateSectionStatus`'s 3x3x3 cube would send up to 27 sections where vanilla sends one.
* 2026-09-05 **No `light_update` packet has ever been recorded by this project's corpus and none can be**: `tools/oracle-runner/src/trace.rs:247` classifies `cb::LIGHT_UPDATE` as housekeeping and drops it, alongside `KEEP_ALIVE` and `SET_TIME`. Any lane that wants to grade the live light path on the wire has to change that filter first, take a fresh recording, and re-sweep `ci/corpus-falsifiable.py` — it is not a matter of writing a scenario against what is already on disk.
* 2026-09-05 **A saved region file does not carry the two light-only padding sections unless the engine has data in them**, so deriving a dimension's geometry as `min(section Y) + 1` from a harvest gives `min_section_y = -3` where vanilla has -4. `oracle-work/vanilla-r7`'s chunks carry sections -4..19, exactly `getSectionsCount()` = 24. The cost of that off-by-one is not a loud error: `min_light_section` becomes -4, section -5 is clipped out of the stored set **and** out of the scoring in the same step, and the comparison comes back clean over a window one section short at each end. Caught on the run that added the `getLightSectionCount() == 26` cross-check, which is the argument for turning a derived quantity into an assertion rather than a comment.
* 2026-09-05 **A region file saves a light layer only when it was materialised, so 13,371 of the 15,028 section/layer pairs in a 289-chunk window carry no array at all** — 89% of the artefact is not comparable and a nibble count over the other 11% has to say so. The sharp reading of the gap is not "how much light do we put where vanilla saved nothing" (above the terrain we *should* put 15 everywhere): it is whether **our** layer is uniform in exactly the pairs vanilla declined to save, because uniformity is what made vanilla decline. Ours is uniform in all 13,371, which is what stops the residual hiding in the blind spot.
* 2026-09-05 **`World`'s section light is a `OnceLock`, so the player-edit overlay it reads is whatever `edits` held the first time a chunk was sent and every later edit is invisible to the light on the wire.** Inert today only because `RUSTMC_SECTION_LIGHT` defaults off and the classification is `LightData::default()` either way. The engine half of the fix now exists (`crate::light_incremental`'s decrease queue, 2340/2340 post-edit sections against vanilla); the missing half is the send path emitting a partial `light_update` after an edit. Recorded in `world.rs`'s own doc comment as well, because a caveat that lives only in a findings file does not travel.
* 2026-09-05 **26.2's `the_end` dimension type has `"has_skylight": true`**, which contradicts what that dimension did in every older version, and `"min_y": 0, "height": 256` — so the End is a `minSectionY == 0` dimension WITH sky light, which is the sharpest available test of the mask-index/section-Y off-by-five `docs/AGENT-BRIEF.md` warns about. A hand-written `has_sky_light: false` in a comparison's dimension table scored **1,079,016 of the End's saved sky nibbles wrong and read exactly like a port defect**. Read `min_y`/`height`/`has_skylight` out of `generated-data/data/minecraft/dimension_type/<name>.json`; never write them down. The nether is `has_skylight: false` and saves no `SkyLight` array at all, which is a second thing an overworld-only comparison cannot see.
* 2026-09-05 **A positive control that names a block absent from the world under test is not a control.** `light_harvest`'s control made `minecraft:stone` emit light and asserted the figure moved; in the nether harvest that moved **0 of 7,409,664** nibbles, because the nether is netherrack and basalt. Green, and measuring nothing. The control now picks the commonest non-air state in each harvest's own window — `minecraft:deepslate[axis=y]`, `minecraft:netherrack`, `minecraft:end_stone` — and moves 1,611,341 / 4,686,430 / 63,837 nibbles respectively. **Any control that names a constant has to be checked against the population it is run over.**
* 2026-09-05 Our light matches vanilla's saved nibbles exactly in the overworld (0 of 6,787,072 over 289 chunks) and the nether (0 of 7,409,664 over 289 chunks), and differs in the End by **5,609 of 1,863,680 — every one of them sky, and every one in section -1**, the light-only padding section below the End's world bottom, where vanilla is uniformly brighter than us down a gradient (`vanilla 1 ours 0` 384 times, `vanilla 15 ours 9` 104 times). Outside that section the End is exact.
