A failure is no longer cached; `cargo test -p rustmc-server --lib` 876 passed, 0 failed.
- world border: transcribed `WorldBorder` + its two extents + the six clientbound border packets; **312 bit-exact comparisons across 13 vanilla-captured states** pass (`cargo test -p rustmc-server --test world_border`, `crates/rustmc-server/golden/world_border.json` from `ci/border-golden.sh`, 78 packet encodings from vanilla's own `STREAM_CODEC`).
- `serde_json`'s default number parser is **one ULP wrong** on a 17-significant-digit double: `14.600000000000001` (bits 4624408687374709556) comes back from `as_f64()` as `14.6` (…555). Measured 2026-09-05 on `crates/rustmc-server/golden/world_border.json`, serde_json 1.0.151, no `float_roundtrip` feature. **26 `as_f64()` sites exist across `crates/*/tests` and `crates/*/src`** (`grep -c`); any of them comparing a golden double that needs all 17 digits is silently wrong in the last bit. `world_border.json` therefore carries `<field>Bits` and the comparison never reads the decimal.
- `WorldBorder`'s constructor and `Settings.DEFAULT` disagree on `warningTime` — **15 vs 300** — and nothing reconciles them until `applyInitialSettings` runs, so a fresh border announces 15 on the wire (`WorldBorder.<init>` offset 25, `Settings.<clinit>` offset 13).
- scoreboard: five clientbound packets + five enum tables transcribed; **18 vanilla-captured packets byte-identical and 45 enum constants match** (`cargo test -p rustmc-server --test scoreboard`, golden from `ci/scoreboard-golden.sh`).
- `ClientboundSetPlayerTeamPacket.Parameters`' 26.2 codec order is `displayName, playerPrefix, playerSuffix, nameTagVisibility, collisionRule, optional(TeamColor), options:byte` — the **options bitfield is last**, the colour is an `Optional<TeamColor>` (its own class now, ids 0..15, not a `ChatFormatting` subset), and both rule enums are varints. Bytes: `set_player_team/create` = `04 72656473 00 | 08 0008 "The Reds" 08 0001 "[" 08 0001 "]" 02 03 01 0c 02 | 02 06"oracle" 07"subject"`. A body in the older documented order is the same length for an uncoloured team, so it decodes silently with the flags in the visibility field.
- `PlayerTeam.deathMessageVisibility` exists in 26.2 and is **not** in `Parameters` — it never reaches a client.
- `PlayerTeam`'s ctor starts both flags true (`<init>` offsets 26, 31), so a fresh team's `options` byte is **3, not 0**.
- `ClientboundSetScorePacket.score` is `ByteBufCodecs.VAR_INT` — a **plain** varint, not zigzag: `-3` is the five bytes `fd ff ff ff 0f`.
- The four scoreboard enums on the wire go through `ByteBufCodecs.idMapper` (an `id` **field**); `ObjectiveCriteria.RenderType` goes through `writeEnum` (the **ordinal**). All five happen to be 0..n today, so nothing separates them but the bytecode.
- portals: `PortalShape.findAnyShape` transcribed; **144 comparisons across 18 vanilla-captured frames** pass (`cargo test -p rustmc-server --test portal_shape`, golden from `ci/portal-golden.sh` driving vanilla's own search against an in-memory `BlockGetter`).
- `PortalShape` never reads the frame's **corners** — it walks the two sides and the top only, so a frame missing all four corners is a valid portal (`no-corners-3x4`, 3x4, valid=true). A perimeter scan refuses it.
- `createPortalBlocks` writes through `BlockPos.betweenClosed`, which normalises the corners and iterates **x fastest, then y, then z** (`BlockPos$4.computeNext`, `ox = index % dx`). For a WEST-facing portal that is the opposite horizontal direction from the search that found the frame.
- **`Bootstrap.bootStrap()` leaves every block TAG unbound and freezes the registry**, so `BlockState.is(BlockTags.FIRE)` is false inside a bare goldengen JVM and `PortalShape.isEmpty` answers differently from a real server. Caught by a guard, not by luck: the first `ci/portal-golden.sh` run reported `fire-inside-2x3` as 0x0/invalid. `bindTags` throws "Registry is already frozen"; `prepareTagReload(new TagLoader.LoadResult<>(...)).apply()` works. **Any goldengen program that reads a tag has this bug** unless it does the same.
- **villager trades are datapack data in 26.2, not `ItemListing` lambdas** — the brief's premise is refuted. `net.minecraft.world.item.trading.VillagerTrades` is a list of `ResourceKey<VillagerTrade>` constants and nothing else; the trades live in `data/minecraft/villager_trade/**` (388 files), the pools in `tags/villager_trade/**` (73), the amounts in `trade_set/**` (68). No JVM, no seeded RNG and no golden are needed: `ci/dump-villager-trades.sh` reads the extract. **3,104 comparisons across 388 trades** pass against the extract itself (`cargo test -p rustmc-server --test villager_trades`).
- villager trade tags **nest**: 15 of the 73 pool files carry a `#minecraft:` reference, and resolving them changes the pool in both directions because three referenced tags are empty (`common_smith/level_3..5`). `toolsmith/level_2` is 1 entry in its file and 2 resolved; `toolsmith/level_4` is 4 and 3.
- 8 of the 68 trade sets ask for more offers than their pool holds (e.g. `librarian/level_5` draws 3 from 2). Legal: `AbstractVillager.addOffersFromItemListingsWithoutDuplicates` stops when the pool empties. `allow_duplicates` is false in all 68.
- the deal loop's two variants differ in one instruction: with duplicates the drawn entry is `get(k)` and removed **only when its offer is null**; without, it is `remove(k)` unconditionally and the counter advances only on a non-null. Both consume a `nextInt` per iteration, so a null draw still moves the RNG.
- **REFUSED: `VillagerTrade.getOffer` and the `merchant_offers` packet.** `getOffer` applies loot functions (41 trades) and a loot predicate (27), which are the loot layer's; the packet carries `ItemStack.STREAM_CODEC` with component patches. `deal_offers` takes the offer decision as a callback rather than guessing. Nothing here is compared against a vanilla *run* — a `LootContext` needs a `ServerLevel` — so the deal loop is a regression check, not a parity check, and says so.
- online mode crypto: SHA-1, AES-128, AES/CFB8, Montgomery-reduced RSA-1024, PKCS#1 v1.5 and X.509/PKCS#8 DER written from scratch (no new dependency) and **all 7 tests match vanilla's own `Crypt` output** (`cargo test -p rustmc-proto --test crypt`, golden from `ci/crypt-golden.sh` driving `net.minecraft.util.Crypt` in the pinned jar).
- `Crypt.digestData(String serverId, PublicKey pub, SecretKey secret)` hashes its arguments in a **different order from its own parameter list** — serverId, then the **secret**, then the public key (offsets 0-31: `aastore` index 1 is `aload_2`, the secret). Reading the signature and hashing key-then-secret is wrong every time and produces a plausible-looking hash.
- the session-server hash is `new BigInteger(digest).toString(16)`: **signed two's-complement, leading zeros stripped**. Measured on the golden's fixed key: server id `notch` gives `-71b199bfb1e232eaeabcf16ac9b5c18b4a9ce77d` (the complement, not the bytes with a `-` glued on) and server id `-` gives a **38-character** hash. A hex encoder is wrong on both.
- `ServerLoginPacketListenerImpl.serverId` is the constant `""` (field initialiser, `<init>` offset 12) — not the server's name, and not configurable. `shouldAuthenticate` in the clientbound hello is `iconst_1`, always true. The challenge is `Ints.toByteArray(RandomSource.create().nextInt())` — four big-endian bytes.
- **`EncryptionConfig` does not exist in the 26.2 jar** — the brief names it; `unzip -l` finds no such class. The encryption setup is `Crypt.getCipher(mode, key)` called twice from `handleKey` (offsets 103-127), decrypt cipher first, and the key doubles as the IV.
- online-mode handshake: `ServerLoginPacketListenerImpl`'s eight-state machine, `ClientboundHelloPacket` (login cb **1**) and `ServerboundKeyPacket` (login sb **1**) transcribed; **5 tests pass against vanilla's own encoded packets** (`cargo test -p rustmc-server --test online_login`). The 171-byte hello is byte-identical.
- `StringUtil.isValidPlayerName` rejects `c <= 32` and `c >= 127` and has **no minimum length** — `isValidPlayerName("")` is `true`. It is NOT `isAllowedChatCharacter`, which sits beside it and permits the space (`c >= 32`, rejecting only 167 and 127).
- **REFUSED by name: `MinecraftSessionService.hasJoinedServer`.** A live HTTPS call to Mojang whose answer depends on a third party's database; no recording can stand in for it and no golden can be taken. `crates/rustmc-server/src/online.rs::has_joined_server` returns an error naming itself, and `tests/online_login.rs::the_refusals_are_refusals` asserts it stays one — so a stub appearing later reddens a test rather than silently admitting anyone who completes a handshake.
- **NOT IMPLEMENTED: `Crypt.generateKeyPair()`.** Unlike the lookup this one is verifiable without an oracle (n == p*q, Miller-Rabin, e*d ≡ 1). It is absent because it needs a CSPRNG and because with the lookup refused a generated key has nowhere to go; `generate_key_pair()` says so and is asserted to keep saying so.
- dimension travel: `PortalProcessor`, the two nether-portal delay gamerules and `WorldBorder.clampToBounds` transcribed; the clamp is checked against **7 vanilla-captured probes** and the coordinate scale is read from the datapack (`cargo test -p rustmc-server --test dimension_travel`, 5 passed).
- `PortalProcessor.processPortalTeleportation` is `portalTime++ >= transitionTime` — a **post**-increment, so a delay of 80 fires on the **81st** tick inside. `decayTick` subtracts **4** per tick outside, floored at zero, so one tick out costs four in.
- `NetherPortalBlock.getPortalTransitionTime` returns **0 for anything that is not a `Player`** (offset 49). For a player the rule is chosen by `abilities.invulnerable`, not the game mode.
- `WorldBorder.clampVec3ToBound`'s upper-edge epsilon is `(double)(float)1.0E-5` = **9.999999747378752E-6**, not `1.0E-5`; **y is not clamped at all**, only floored by `BlockPos.containing`. Vanilla's own answers on a 32-wide border: `(16.0, 64, 16.0) -> (15, 64, 15)`, `(-15.5, 64, -15.5) -> (-16, 64, -16)`.
- `getPortalDestination` scales **x and z only**: `clampToBounds(x * scale, y, z * scale)` with `scale = from.coordinateScale / to.coordinateScale`. `PortalForcer`'s search radii are 16 (nether) and 128 (overworld) — the same 8:1 ratio.
- `merchant_offers` (play cb **52**) transcribed and byte-identical to vanilla's own encoding over a 152-byte four-offer packet and a 6-byte empty one (`cargo test -p rustmc-server --test villager_trades::merchant_offers_matches_vanilla`, golden from `ci/merchant-golden.sh`). This **overturns the earlier refusal in this file** — the component-free case is encodable with the crate's existing item writer; only a stack carrying components is refused, and that refusal is an error rather than a silent drop.
- `MerchantOffer.writeToStream` is a hand-written lambda, not a composite, and **six of its ten fields are `writeInt`** — fixed 32-bit big-endian — where every other integer on this wire is a varint: `uses`, `maxUses`, `xp`, `specialPriceDiff` (signed), `demand` (signed). The packet's own `containerId` and `villagerXp` ARE varints, so one packet mixes both encodings. The golden uses 200 and 150 for those two because below 128 the two agree.
- **`Bootstrap.bootStrap()` leaves item DATA COMPONENTS unbound**: `new ItemStack(Items.EMERALD)` throws `NullPointerException: Components not bound yet` in `Holder.Reference.components`. `BuiltInRegistries.DATA_COMPONENT_INITIALIZERS.build(VanillaRegistries.createLookup()).forEach(PendingComponents::apply)` binds them. Second instance of the same class as the unbound block tag in `PortalGolden` — **a bare bootstrap is not a loaded server**, and any goldengen program touching items or tags hits one of these.
- `ItemStack.STREAM_CODEC` (non-optional) has **no zero sentinel** — it is `count, item, added, removed` — unlike `OPTIONAL_STREAM_CODEC`, which `crate::menu::write_item` implements. `merchant_offers` uses the non-optional one for the result.
- villager professions and POI types: **21 POI types and 15 professions** captured from the code registries (`ci/villager-golden.sh`), and the **65-entry join** between `VillagerProfession.tradeSetsByLevel` and the datapack's `trade_set/**` verified (`cargo test -p rustmc-server --test villager_trades::professions_and_poi_types`).
- That join is a **naming convention, not a lookup**: `tradeSetsByLevel` is an `Int2ObjectMap<ResourceKey<TradeSet>>` and nothing in the jar enforces that the key is `minecraft:<profession>/level_N`. 13 professions x 5 levels = 65; the datapack's other 3 sets are the wandering trader's, which is not a `VillagerProfession` at all.
- `PoiType.maxTickets` takes three values — **1** (job site), **32** (`minecraft:meeting`, the bell), **0** (marker: bee nest, beehive, lodestone, lightning rod, nether portal, test instance) — and `validRange` is 1 for all 21 except `meeting`, which is 6.
- a POI matches **block states**, not blocks: `minecraft:home` is 128 states over 16 bed blocks, but `minecraft:leatherworker` is only **8 states over 4 cauldron blocks**, so most cauldron states are not a job site and a matcher keyed on the block id is wrong there.
- `nitwit` and `none` are the two professions with no trade sets and no work sound, which is why the datapack has 14 profession directories and the registry has 15 entries.
- end portals have **no delay for anyone**: `Portal.getPortalTransitionTime` is a default method returning 0 and `EndPortalBlock` does not override it, so `players_nether_portal_default_delay` (80) reaches the nether portal only. `Portal.getLocalTransition` is likewise a default `NONE` that only `NetherPortalBlock` overrides, to `CONFUSION`.
- `ServerLevel.END_SPAWN_POINT` is **(100, 50, 0)** (`<clinit>` offsets 4-9). Entering the End goes there; leaving it uses the level's respawn data, and for a `ServerPlayer` `findRespawnPositionAndUseSpawnBlock(false, DO_NOTHING)` overrides that — neither is a constant, so `end_portal_target` names the branch and refuses to answer the player half.
- **the serde_json ULP defect is a guard now, and the sweep it demanded came back clean.** `crates/rustmc-data/tests/golden_float_precision.rs` walks every committed `golden/` fixture, parses every decimal literal through serde_json AND through Rust's own correctly-rounded parser, and refuses growth. Measured 2026-09-05: **61 files, 843 decimal literals, 5 files carrying 38 doubles serde_json cannot read back** (`legacy_lcg_derived` 12, `xoroshiro128pp_derived` 10, `xwr_derived` 9, `noise_vectors` 5, `world_border` 2; `data/vanilla/attributes.json` a further 7, excluded because it is generated per tree).
- **No reader in this workspace parses any of those 38.** The project had already converged on carrying the bits, independently, in three spellings: `golden_rng.rs:135` "Compare IEEE-754 bits, never the decimal repr", `golden_noise.rs:5` "Everything is compared as raw IEEE-754 bits", `attributes.rs:44` "the bits are what is read". So the earlier "26 as_f64 sites, any could be silently wrong" reading **overstated the live exposure**: the convention was already right everywhere it mattered, and the guard now keeps it that way for goldens added later.
- **a raw `rsync --delete` into a remote dir while a gate runs there voids the gate, and the failure reads exactly like a branch defect.** Measured 2026-09-05: `worldsys-t0c` came back `T0 RED — ci/entity-id-allocator-falsify.sh: an injected fault went unrefused`, on the arm `counter retyped to AtomicI64  rc=0 FAIL`. That falsifier `sed`s `crates/rustmc-server/src/entities.rs` in place and restores it; my own inner-loop rsync landed inside its window (t0c ran ~18:56–19:05:59, the rsync at ~19:00) and put the unpatched file back before the check read it, so the injection simply was not there. The same tree was **T0 GREEN** two commits earlier (`worldsys-t0b`, 18:54) and this branch touches nothing in `entities.rs`. `ci/gate-lock.sh` is the mtime mutex for exactly this and a hand-rolled rsync takes none of it — so the inner loop in `docs/AGENT-BRIEF.md` is safe only while no gate is running in the same `RUSTMC_REMOTE_DIR`.
- 2026-09-05 **`WorldBorder.tick()` reaches no listener, so the border sends nothing per tick and the whole shrink animation is client-side.** It is three instructions — `extent = extent.update()` — and every `BorderChangeListener` call site in the class is in a setter (`setCenter`, `setSize`, `lerpSizeBetween`, `setSafeZone`, `setDamagePerBlock`, `setWarningTime`, `setWarningBlocks`). The brief for this lane said the `set_border_*` broadcasts come from `tick`; they come from one `set_border_lerp_size` and the client interpolates. Ticking is still required server-side, because `getSize()` and a later joiner's `initialize_border` both read the extent — `crates/rustmc-server/src/status.rs`, the first step of the clock closure, matching `ServerLevel.tick` offsets 32..48.
- 2026-09-05 **`PlayerList$1` has seven listener methods and only five send anything: `onSetDamagePerBlock` and `onSetSafeZone` have empty bodies.** So `/worldborder damage amount` and `/worldborder damage buffer` change server state, answer the caller, and put **no packet on the wire at all**. A port that broadcast something there adds a packet vanilla does not send, on a channel `tools/oracle-runner` digests whole. `crates/rustmc-server/src/bordercmd.rs::tests::damage_sends_no_packet`.
- 2026-09-05 **Java's `%f` is not Rust's `{:.N}` and three `/worldborder` replies reach the difference.** Java's `Formatter` applies HALF_UP to `FloatingDecimal`'s shortest round-trip digit string; Rust applies half-to-EVEN to the exact binary value. `%.1f` of `0.15` is `0.2` and `0.1`; `%.2f` of `0.125` is `0.13` and `0.12`; `%.0f` of `2.5` is `3` and `2`. All three are typeable. `bordercmd::java_format_f` is the transcription and `golden/commands.json`'s ten `worldborder*` replies are vanilla's own bytes for values chosen to separate the two.
- 2026-09-05 **`Vec2Argument.vec2()` is `new Vec2Argument(true)` and that `true` adds half a block.** `WorldCoordinate.parseDouble` offsets 111..137 add `0.5` when the text held no `.`, the coordinate was not relative and `centerCorrect` is set — so `/worldborder center 10 20` centres the border at (10.5, 20.5) and `/worldborder center 10.0 20.0` at (10.0, 20.0). A relative coordinate with no digits returns `new WorldCoordinate(true, 0.0)` early and never picks it up. `Vec2Argument.parse` also has **no `^` dispatch at all** where `Vec3Argument.parse` opens with one, so `^1 ^1` is `ERROR_MIXED_TYPE` here and a local coordinate there.
- 2026-09-05 **`ServerScoreboard.onObjectiveAdded` broadcasts no packet**, so `/scoreboard objectives add` is invisible on the wire. Every scoreboard packet goes through `trackedObjectives`, a field on the server subclass and absent from `Scoreboard`: `setDisplayObjective` on an untracked objective calls `startTrackingObjective`, whose `getStartTrackingPackets` sends `set_objective` METHOD_ADD **then** one `set_display_objective` per slot showing it. So the first packet a client sees for an objective is the one `setdisplay` emits, and a removal is one packet or none depending on whether it was ever displayed.
- 2026-09-05 **`ObjectiveCriteria.byName` has three arms and only one needs a registry**, which is what makes `minecraft:objective_criteria` transcribable where `minecraft:block_state` was not. A hit in `CRITERIA_CACHE` — 11 custom names plus `killedByTeam.<colour>` and `teamkill.<colour>` for 16 colours, 43 in all — accepts; a name with **no `:`** and no cache hit returns `Optional.empty()` at offsets 22..36 and is a definite reject; only the `:` form reaches `BuiltInRegistries.STAT_TYPE`. The two per-colour format strings are recoverable only with `javap -v`: `makeConcatWithConstants` hides them from `javap -c`.
- 2026-09-05 **`ObjectiveCriteria.byName` THROWS an unchecked exception on a `:` name that is not a valid identifier.** `Identifier.bySeparator` calls `assertValidPath`, so `stone{Foo:1b}` raises `IdentifierException` — a `RuntimeException` that escapes `ObjectiveCriteriaArgument.parse` and reaches `Commands.executeCommand`'s catch-all as an internal error, not a syntax error. `ci/command-parse-golden.sh` marks those rows and `command_parse_vanilla.rs` requires this server to answer `Unsupported` on every one: claiming a verdict where vanilla gave none is a stronger error than having no verdict. 3 rows of 3,534.
- 2026-09-05 **`ci/command-parse-golden.sh` recorded a non-deterministic field and no parser had reached it before.** `String.valueOf(e.getType())` is an identity hash for any exception type with no `toString`, which `DynamicCommandExceptionType` is; the two runs the dumper compares disagreed on every row raised by one, the moment `minecraft:objective_criteria` and `minecraft:scoreboard_slot` landed. It records the type's class name now. The same commit found the corpus held no criterion or display-slot name at all, so both new parsers would have accepted nothing and been refused by the dumper's own "a parser that accepts nothing is indistinguishable from one that always fails" check.
- 2026-09-05 **`ObjectiveArgument.parse` is `readUnquotedString(); areturn`** — two instructions, no length check, no lookup, no rewind — so it accepts the empty string and joins `brigadier:string word`/`greedy` in `NEVER_REJECTS`. Every constraint on an objective name is in `ScoreboardCommand`'s executors, which is why `/scoreboard objectives remove nosuchthing` is a parse success followed by an executor refusal. Its neighbour `ScoreboardSlotArgument.parse` does **not** rewind on failure where `ObjectiveCriteriaArgument.parse` does, and the two sit in the same command.
- 2026-09-05 **The gameplay corpus could not reach any permission-level-2 command, and the fix was already half-built.** `Step::Command` is level 0 (`oracle`/`oracle_eye`, no `ops.json`, and op-ing them was considered and rejected because `sendPlayerPermissionLevel` changes join traffic for every check). `record::Console`'s header says there is no console on our side; that has not been true since `ci/t2-gameplay.sh` started passing `--console` on **both** record passes. `Step::Console` is the symmetric route, refused rather than skipped when no console is given, and `crate::console` now dispatches `worldborder` and `scoreboard` through the same tree and executors a typed command reaches — plus `Commands.trimOptionalPrefix`, without which a line typed with a slash runs on vanilla and not here.
- 2026-09-23 **`world-systems-2` was RED on T0 before any rebase: 941dfd27d adds gameplay checks 113, 114, 115 with no row in either ledger** — `ci/corpus-falsifiable.py --check` and `ci/corpus-grades.py --check` both refused 3 UNREGISTERED. Rows re-taken over the lane's own pair `~/rustmc-misc2/oracle-work/gameplay/{vanilla,rustmc}.json` (111 scenarios) with this tree's oracle-runner: all three tier 2, grades unresolved N=1 of 37. Its `Cargo.lock` also lacked the `serde_json` edges its own `Cargo.toml` declares for rustmc-data and rustmc-proto (`cargo metadata --offline` dirtied it).
- `minecraft:respawn` is clientbound play **82 (0x52)** (`reports/packets.json`), and `ClientboundRespawnPacket` is a `CommonPlayerSpawnInfo` plus one `dataToKeep` byte — the same 36 bytes the login packet already carries inline, verified as a slice of the 109-byte vanilla login capture (`crates/rustmc-server/src/play.rs::the_respawn_packet_is_the_spawn_info_and_one_byte`).
- The `minecraft:dimension_type` registry the configuration phase already sends holds **four** entries, so the varint `CommonPlayerSpawnInfo` carries is `the_end` = **2** and `the_nether` = **3**, not 1 and 2: `minecraft:overworld_caves` is numbered at index 1 though no level uses it (`data/vanilla/registries.bin`, `dimension_travel.rs::the_registry_indices_are_the_captures_own_order`).
- **The End has `has_skylight: true`** and only the nether has none; the End's darkness is `sky_light_factor: 0.0` + `ambient_light: 0.25` in the entry's `attributes`, not an absent sky-light section (`dimension_type/the_end.json`, and the registry capture's own NBT).
- The nether's `logical_height` is **128** against a stored `height` of 256 — the only dimension of the three where the two differ; a chunk packet still carries 16 sections.
- `CommonPlayerSpawnInfo.seaLevel` is `ServerLevel.getSeaLevel()` = `chunkSource.getGenerator().getSeaLevel()`, i.e. the **noise settings'** `sea_level` (63 / 32 / 0), which appears nowhere in the `dimension_type` registry entry — a server reading it from the dimension type would send 0 for all three.
- **There is no `DIM-1` and no `DIM1` in 26.2, and the overworld is not the world root.** `LevelStorageSource$LevelStorageAccess.getDimensionPath` is one call to `DimensionType.getStorageFolder(key, path)` = `key.identifier().resolveAgainst(path.resolve("dimensions"))`, so every dimension saves at `<world>/dimensions/<namespace>/<path>/` with no special case — `dimensions/minecraft/overworld`, `.../the_nether`, `.../the_end`.
- A served nether/End chunk **is** the dump's chunk: 196,608 blocks (chunk (0,0), seed 20260722, all three dimensions, whole column) compared through `World::chunk` -> encode -> decode against `worldgen_assembly::generate::generate_nether`/`_end`/`generate`'s own canvas — **0 differ** (`crates/rustmc-server/tests/served_dimensions.rs::a_served_chunk_is_the_dumps_chunk`, 58.0 s, Threadripper, rustmc-dims). So `docs/PARITY.md`'s nether and End figures are about the world the server serves.
- `ci/checks/test-targets.toml`'s `world-systems-wiring` bucket declared `join_border` as running in T0 and **no `ci/t0.sh` selector reached it** — a target under `tests/` is invisible to `--lib --bins`. T0 was red on its own manifest from the commit that added the bucket; the selector is now there (`ci/t0.sh`).
- `BlockPos.spiralAround`'s **first yielded position is the centre**, and the iterator's cursor starts one step along `second` with the first move being `directions[(-1+4)%4] = second.getOpposite()` which undoes it; a transcription starting the cursor at the centre is off by one leg for its whole length. Leg sizes are `leg/2 + 1` over leg values **0..=4r inclusive** (1, 9, 25 positions at r = 0, 1, 2), so the half-open `range(4r)` reading is short by exactly the last leg. Scored against the jar: `crates/rustmc-server/golden/spiral_around.json`, 60 positions over 4 walks, 0 differ.
- `PortalForcer.createPortal` keeps **two** candidates, not one: `best` (a frame that stands at lateral -1, 0 and +1) and `fallback` (only lateral 0). A fallback is recorded only while `best` is unset and used only if the whole spiral ended with no `best` — collapsing them into "closest that fits" gives a different portal wherever a tight site is nearer than a roomy one.
- `createPortal` returns `Optional.empty()` in exactly one place — `maxY - 9 < max(minY + 1, 70)` — so on any world 79+ blocks tall a portal is always built; a search that finds no site **carves** one (a 3x2x4 box of air over a 3x2 obsidian floor) rather than failing.
- The portal blocks go in at `setBlock(.., 18)` (`UPDATE_CLIENTS | UPDATE_SUPPRESS_DROPS`, **no** `UPDATE_NEIGHBORS`) and the frame at 3; and the frame is written before the opening.
- `PortalForcer.findClosestPortalPosition`'s comparator is `comparingDouble(distSqr).thenComparingInt(BlockPos::getY)` over `PoiManager.getInSquare(NETHER_PORTAL, pos, r, ANY)`, r = 16 nether-side / 128 overworld-side, filtered by `WorldBorder.isWithinBounds` then by `state.hasProperty(HORIZONTAL_AXIS)`. `Stream.min` keeps the first minimum, so the POI iteration order is load-bearing **only** for exact (distSqr, y) ties.
- **A player entering the End lands at (100.5, 49.0, 0.5) facing yaw 90 (WEST); anything else lands at y 50.** `EndPortalBlock.getPortalDestination` offset 142 subtracts `(0, 1, 0)` from `Vec3.atBottomCenterOf(END_SPAWN_POINT)` only when the entity `instanceof ServerPlayer`. The platform's origin is `containing(pos).below()` = (100, **49**, 0), so its obsidian layer is at y 48 and the player's feet at 49.
- `EndPlatformFeature.createEndPlatform`'s loop nesting is **z-major, then x, then y** — `move(III)` takes `(dx, dy, dz)` and the bytecode pushes local slots 5, 6, 4, so the argument list's order is not the loop order. 100 positions, 25 obsidian at `dy == -1` and 75 air; a position already holding the right block is skipped with no destroy and no set.
- Entering the End is a constant and leaving it is not: `getPortalDestination`'s in-the-End branch reads `ServerLevel.getRespawnData()` and, for a `ServerPlayer`, returns `findRespawnPositionAndUseSpawnBlock(false, DO_NOTHING)` outright — a different `TeleportTransition`, not the same one with a different position.
- `ServerPlayer.changeDimension` sends `ClientboundRespawnPacket(createCommonSpawnInfo(level), 3)` — `iconst_3` at offset 128, i.e. **`KEEP_ALL_DATA`**, on every dimension change.
- The dimension-change clientbound sequence is **not the join sequence rearranged**: `ServerPlayer.teleport(TeleportTransition)` sends `respawn` FIRST (offset 115) and `PlayerList.sendLevelInfo` — which is where the world border comes from — LAST (offset 314), after the teleport. At join `placeNewPlayer` calls `sendLevelInfo` before the game event. A wiring that reused the join order puts the border ahead of the respawn, and a client applies a border to the level it thinks it is in.
- Full order, by offset: `game_event NO_RESPAWN_BLOCK_AVAILABLE` (16, conditional), `respawn(.., 3)` (115), `change_difficulty` (135), `sendPlayerPermissionLevel` (172), `connection.teleport` (253), `resetPosition` (272), `player_abilities` (296), `sendLevelInfo` (314), `sendAllPlayerInfo` (321), `sendActivePlayerEffects` (327), `postTeleportTransition.onTransition` (334).
- **Leaving the End is absolute where entering it is relative.** `TeleportTransition.createDefault` and `.missingRespawnBlock` both pass `Set.of()` (no arguments, offset 39) as the relatives, so the destination, the delta, the yaw and the pitch are all absolute; `EndPortalBlock`'s entering branch passes `Relative.union(DELTA, Set.of(X_ROT))`, so an arriving player keeps its velocity and its pitch.
- `createDefault` and `missingRespawnBlock` are the same method byte for byte apart from `iconst_0` vs `iconst_1` at offset 37. That flag is read once — by `ServerPlayer.teleport` at offset 16 — to send `game_event NO_RESPAWN_BLOCK_AVAILABLE` ahead of the respawn packet. The destination is identical either way.
- `MinecraftServer.findRespawnDimension()` reads the **overworld level data's** respawn dimension (`getWorldData().overworldData().getRespawnData().dimension()`), not the player's and not the level being left, and falls back to `overworld()` when that level does not exist.
- `PoiManager.getInSquare`'s final filter is **Chebyshev in x and z with no y bound** (`abs(dx) <= r && abs(dz) <= r`), so a portal 186 blocks above the arrival point is a candidate; y enters only as `thenComparingInt(BlockPos::getY)`, ascending, so the **lower** of two equidistant portals wins.
- `findClosestPortalPosition`'s radius argument is `toNether`, and the **small** radius (16) is the one used when arriving IN the nether, 128 coming back — the same 8:1 as the coordinate scale, so a 128-block overworld square maps onto a 16-block nether one.
- Its second filter is `state.hasProperty(HORIZONTAL_AXIS)`, **not** `is(NETHER_PORTAL)` — a weaker test than block identity, and the re-check that catches a POI record whose block was broken before the section was revalidated.
- `Tiles::build` reads `data/vanilla/block_props.json` and `registries.bin` by **relative** path, so the live chunk source (`Tiles::prepare_in` + `Tiles::chunk`, the pair `status.rs`'s `chunk_at` calls) had never been reachable from any test, whose cwd is the crate directory. `served_dimensions.rs::the_tile_grid_serves_all_three_dimensions` is the first to reach it, by moving cwd to the workspace root — safe in that binary because every other test there resolves fixtures through `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`.
- **A nether portal cannot be lit in the End.** `BaseFireBlock.isPortal` opens with `inPortalDimension(level)` = `dimension() == OVERWORLD || dimension() == NETHER`, and nothing else; flint and steel in the End places fire whatever the frame looks like.
- Lighting a portal by clicking the **top** of a block consumes a **level-RNG draw**: `isPortal`'s axis is `direction.getCounterClockWise().getAxis()` for a horizontal face but `Direction.Plane.HORIZONTAL.getRandomAxis(level.getRandom())` for up/down, so it can pick the axis the frame is not on and place ordinary fire instead. A transcription deriving the axis from the frame lights portals vanilla refuses and consumes one draw fewer.
- `isPortal`'s obsidian test is a **neighbour scan over `Direction.values()`** (Y, then Z, then X) stopping at the first obsidian, not a frame test — a cheap pre-filter ahead of `findEmptyPortalShape`.
- **The corpus leaks the world border between scenarios, and it cost 73 of 111 scenarios on `world-systems-2` and every branch stacked on it.** `ci/checks/gameplay/113-a-console-worldborder-set-reaches-a-client.toml` sends `worldborder set 137` from the console at scenario index 14 and **nothing in the corpus ever restores it** — 113 and 114 are the only two files that mention `worldborder`. Scenario anchors march 32 blocks apart in −x, so from index 17 (`slab-half-follows-the-cursor`, anchor x −576) onward every scenario is anchored outside a 137-wide border centred on the origin, and vanilla refuses the placement. Measured on `oracle-work/gameplay/vanilla.json`, my run and `~/rustmc-misc2`'s (the base's own) being identical at **66 `build.tooHigh` scenarios and 15 errors**, against **0 and 0** in `~/rustmc-stack` and **0 and 1** in `~/rustmc-combat`, neither of which carries those three scenarios.
- The partition is exact and is what identifies the border as the cause rather than the anchor distance: of the 21 scenarios after index 17 that **survive**, **0** emit a non-air `block_update`; of the 73 that fail, **33** do, and the rest are container clicks that depend on a chest or table the failed placement never put there. A world border refuses placing and permits digging, and the surviving list is digs, drops, moves and chat.
- **A sweep over that corpus records the breakage as the ledger's new truth.** `ci/corpus-falsifiable.py --sweep` on it rewrote `ci/checks/gameplay-falsifiable.toml` with **15 checks demoted from `tier = 2` to `tier = 0`** — every one of them a working container or crafting check whose scenario merely *errored* — plus `dead_fields` 4 -> 0 and a moved field universe. Discarded rather than committed: the tier-2 floor and the dead-field ceiling are ratcheted, so committing it would have silently retired 15 checks and widened two guards.

* 2026-09-05 **`ci/corpus-falsifiable.py`'s own selftest carried an injection that stops injecting.** The case "a field that stopped being dead refused" was `write_ledger(..., list(DEAD_FIELDS[:-1]), ...)`; `()[:-1]` is `()`, so the moment `DEAD_FIELDS` emptied the case wrote the pinned set unchanged and asserted a refusal nothing could raise — green, and testing nothing. Not repaired here: the repair reds `main`'s own ledger and belongs to the lane that owns the gate.

* 2026-09-05 **The `DEAD_FIELDS[:-1]` inert-injection defect was found independently by two lanes in one evening, in two files, and each lane found only its own instance.** `mob-in-world` found it in `ci/corpus-falsifiable.py`'s selftest; `origin/packets` (5ea91cfff) found it one layer out in `ci/corpus-machinery-falsify.py`'s case 1c. That commit is cherry-picked onto `mob-in-world`, so the two land as one patch at main. The generalisation worth carrying: **a mutator written as a slice, a `replace`, or a delete of a named element goes inert the moment the population it edits empties or is renamed** — and the third instance was in the repair itself, whose anchored `^DEAD_FIELDS = \(` rewriter got 0 matches against `DEAD_FIELDS: tuple = ()` and, at its `re.sub` call site, would have run every arm against the live ceiling and passed.

Any goldengen that needs a stack must read the structure it is probing directly — `ci/fuel-values-golden.sh` reads `FuelValues`' private `Object2IntSortedMap` by reflection for that reason. `ItemStack.EMPTY` is still usable.
* 2026-09-05 **`VanillaRegistries.createLookup()` cannot back a dumper that touches a tag.** Its item lookup is still under construction, so the `HolderSet` for `ItemTags.LOGS` throws `Tag ... can't be dereferenced during construction` the moment `FuelValues.Builder.add(TagKey, int)` iterates it — before any datapack tag is consulted and regardless of what has been bound. `RegistryAccess.fromRegistryOfRegistries(BuiltInRegistries.REGISTRY)` after a `prepareTagReload(...).apply()` is the provider that works. `tools/goldengen/vanilla/FuelValuesGolden.java`.
* 2026-09-05 **`VanillaRegistries.createLookup()`'s registry order is not reproducible between JVM runs.** Three runs of `JukeboxSongGolden` over `JUKEBOX_SONG` gave three different orders (`tears, pigstep, ward, 11, …` / `11, ward, pigstep, tears, …` / `tears, chirp, mellohi, relic, …`), so `Registry.getId` taken through that path is not the wrong number, it is a fresh random one per regeneration. `JukeboxSongPlayer.play` sends exactly that index in `level_event(1010, pos, id)`, so **no jukebox song id extracted this way is quotable**; `data/vanilla/jukebox_songs.json` therefore emits rows sorted by id, carries the alphabetical rank as an explicitly unverified stand-in, and flags itself `registryIdIsUnverified`. Any goldengen that emits a dynamic-registry index has the same hole. `ci/jukebox-song-golden.sh`.
* 2026-09-05 `ItemTags.BOOKSHELF_BOOKS` has **five** members in 26.2, not the four a hand list produces: it also holds `minecraft:knowledge_book`. A `ChiseledBookShelfBlockEntity.acceptsItemType` built on the obvious four would refuse a knowledge book through `setItem`'s branch that reports nothing and changes nothing. The hand list was written first here and was caught only by opening the datapack. `crates/rustmc-server/src/bookshelf.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `AbstractFurnaceBlockEntity.serverTick` samples `isLit` **after** the `litTimeRemaining` decrement, so a furnace burning its last lit tick with no fuel present takes the **decay** branch at offset 344 rather than the zeroing branch at 320: its `cookingTimer` goes 10 -> 8, not 10 -> 0. A unit test asserting 0 there was wrong and the bytecode corrected it. The decay is minus **two** a tick, clamped into `[0, cookingTotalTime]`. `crates/rustmc-server/src/cooking.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 The completion tests of the two cookers one class apart are different comparisons: `AbstractFurnaceBlockEntity` uses `if_icmpne` (strict equality, and `cookingTotalTime` is rewritten on every completion so an overshoot never completes again) while `CampfireBlockEntity.cookTick` uses `if_icmplt` (`>=`). A campfire slot whose recipe has gone away drops the **raw** food from `orElse(theRawStack)`; a furnace in the same state does nothing at all.
* 2026-09-05 **The behaviour half of the block entities cannot be wired to the world until `origin/block-entities-2` lands, and the reason is one struct.** A furnace ticker needs `litTimeRemaining` and `litTotalTime`, a hopper a `cooldownTime` and a `tickedGameTime`, a campfire two four-int arrays; `main`'s `crate::blockentity::BlockEntity` carries `kind`, `items` and `sign` and nothing else, and that branch is rewriting it. So thirteen types are transcribed as pure functions over caller-owned state and **the join is one commit after the merge** — an `Option<Box<Behaviour>>` beside `sign`, plus one pass in `status.rs`'s `clock.spawn` closure at `ServerLevel.tickBlockEntities`' position, after the scheduled-tick drain and before the broadcast. `docs/cold-start/block-entity-behaviour.md`.
* 2026-09-05 `PotionBrewing.mix` cannot be ported to this server at all: a potion in 26.2 is `minecraft:potion` plus a `POTION_CONTENTS` data component and `crate::item::ItemStack` is an id and a count. An id-keyed mix table would collapse every potion onto one row and turn any ingredient into any other potion — a different mechanic, not an approximation. `crate::brewing` transcribes the fuel rule, the 400-tick timer and both cancel paths, and `NoBrewing` refuses the three `PotionBrewing` questions so a stand fuels, shows its bottles and never brews.
* 2026-09-05 **`ci/gen-gameplay-tables.sh` needs a second pass on a tree that is generating `loot.json` for the first time, and until it gets one `ci/t2-gameplay.sh` exits 4 and records nothing.** `ci/loot-golden.sh` writes four tables in one JVM run and the derived loop calls it once per table, so each later invocation rewrites the tables the earlier ones already stamped; the first pass ends with `loot.json: changed since it was stamped` and the gate refuses the whole corpus rather than measuring it. A second `./ci/gen-gameplay-tables.sh` settles it and `--check` then says "all current". Measured in `~/rustmc-beh` on 2026-09-05: pass 1 left 3 stale, pass 2 left 0. Cost when unnoticed: a whole detached corpus chain that produced no recordings (`~/.rustmc-detached/beh-gate.log`).
* 2026-09-05 **`main`'s `ci/corpus-falsifiable.py --sweep` files a `tier = 0` row for a scenario the recording does not contain, and `--check` then refuses it — so a new scenario cannot be registered honestly on `main` at all.** Measured by sweeping `795-a-bell-rings-when-its-side-is-clicked` against `~/rustmc-corpus`'s pair: the 108 existing scenarios reproduced the committed ledger exactly (108/108 tier 2, floor 108, same dead/undetermined/unaskable sets), and the bell got **both** `unswept = ["a-bell-rings-when-its-side-is-clicked"]` **and** a `tier = 0` row, which `--check`'s row branch refuses before the `unswept` branch is reached. `origin/corpus-instruments` (d055a470) is the fix — its sweep writes no row rather than filing tier 0 — so the shape that survives both is *name in `unswept`, no row*. Anyone registering a new scenario before that lands has to take a real recording or refuse the scenario by name. **Resolved for this branch by taking the recording**: over its own corpus run 795 swept to tier 2, 109/109, 0 unswept.
* 2026-09-05 **`ci/t2-gameplay.sh` exits 1 for a scenario that has no row in `ci/checks/gameplay-baseline.toml`, and that exit is not a divergence.** It prints `REGRESSED <name>: not in the baseline — a new check must record what it currently does` beside the scenario's actual verdict, which for `795-a-bell-rings-when-its-side-is-clicked` was `match` four lines earlier in the same log. A detached chain that refused the scenario on `T2-EXIT != 0` therefore deleted a check that matched vanilla and graded tier 2. **A new gameplay scenario needs its baseline row in the same commit that adds it**; a chain that decides on the exit status alone cannot tell "the corpus went backwards" from "this check is new".
* 2026-09-05 **`ServerLevel.tick` calls `tickBlockEntities()` LAST, not before the broadcast, and the note this lane was briefed with said the opposite.** The call order out of the bytecode is `LevelTicks.tick` (fluid), `LevelTicks.tick` (block), `Raids.tick`, `ServerChunkCache.tick`, `runBlockEvents`, `EnderDragonFight.tick`, `EntityTickList.forEach`, `tickBlockEntities`. The block broadcast lives inside `ServerChunkCache.tick`, so a block state a ticker rewrites is broadcast on the **next** tick: a furnace that lights at tick N sends its `lit` update at N+1. A pass placed before `changed.flush` would send it at N, and no instrument would have said which was right until a scenario counted ticks. `crate::behaviour::tick_block_entities`, `crate::status`.
* 2026-09-05 **`minecraft:piston` is ticked on the server and this server does not tick it, and a sweep of `net.minecraft.world.level.block` alone cannot see that.** `MovingPistonBlock` is in `net.minecraft.world.level.block.piston`; its `getTicker` has no `isClientSide` test at all and hands `PistonMovingBlockEntity.tick` to both sides. That method is what finishes a push — it advances `progress`, writes the moved state at the destination and removes the moving block entity — so `crate::containers::run_piston_event` starts a push this server never ends. Found by `ci/block-entity-golden.sh`'s new `blockTicker` column on its first run, against a hand table that said `Ticker::None`.
* 2026-09-05 **`AbstractFurnaceBlockEntity` declares no `getUpdatePacket`, so a furnace's cooking progress reaches no client as `block_entity_data` and a scenario waiting for one would wait forever.** 19 of the 49 types declare one and the furnace family is not among them; the four counters travel only as the menu's data slots. `CampfireBlockEntity` **is** among the nineteen, and `placeFood`/`cookTick` end in `markUpdated` — `setChanged()` plus `sendBlockUpdated(pos, state, state, 3)` with the same state on both sides — which is why a campfire is the one block entity in this branch with a cheap gameplay observable. `ci/checks/gameplay/796`, `797`.
* 2026-09-05 **Twelve of the forty-nine block-entity types are ticked by this server, fifteen more are ticked by vanilla and not by us, and twenty-two are ticked by nobody.** Collapsing the middle group into "we do not tick it" is how a gap stops being visible, so `crate::behaviour::Ticker` is three-valued and every `Unported` row names the vanilla method it is not running: `SignBlockEntity.tick`, `ShulkerBoxBlockEntity.tick`, `BeehiveBlockEntity.serverTick`, `CreakingHeartBlockEntity.serverTick`, `SculkCatalystBlockEntity.serverTick`, `VibrationSystem.Ticker.tick` (three sculk types), `TheEndGatewayBlockEntity.portalTick`, `PistonMovingBlockEntity.tick`, `DaylightDetectorBlock.tickEntity`, and the captured-lambda tickers of `TrialSpawnerBlockEntity`, `VaultBlockEntity` and `PotentSulfurBlockEntity`.
* 2026-09-05 **A declared-method walk cannot tell which SIDE a block-entity ticker is handed to, and four types prove it.** `ChestBlockEntity`, `TrappedChestBlockEntity` and `EnderChestBlockEntity` declare `lidAnimateTick` and `SkullBlockEntity` declares `animation`; all four blocks override `getTicker` and all four null the **server** arm (`ChestBlock` offset 4 `ifeq 23`, `EnderChestBlock` `ifeq 22`, `AbstractSkullBlock` `ifeq 72`). So `ci/block-entity-golden.sh`'s `tickers` column is a necessary and not a sufficient test, the residue lives in a fixed five-name exception list, and a *fifth* type acquiring a client-only ticker reds the check rather than joining them quietly.
* 2026-09-05 **`cargo fmt` and clippy cannot be run on the laptop at all — there is no `cargo` on it** — so a lane's formatting round trip is `ci/t0-remote.sh --sync`, `cargo fmt` on the box, and `rsync --existing --include='*.rs'` back. Worth writing down because `docs/AGENT-BRIEF.md` lists `cargo fmt` under "local is fine for", and it is not.
* 2026-09-05 **A hopper transfer produces nothing on the wire on `main`, so the scenario this lane was briefed to close is refused rather than unwritten.** `container_set_slot` comes from `AbstractContainerMenu.broadcastChanges`, which vanilla runs every tick from `ServerPlayer.doTick`; here `broadcast_changes` is called only from a click handler, and the open menu lives in `Session.menu.open` on the connection thread's stack where the clock thread cannot reach it. The join point is exactly that: the open `Menu` has to move onto `crate::players::Connection` behind a lock before the tick pass can diff it. Menus are `origin/stack-gameplay`'s area and this branch does not move them.

All fourteen are now rows in one table and there is one filter; `ci/ambient-falsify.py` R1 refuses any `.filter`/`.retain`/`.take_while` that names a kind outside it.
* 2026-09-06 `ci/vanilla-reach-census.py`'s block-entity ceiling (19 of 49 types) declared it was spending "`is_housekeeping` DROPS `LEVEL_CHUNK_WITH_LIGHT`". That was never the load-bearing half: the chunk payload carries every block entity with no `getUpdatePacket` filter, so what keeps a type illegible is that **no decoder arm reads one out of the payload**. Under the old guard, recording the packet (ceiling moves by nothing) would have been refused and writing its arm (ceiling moves by 29 of 49, the whole registry) would have passed — exactly backwards. `ceiling_premise_housekeeping` now takes the undecoded set as well and is fired a fourth way.
* 2026-09-06 **`ci/run-detached.sh` cannot be stopped by killing the pid it hands you, and every waiter then reports the run as finished while it keeps going.** The launcher is `setsid nohup bash -c '... echo $$ > pidf; bash "$body" ...'`, so the pid in the pidfile is the WRAPPER and `bash "$body"` is its child. A `kill <pid>` takes the wrapper, orphans the payload to systemd, and leaves the pidfile holding a dead pid — after which `--status`, the printed waiter (`kill -0 $(cat pidf)`), and the "already running" refusal at line 164 all agree the run is over. Measured here: killing `rec2` left `rec2.body.sh` running, so relaunching the same sequence as `rec2b` produced **two `ci/ambient-census.sh` runs in one remote directory**, racing for port 25894 and for `oracle-work/gameplay/ambient-census` — the second `rm -rf "$WORK"` deleted the first's world directory and the first's recorder died with `status failed twice: Connection refused`. **Until it is fixed, stop a detached run with `kill -TERM -<pid>` (the process GROUP, which `setsid` made) and check `pgrep -f '<name>.body.sh'` before relaunching.** NOT REPAIRED HERE: `ci/run-detached.sh` is on main and being changed by another lane (see the 2026-09-05 entry above); the fix is a `trap "kill -TERM -$$" TERM INT` in the wrapper plus a `pgrep -f "$NAME.body.sh"` arm on the already-running refusal, and it needs whoever owns that file.

* 2026-09-05 `minecraft:buried_treasures` is the only structure set vanilla places in the seed-20260722 r7 harvest that this server does not, and its whole cost there is **one block**: `minecraft:chest[facing=east,type=single,waterlogged=false]` at (1177,53,745), where we serve `minecraft:sand`.
* 2026-09-05 **Most of the residual inside a structure box is not the structure.** Attributed by `vanilla -> ours` block name over the twelve boxes: `minecraft:sulfur` accounts for 1,519 of the 5,556 — 27.3% — across `trial_chambers (53,74)` (1,057), `mineshaft (70,62)` (351) and `mineshaft (67,61)` (111), and it is a 26.2 decoration feature this port does not place, not structure code. * 2026-09-05 The parked village hay pile is **30 blocks** at `village_savanna (81,77)`: `minecraft:hay_block -> minecraft:air`, the third-largest class in that box after `stone -> coal_ore` (77) and `acacia_leaves -> air` (57). * 2026-09-05 **A structure start recorded in a PROTO column is a structure the harvest knows about and has no blocks for**, and it reads as "vanilla does not place this here" to any reader that filters columns by status before looking for starts. * 2026-09-30 `shard-equivalence`: the 2026-09-24 RED shard proof has four harness causes, read off the kept recordings (`~/rustmc-cpar-a/oracle-work/gameplay` serial vs `~/rustmc-cpar-b/oracle-work/gameplay` 4 shards, `oracle-runner explain --strict`, sayer-desktop): (1) inventory leaks between scenarios — `607` saw an earlier scenario's stone in slot 44 serially and not at a shard start, both servers, declared diverge; (2) held item leaks — `530` and `788` `update_attributes` digest differs, both servers; (3) `container_set_data` (0x13) was undecoded, so the per-connection menu counter sat in `rest` — `470` strict-diverge on both; (4) waypoint (0x8A) was undecoded and carries absolute coordinates, which a shard's restarted workspace index changes — `540` strict-diverge on rustmc. Fixed in `f3e1c6f8a`, `fec6e199d`, `cea35d077`.
* 2026-09-30 `shard-equivalence`: the proof's serial control is N=2, so a vanilla scenario that is random per run (`container-click-throw-*` item motion, `torch-falls-with-its-support` drop position in the 2026-09-24 recordings) is charged to sharding whenever the two serial runs happen to agree. Only 5 names are declared in `gameplay-unstable.toml`/`gameplay-floor.toml`; that list, not the N=2 control, is what bounds vanilla's own floor for this proof.
* 2026-09-30 `shard-equivalence`: the inventory reset (`f3e1c6f8a`) does NOT explain train-b's two serial-only regressions (`~/rustmc-train-b/oracle-work/gameplay`, 179 scenarios, `oracle-runner explain`). `083` holds its carrot in setup either way; vanilla's window holds 30 observations of TWO pigs, where 083 spawns one (vanilla entity 1's `rotate_head` digest `af75c84c86020609` is ours, entity 0 is a second pig walking in, `move_entity` d up to 0.8 block per packet) — a mob left by an earlier scenario (080/081/082 all spawn pigs 32-96 blocks east), tempted by the carrot. That is leftover WORLD state, which no player reset reaches; it needs entity cleanup between scenarios, and a sharded run changes which pigs exist. `459` is vanilla-nondeterministic: its two block updates straddle a chunk border ([1,0,0] vs [1,0,-1]) and `ServerChunkCache.chunkHoldersToBroadcast` is a `ReferenceOpenHashSet` (javap, constructor offset 48), iterated by identity hash, so their order is per-JVM; "matches alone" is a coin that landed.
* 2026-09-30 `shard-equivalence`: `ci/handoff.sh` released the corpus lock between stage 6 and stage 7 (two remote calls, each harness acquiring for itself), so a queued run took the box mid-gate (8.2 of 146.8 min on train-a's gate, gate-pipeline lane). Stages 6+7 now run under one `ci/corpus-lock.sh hold`; `case_hold_spans_harnesses` serves `A B R` with the queued run last, and the injection restoring per-harness acquisition is caught.

* 2026-10-01 `lock-pool-lanes`: on main a merge gate took ONE corpus-lock pool slot like any run, and the pool size is read per process, so a gate started with the default (1) waited only on slot 0 while lanes started with a larger pool recorded beside it in slots 1+. A gate now drains slots 0..7 (`_cl_gate_take_all`); `case_pool_gate_exclusive` shows 3 lanes overlapping and a default-pool gate overlapping none of them, and the one-slot gate is caught (`ci/corpus-lock-falsify.sh` 57 passed, 0 injections missed, sayer-desktop).
* 2026-10-01 `shard-equivalence`: with a third serial control the isolated shard proof is down to ONE effect at 8 shards (full-S8b and full-S8) and at 4: `084-a-pig-tempted-from-outside-its-pit-turns-its-head-and-stays-put`, in the first shard. Our pig plays an ambient `sound` in its window in 3/3 serial runs and 0/3 sharded runs (rustmc strict; vanilla does not move) — a per-tick-random sound tracking wall-clock ticks, i.e. load. Still a verdict a sharded gate reads differently, so RED; default shards stays 1. Lock hold: serial ~5.0 ks, 8 shards 499-521 s (`~/grit/shard-eq-results/fix1`, `ci/checks/corpus-shard-equivalence.toml`).
* 2026-10-01 `shard-equivalence` CORRECTION to the 084 line above: the ambient pig `sound` in 084's window is random on BOTH sides, not "3/3 serial vs 0/3 sharded": over the ten full recordings in `~/grit/shard-eq-results/{base,fix1}` it is in vanilla's window 3 times and ours 2 (fix1 serial C has none on our side). It flips the strict verdict whichever side rolls it, and the sharded runs happened to roll it on neither. It cannot be windowed out: `Mob.baseTick` (javap 24..55) plays it when `random.nextInt(1000) < ambientSoundTime++`, the field is never initialised (0 at spawn), and `resetAmbientSoundTime` sets it to `-getAmbientSoundInterval()` = -80 only AFTER a sound — so every tick from the second after spawn until the first sound has a non-zero chance, and a ~40-tick window ~50 ticks after spawn holds the first sound about 30% of the time in vanilla itself. No rig-only fix exists; a silent pig (entity_data `Silent`) would, if rustmc honoured it.
* 2026-10-01 `shard-equivalence`: the shard proof is EQUIVALENT at 8 shards once 084 is deterministic — its pig spawns `Silent` (spawn-egg `minecraft:entity_data`, now honoured by this server, a3047fa1a), and three serial runs of 010..084 show no ambient sound on either side. * 2026-10-01 `be-furnace-brewing`: on main (d48904cb5) a furnace never smelted. Fixed by `BlockEntity::set_item_from_menu`; unit test `raw_iron_into_the_ingredient_slot_sends_a_cook_total_of_200`.
* 2026-10-01 `be-furnace-brewing`: on main the brewing stand was `Ticker::Ported` with a behaviour and no arm — `tick_one` fell to `_ => {}` (`crates/rustmc-server/src/behaviour.rs:476`), so it never consumed fuel, set `has_bottle_*` or brewed. Wired with `brewing::VanillaBrewing`.
* 2026-10-01 `be-furnace-brewing`: `crate::brewing`'s "no data components, so refuse the mix table" over-refused. A component-less stack has the item's default `PotionContents.EMPTY`, and `PotionBrewing.hasPotionMix`/`mix` return early on an empty `potion()` (offsets 0-25 / 26-34) while `isIngredient`/`hasContainerMix` test items only — so brewing is exact for every stack the server can hold (e.g. potion + gunpowder brews 400 ticks and returns the bottle unchanged).
* 2026-10-01 `be-furnace-brewing`: on main nothing carried a block entity's own writes to an open menu: there was no per-tick `broadcastChanges`, and data slots were sent once as literal zeros (`crates/rustmc-server/src/world_send.rs:1434`). Fixed by `click::sweep_container` + `world_send::sweep_open_menus`.
* 2026-10-01 `be-furnace-brewing`: on main a furnace and brewing stand saved the constructor's zeros for their counters (`crates/rustmc-server/src/blockentity.rs:1845` writes `extras`, which the ticker never updates), so a retired tile reloaded cold; and `consume_fuel` deleted a lava bucket instead of leaving `BUCKET` (`crates/rustmc-server/src/cooking.rs:258`). * 2026-10-01 `loot`: `minecraft:container_loot` has no stream codec (`DataComponents.lambda$static$80` is `persistent(SeededContainerLoot.CODEC)` alone), so no client can put a loot table on a chest through it; `minecraft:block_entity_data` (`lambda$static$60`, `networkSynchronized(TypedEntityData.streamCodec(...))`) is the route, loaded by `BlockItem.updateCustomBlockEntityTag` at `BlockItem.place` offset 143. The serverbound patch is `DataComponentPatch.DELIMITED_STREAM_CODEC`, so every added component is length-prefixed and an unknown one can be skipped.
* 2026-10-01 `loot`: `origin/entity-ports` (and `origin/ranged`, same commits 999194dee/8a267c798/e07e94234) adds `crate::randomsequences` but nothing in `crates/rustmc-server/src` calls `randomsequences::init` — only `tests/mob_combat.rs:118` (`init(0)`) — so on a live server `drops::death_loot` answers "the random-sequence store has no world seed" and every death drop refuses. This branch's `--seed` (main.rs) is the missing wiring; the two stores compute the same `RandomSequence.createSequence`, and this branch's is gated against 12 vanilla sequence vectors.
* 2026-10-01 `loot`: block drops draw from a random sequence too — `LootTable.getRandomItems(LootParams)` builds `LootContext.Builder(params).create(randomSequence)` with no seed, and `BlockBehaviour.getDrops` calls exactly that — so `origin/mining`'s `dig.rs` `drop_resources` (a fresh `unique_random()` seed per break, documented there as a deviation) can now take `rustmc_loot::context_random(table, 0, &mut lootroll::sequences())` for `minecraft:blocks/<id>`.

* 2026-10-01 `item-components`: `tools/oracle-runner/src/trace.rs:224` multiplies by `0x1000_0000_01b3` (2^44 + 0x1b3), not FNV-1a's 64-bit prime `0x100000001b3` (2^40 + 0x1b3). Every `rest` digest is therefore not FNV-1a, and an external FNV-1a reimplementation reproduces only its low 44 bits (885's tail: `760f04baff85d6c0` vs recorded `bf076fbaff85d6c0`). Harmless for comparison; a trap for anyone decoding a digest.

* 2026-09-05 **`handleMovePlayer`'s after-move vertical residual is dropped unconditionally.** Offsets 720-740 are `dcmpl/ifgt` then `dcmpg/ifge` over `-0.5` and `0.5`, which is `if (dy > -0.5 || dy < 0.5) dy = 0.0` — a tautology for every finite double — so the vertical component NEVER contributes to the `0.0625` "moved wrongly" distance. A port writing the obvious `&&` rubber-bands a player whose vertical residual exceeds half a block, and vanilla does not. `crates/rustmc-server/src/move_player.rs::after_move`.
* 2026-09-05 **A move-packet flood is clamped to ONE packet's budget, not five.** `handleMovePlayer` offsets 419-449: `if (i > 5) { LOGGER.debug("sending move packets too frequently"); i = 1; }`. Reading that branch as a clamp to 5, or as log-only, gives a flooding client five or `i` times vanilla's `100.0f * i` allowance.
* 2026-09-05 **`firstGood` is reset at the top of every tick, not only at a teleport.** `ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.tickPlayer()` offset **0** is `resetPosition()`, before `doTick` at 50; `tick()` reaches it at offset 38. `handleMovePlayer` read alone says the opposite and this branch shipped that wrong claim for three commits. With `firstGood` pinned at the join a player is rubber-banded eleven blocks from spawn and `530-walking-away-drops-a-standing-player-from-view` walks 85.
* 2026-09-05 **The server-side player bounding box does not depend on the pose in 26.2.** `LivingEntity.getDimensions(Pose)` branches on `SLEEPING` only (offsets 0-25) and `getDefaultDimensions(Pose)` **discards its argument** — `getType().getDimensions().scale(getAgeScale())`; `EntityDimensions` is a record of width/height/eyeHeight/attachments/fixed with no pose component; `EntityTypes.<clinit>` offsets 7318-7329 register the player `sized(0.6f, 1.8f).eyeHeight(1.62f)`. So `canPlayerFitWithinBlocksAndEntitiesWhen(CROUCHING)` tests the same box as `STANDING` and there is no server-side "sneak under a slab" — the shorter hitbox is the client's. A pose-to-dimensions table carried from an older version gives a sneaking player a box vanilla never had.
* 2026-09-05 **`ServerboundPlayerCommandPacket.Action` has seven values in 26.2 and none of them is a shift key** — `STOP_SLEEPING, START_SPRINTING, STOP_SPRINTING, START_RIDING_JUMP, STOP_RIDING_JUMP, OPEN_INVENTORY, START_FALL_FLYING`. Sneak arrives on `ServerboundPlayerInputPacket` and is `setShiftKeyDown(input.shift())`. Sprint is the only movement state `player_command` still carries.
* 2026-09-05 **`Entity.checkFallDamage` narrows the delta through `float`**: `fallDistance -= (double)(float)dy`, `d2f; f2d` at offsets 19-20, on a `double` field. A hundred 0.1-block steps differ from the unnarrowed sum by more than 1e-9 and `calculateFallDamage` ends in `Mth.floor`, so the difference is a whole damage point at the boundaries. `calculateFallPower` also adds `1.0E-6` **before** subtracting `SAFE_FALL_DISTANCE`, which is what puts a fall of exactly the safe distance on the safe side.
* 2026-09-05 **No scenario in the gameplay corpus could make either server refuse a move.** `Session::hop_to` is capped at four blocks a packet precisely so that a walk is never rubber-banded (its own header records that sixteen made "the whole of a walk past about 16 blocks fiction"), so the refusal third of `handleMovePlayer` — the speed check, the correction teleport, `awaitingPositionFromClient`, the teleport counter — had no instrument pointed at it. `Step::Lurch` sends one `MOVE_PLAYER_POS` at any distance and then reads the socket, which a walk deliberately never does.
* 2026-09-05 **The corpus can only ever reach the SPEED refusal, never the geometry one.** `shouldCheckPlayerMovement` is `isSingleplayerOwner`, `isChangingDimension` and two game rules, and `isCreative` is not among them; the "moved wrongly" branch at offsets 804-821 IS skipped for a creative player and `ci/t2-gameplay.sh` joins in creative. A refusal scenario written against `0.0625` would record two servers agreeing that nothing happened and score `match` for ever.
* 2026-09-05 **`containsInvalidValues` accepts an infinite position and refuses an infinite rotation** — the three doubles go through `Double.isNaN` and the two floats through `Floats.isFinite` (offsets 0-35). A port testing all five the same way disconnects a client vanilla keeps, or keeps one vanilla drops. The horizontal clamp is 3.0E7 and the vertical 2.0E7, and `Mth.clamp` is `d < min ? min : Math.min(d, max)` with a `dcmpg`, not Rust's `f64::clamp`.
* 2026-09-05 **A sneaking player is silent but its step cadence keeps running.** `Player.getMovementEmission()` returns NONE for `flying || (onGround && isDiscrete())` and `Entity.isDiscrete()` is exactly `isShiftKeyDown()` — one instruction, no pose. `applyMovementEmissionAndPlaySound` accumulates `moveDist` at offsets 52-70 **before** the emission is read, and `vibrationAndSoundEffectsFromBlock` returns `1` at offset 93 whether or not `emitsSounds`, so `nextStep` advances anyway. A port that gates the accumulation — the obvious "if silent, do nothing" — freezes the cadence and puts the first step after un-sneaking in the wrong place; the difference is invisible to a scenario that only sneaks. `Entity$MovementEmission` is `NONE(false,false) SOUNDS(true,false) EVENTS(false,true) ALL(true,true)`, from its `<clinit>`. `crates/rustmc-server/src/step.rs::advance_emitting`.
* 2026-09-05 **A wire-level test of the play loop found two joint defects that every module test passed through.** `crates/rustmc-server/tests/move_correction.rs` drives real `move_player_pos` bytes through `keep_alive_loop`: (a) an edit adding `adopt_join_teleport` to the loop had silently not applied — a `python .replace` whose pattern rustfmt had reformatted, committed without assertion — so the correction carried teleport id 1 where vanilla carries 2, and the corpus compares that field; (b) `Outcome::RotationOnly` fell through to `player::apply`, so `absSnapRotationTo(f, f1); return` at offsets 130-140 took the rotation AND the position, and a rubber-banded client kept walking — the correction was sent and did not stick. Same shape as `session_joint.rs`: the mechanism was ported and gated, and nothing asked whether the wiring did anything.
* 2026-09-05 **`ci/order-selfconsistency.py --check` is red on unmodified `main` b6237f29c and cannot be attributed to a branch.** Its recordings glob is `~/rustmc*/oracle-work/gameplay*/vanilla.json` — the union of every tree on the box — so any lane that records a vanilla run moves the verdict set and reddens T0 for every branch at once. Reproduced by `git archive b6237f29c ci` into a scratch dir: exit 1 there too. Not rewritten, because the script's own header says rewriting the pin to get green is the failure it is shaped against.
* 2026-09-05 `DamageTypeTags` declares **35** `TagKey`s and the pinned jar ships **34** tag files: `minecraft:bypasses_cooldown` has none, and a `TagKey` with no data file is an EMPTY tag in vanilla, so `DamageSource.is(BYPASSES_COOLDOWN)` is false for all 51 damage types and the `&& !source.is(BYPASSES_COOLDOWN)` conjunct at `LivingEntity.hurtServer` offset 196 is **dead in 26.2**. No corpus scenario can exercise that branch with vanilla content. `data/vanilla/damage_types.json` (`ci/damage-types-golden.sh`), pinned by `crates/rustmc-server/tests/damage_types_table.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 Of the **40** mob effects, only **10** ever reach `applyEffectTick`: `MobEffect.shouldApplyEffectTickThisTick` returns `false` in the base class and 5 of the 14 subclasses (`MobEffect`, `Infested`, `Oozing`, `Weaving`, `WindCharged`) never override it. The periodic rule's `i > 0` fallback is not decoration — `25 >> 5` is 0 and the effect then fires EVERY tick, so `d % (base >> amp) == 0` divides by zero. Probed rather than read: `data/vanilla/mob_effects.json` carries 38,720 (effect, duration, amplifier) cells and `crates/rustmc-server/tests/mob_effects_table.rs` compares `crate::effects::TickRule` against all of them.
* 2026-09-05 `LivingEntity.hurtServer`'s invulnerability cooldown is a DIFFERENCE, not a skip: inside it a larger hit than the last still lands, for `damage - lastHurt`, and a smaller or equal one is refused without updating `lastHurt`. 4 then 6 within ten ticks deals 4 then 2. The `fullHit` flag, cleared only by that arm, gates the `damage_event` broadcast, `markHurt`, the knockback and every sound, so a hit landing inside the cooldown moves health and is silent on the wire. `crates/rustmc-server/src/hurt.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `ServerLevel.advanceWeatherCycle` draws THUNDER first then RAIN, each only when its own timer has reached zero, and a timer that reaches zero FLIPS the state without drawing. `UniformInt.sample` is one `nextInt(max - min + 1)`. The two DURATION spans are both 12001, so a spans-only test cannot separate a reversed draw order — only the values can. `crates/rustmc-server/src/weather.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 The `raining` flag leads the `isRaining()` predicate by **20** ticks, not the 21 exact arithmetic gives: twenty `f32` additions of `0.01` land on `0.20000001788139343`, above the `0.2d` threshold, and nineteen land on `0.1900000125169754`, below. A port that kept a tick counter and multiplied announces `START_RAINING` one tick late, every time. `crates/rustmc-server/src/weather.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `ServerPlayer.startSleepInBed` writes the respawn point at offset 132, **before** the second `bedRule.asProblem()` return at 164 — so in a dimension where you may set spawn and may not sleep, vanilla sets your spawn and then refuses. Merging the two `asProblem()` returns into one early test silently stops that. `crates/rustmc-server/src/sleep.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 Fall damage: `calculateFallPower`'s `1.0E-6` is load-bearing (a fall accumulating to a hair under 4.0 floors to 1 with it and 0 without), `Mth.floor` is a floor and not a truncation so a short fall's power is -3 rather than -2, and `Player.causeFallDamage` tests `abilities.MAYFLY` — not `flying` — and returns before the `FALL_ONE_CM` statistic. `crates/rustmc-server/src/falldamage.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `FoodData.tick` offsets 96..324 are ONE `if/else-if` ladder, not four tests: 19 food fully saturated takes the 80-tick arm, never the 10-tick one. The starve floor's third disjunct is a conjunction (`health > 1.0 && difficulty == NORMAL`), and `Player.giveExperiencePoints`'s two normalisation loops each rescale the leftover ACROSS the level change, so 8 points at level 0 lands 1/9 and not 1/7. `crates/rustmc-server/src/{food,experience}.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 **No player-systems mechanic can be scenarioed today, and the reasons are structural rather than unwritten checks.** Fall damage: creative sets `abilities.mayfly` so `Player.causeFallDamage` returns at offset 11, and in survival the harness cannot lift the player because `Step::Walk`'s teleport is accepted only by `handleMovePlayer`'s creative skip of the 0.0625 check (offsets 785..837). Hunger: `ServerPlayer.doTick` resends `set_health` only when health, food level or the saturation-IS-ZERO flag moves, and saturation falling 5.0 -> 4.0 moves none of them. Effects: `/effect` is permission level 2 and the harness players are not operators, and a potion needs a `hold` that `handleSetCreativeModeSlot` refuses for a non-creative player.
* 2026-09-05 `ci/checks/survival` has **no falsifiability ledger and no sweep**: `ci/corpus-falsifiable.py`'s `DEFAULT_CHECKS` is `ci/checks/gameplay` and `DEFAULT_LEDGER` is `ci/checks/gameplay-falsifiable.toml`, and nothing in `ci/t0.sh` reads a survival one. So a survival check carries no tier and none of the five in that directory has ever been shown able to go red. The script accepts `--checks` and `--ledger`, so the missing pieces are the ledger and the T0 hook, not the tool.
* 2026-09-05 `invulnerableTime` is decremented in **two** places and a `ServerPlayer` gets exactly one of them: `LivingEntity.baseTick` offset 475 is `instanceof ServerPlayer; ifne 491`, which SKIPS the decrement for a player, and `ServerPlayer.tick` offsets 21..38 does it instead. Copying `baseTick` alone leaves a player permanently invulnerable after one hit; copying both halves the hurt cooldown from ten ticks to five. `hurtTime`, one branch above at 451..467, has no such exclusion. `crates/rustmc-server/src/breath.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 `ClientboundSetExperiencePacket`'s constructor takes `(progress, total, level)` and its **wire** order is progress, **level**, total — writing the packet from the constructor's argument order transposes two varints. `LivingEntity.decreaseAirSupply` is not `air - 1` either: with a positive `Attributes.OXYGEN_BONUS` it keeps the air with probability `1/(bonus+1)`, drawing one `nextDouble()` from the entity's own random and only then. Measured: a drowning entity from empty takes 2.0 damage five times in a hundred ticks, one every twenty, because -20 IS the threshold and the reset is to zero rather than to the maximum. `crates/rustmc-server/src/{playersync,breath}.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 **Killing a `ci/run-detached.sh` anchor does not kill its payload, and T0's own falsifiers patch source files in place** — `ci/mob-tick-census-falsify.sh:147` and `ci/entity-id-allocator-falsify.sh:64` both inject a `pub const FALSIFY_PIG: EntityKind` into `crates/rustmc-server/src/entities.rs` and revert it afterwards. Killing one detached T0 and starting another in the same `RUSTMC_REMOTE_DIR` therefore leaves the first run's falsifier injecting into the tree the second is compiling: `player-systems` came back `T0 RED — error[E0063]: missing fields track_deltas, tracking_range_chunks and update_interval in initializer of EntityKind at entities.rs:88 (FALSIFY_PIG)` on a tip where that const does not exist in the branch at all. A clean re-sync of the same commit greps 0 occurrences and `cargo clippy --release -p rustmc-server --all-targets -- -D warnings` is clean. **`ci/gate-lock.sh` keys per gate-directory and a detached T0 takes no lock**, so nothing refuses the overlap; do not kill a detached run to start another in the same remote dir.
* 2026-09-05 **`FoodData.tick`'s `difficulty != PEACEFUL` guard is on the FOOD-LEVEL DECREMENT (offsets 10..78), not on the starve arm (238), and this branch's own commit message got it backwards before the test refuted it.** So on the corpus substrate — both passes write `difficulty=peaceful` — a player at food 0 *does* starve, and stops at exactly 10.0 health because the starve gate is `health > 10.0 || HARD || (health > 1.0 && NORMAL)`; but the food level can never reach 0 from exhaustion, because that decrement is the guarded one. Measured: peaceful and easy stop at 10.0, normal at 1.0, hard at 0.0. `crates/rustmc-server/src/playertick.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 **A fall CAN be scenarioed on the survival substrate, which refutes this file's own 2026-09-05 entry saying no player-systems mechanic can be.** `Step::Lurch` sends one `move_player_pos` at any distance and reads the socket afterwards; five blocks is `distanceSq = 25` against `handleMovePlayer`'s `100.0f * i` budget, so a lurch five blocks up with the on-ground bit clear and one back down with it set accumulates `fallDistance` and lands. `Step::Walk` cannot do it: `hop_to` caps at four blocks a packet and applies one `on_ground` flag to all of them, and `Entity.checkFallDamage` offsets 25..98 reset `fallDistance` on every on-ground packet. `ci/checks/survival/006-a-five-block-fall-costs-two-health.toml`.
* 2026-09-05 **Sprinting is unreachable on this server and therefore no exhaustion source that matters is.** Five of `checkMovementStatistics`' eight predicates have no field here; the ground arm charges `0.0f * i * 0.01f` for a walk and a crouch, and `isSprinting()` needs the `SynchedEntityData` dirty path `origin/player-movement` refused. The only non-zero source wired today is `jumpFromGround`'s 0.05f. Measured end to end through `FoodData.tick`: **241** one-block sprints drop a food level, not the 40 the arithmetic suggests, because each 4.0 of exhaustion is spent on SATURATION while there is any and `START_SATURATION` is 5.0. `crates/rustmc-server/src/movestats.rs`.
* 2026-09-05 **`ci/use-golden.sh` cannot answer for a bed and the table says so in a way a reader will miss.** All sixteen `BedBlock` rows in `data/vanilla/use_without_item.json` carry `"refused": "UnsupportedOperationException: UseGolden's level was asked for environmentAttributes"` on every `part=head` state — `BedBlock.useWithoutItem` offset 58 reads `EnvironmentAttributes.BED_RULE` — while the `part=foot` rows record `Success`/`consumes`, which is offset 53's "the block at the head is not this bed" arm and not a sleep. A caller going through `UseTable` gets `Pass` for a bed and falls into the placement path, i.e. right-clicking a bed places a block. Hand-ported at `crates/rustmc-server/src/sleep.rs::bed_use_without_item`.

Transcribed in `crates/rustmc-server/src/javamath.rs` with a 4,000-value control showing the two forms agree everywhere else.

Writing the packet without it means inventing a number. Adding a `registryId` column is the one thing standing between this branch's fall damage and a `damage_event` a scenario could grade.

Keep the exact constants — that costs nothing — but do not write "under test" beside one without asking what granularity the test has.

Fixed with two more arms that can move — stair-against-`StairBlock.base` over a non-zero stair count, and the state denominator against a **different dumper**'s `data/vanilla/block_props.json` — and the script exits 1 on `stairsChecked == 0` because that makes arm 1 vacuous.
