* 2026-09-06 `ServerExplosion.explode()` holds **one** `List` object: `Util.shuffle` permutes it in place at the head of `interactWithBlocks` and `createFire` is handed that same object afterwards, so **the fire pass walks the shuffled order** and which blocks catch fire depends on whether the interaction was `KEEP`. A port that defensively copies the list before shuffling breaks the right blocks and burns the wrong ones, and no assertion about the broken set can see it.
* 2026-09-06 **Editing a `ci/*-falsify.sh` while its own sweep is running corrupts the run silently, and the sweep leaves a mutated source on disk.** `ci/explosion-falsify.sh` mutates `crates/rustmc-server/src/explosion.rs` in place, one patch at a time; inserting ten lines near the top of the script while it ran shifted the byte offset bash was parsing from and the run had to be killed with the tree still poisoned. The repaired shape is worth copying: the **dry arm works on a copy** (`mktemp`) so a T0 that is `kill -9`ed cannot leave a mutated tracked file, the **full arm refuses a dirty source** rather than reporting on a mixture of somebody's edits and its own patch, and `trap ... EXIT` restores from a backup taken before the first patch. Same class as `chain-keeper.sh`/`supervisor.sh` holding their body in memory, but worse, because a shell script is re-read from an offset rather than held whole.
* 2026-09-06 A `ci/*-falsify.sh` whose script computes `ROOT` from its own `dirname` **cannot be run from a copy**, which is the obvious way to avoid the hazard above: `--manifest-path "$ROOT/Cargo.toml"` then points outside the tree, every `cargo test` fails to start, and every case reports CAUGHT — so the declared-MISSED rows fail and the declared-CAUGHT rows pass for the wrong reason. The failure is loud only because this one declares four MISSED outcomes; a falsifier that expects everything to be caught would have gone green from a copy while running nothing.
* 2026-09-06 `ServerExplosion.explode()`'s fire pass is now goldened from vanilla rather than argued from the bytecode: `ci/explosion-ray-golden.sh` reflectively invokes the private `createFire(List)` over **both** orders `explode()` can hand it — the raw `HashSet` order a `KEEP` explosion leaves and the permuted one every other interaction produces — each from the stream state the 1,352 ray draws leave behind. Both the generator and the Rust test refuse a run where none of them moves, because two empty lists agree and that is exactly how that assertion would go quiet.
* 2026-09-06 **`cargo test`'s exit status cannot tell a caught mutation from a build that never ran, and a mutation-sweep that reads it as one number will publish a green it did not earn.** `ci/explosion-falsify.sh`'s first full run reported all four of its declared-MISSED precision cases as CAUGHT — which would have been a genuine finding, "the goldens do pin the float widths after all" — while a gate was compiling on the same 24-thread box. Re-measured one case at a time on a quiet box: all four MISSED. The repair is `cargo test --no-run` as a separate step, with a build failure reported as `INCONCLUSIVE` and exit 3 rather than folded into the pass/fail axis; **any `*-falsify.sh` that decides an injection was caught by looking at one exit status has this hole**, and the ones that expect everything to be caught will never notice, because their false CAUGHT reads as success.

So it really is `Mth.floor`'s granularity against ~1e-7 blocks and not a thin case set.
* 2026-09-24 **`ci/gameplay-substrate-falsify.sh:94` could go red on an unchanged tree**: `code "$f" | grep -q` under `set -o pipefail` returns 141 when `grep -q` exits on its match before `code` finishes writing, so the arm reported that `ci/t2-gameplay-subset.sh` passes no `--console` when line 181 passes one. It failed T0 on `explosions` 6ae8d3b1c (rustmc-gpl-a, threadripper); reproduced at **17 of 2000** loop runs of that pipeline, **0 of 2000** with a `grep` that reads to EOF, which is the fix. The shape `| grep -q` in an `if`/`[` condition appears at **34** more sites across the `ci/*.sh` files that set `pipefail` (`grep -rlE pipefail ci/*.sh | xargs grep -nE "^\s*(if |\[\[? ).*\| *grep -q"`); each is only a flake if its producer writes after the matching line, and none was checked.

* 2026-09-30 `damage`: **`Player.actuallyHurt` does not repeat `LivingEntity.actuallyHurt`'s second absorption subtraction** (LivingEntity offsets 137..144; Player's method ends at `setHealth` 120, stat 143, game event 150). `crate::health::Health::actually_hurt` is the LivingEntity form and the player path uses it, so a player with absorption would lose shield twice. Unreachable in the corpus (nothing grants absorption); named, not fixed.
* 2026-09-30 `damage`: **a no-knockback `/damage` still sends `set_entity_motion`, and one with no source position gets a RANDOM direction.** `markHurt` (hurtServer 349) is gated on NO_IMPACT, not NO_KNOCKBACK, and `ServerEntity.sendChanges` 1202..1235 sends the entity's current velocity to tracking+self on `hurtMarked`; `LivingEntity.knockback` 30..95 re-rolls x,z off the entity's unseeded `random` while `x*x+z*z < 1e-5`, so `/damage <p> 1 minecraft:mob_attack` (no `by`, no `at`) is not reproducible between two vanilla runs. `generic` is in `no_knockback`; only `by`/`at` give a gradeable push.

Fixed on `damage`.
* 2026-09-30 `damage`: **a `ServerPlayer` runs `LivingEntity.travel` on the server every tick, and `physics.rs` said it could not.** `Player.canSimulateMovement` and `Player.isEffectiveAi` are both `!level.isClientSide() || isLocalPlayer()` (Player.class, 26.2) — true on the server — so `LivingEntity.aiStep` 601..617 calls `travel` for every player, and `Entity.move` 564 restitutes a player's velocity after collisions. `crate::physics::Profile::client_authoritative`'s doc read `canSimulateMovement` as `!isClientAuthoritative`, which is `Entity`'s definition and not `Player`'s. * 2026-09-30 `damage`: **vanilla's own velocity after a fall is not reproducible.** Two vanilla runs of `006` sent `set_entity_motion` (0, -0.649, 0) and (0, -0.715, 0): the server's `travel` integrates gravity over however many ticks fall between the two lurch packets, and a 200 ms wait does not pin that count. `006` no longer observes the kind.
* 2026-09-30 `damage`: **a restore by death cannot be used between corpus checks.** `kill` + the client's respawn puts the actor back at the world spawn; the next rig is `(n+1) * 32` blocks out, and walking 200+ blocks starves the observer's keep-alive — vanilla logged `oracle_eye lost connection: Timed out` and 5 of 15 reference scenarios errored (`~/rustmc-damage/oracle-work/gameplay/survival-r2`, 07:30). Instant Health IV (`4 << 4` heal on its one tick) restores health in place.
* 2026-09-30 `damage`: **this server never set `TCP_NODELAY` on a player's socket; vanilla does** (`ServerConnectionListener$1.initChannel` offsets 0..13). Consequence, measured on `008`: a burst of small frames reaches the client in two instalments ~60 ms apart (Nagle against the client's delayed ACK) — a hurt sound written immediately after its `damage_event` was recorded 60 ms later, in the harness's next step. Every corpus timing on this server before `damage` carries it.
* 2026-09-30 `damage`: **`ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.tickPlayer` snaps the player back after `doTick`** (`resetPosition` then `absSnapTo(firstGood…)`, offsets 0..83), so the server's own `travel` of a player changes its VELOCITY and never its position. That is why vanilla's standing player reports `(0, -0.0784, 0)` and still stands where its client says.
* 2026-09-30 `damage`: **vanilla re-syncs `set_entity_data` when a mob effect ends** (`014`: a second one ~340 ms after the hit, as Resistance expires — the effect-particle accessors). This server carries no effect-particle state, so any check that observes `set_entity_data` across an effect's end will diverge; `014` does not observe it.
* 2026-09-30 `damage`: **`ci/order-blind.py`'s verdict depends on which recordings exist on the box, not on the branch.** Its default glob is `~/rustmc*/oracle-work/gameplay*/vanilla.json`; a recording `~/rustmc-train-c` wrote at 15:09 today (two entities heartbeating in one tick of `520`) turned `520` into `CAN-GRADE-ORDER` and reddened `ci/order-blind-falsify.sh` in this branch's T0 without a line of this branch touching it. The falsifier was fixed on main (`b6061e72b`); every branch cut before it, and every branch whose committed `order-blind.toml` predates the box's newest recording, goes red the same way.
* 2026-09-30 **`ServerEntity.sendChanges` sends dirty data LAST and only inside its gate** — the `sendDirtyEntityData` call at offset 205 is inside the `ItemFrame && tickCount % 10 == 0` map arm; the ones every other entity reaches are 411 and 1093, inside `tickCount % updateInterval == 0 || needsSync || entityData.isDirty()` and after the position and velocity packets. train-b's tracker sent `set_entity_data`/`update_attributes` first, every tick, ungated. A sprinting strong hit plays the knockback sound before the hit AND the strong sound after it; train-b's `crate::attack::melee` dropped the strong sound and its test asserted the drop. Fixed `entity-ports` 2a8187bef.
* 2026-09-30 **`FlowingFluid.getShape` never returns `Shapes.block()`** — its arm tests `getAmount() == 9` and no fluid state exceeds 8 — so every fluid clip goes through `shapes.computeIfAbsent(state, s -> box(0,0,0,1,s.getHeight(level,pos),1))`: the first position a fluid state's outline is asked at fixes its height (1.0 under water, 8/9 in the open) for the rest of the server's life. `crate::boat::fluid_shape_height` keeps the cache rather than recomputing.
* 2026-09-30 **`ServerPlayer.swing` resets the attack-strength ticker** (offsets 2-6), and the harness swings in the same tick it attacks, which is why vanilla's player-on-player hit in survival `001` took 0.208 (scale `(0 + 0.5)/5 = 0.1`, `1.0 * (0.2 + 0.1^2 * 0.8)`) and not 1.0. `Player.attack`'s own `onAttack()` resets it again at offset 76. `~/rustmc-damage/oracle-work/gameplay/survival/vanilla.json`, 2026-09-30.

The snow golem is ported to spawn and goals and has no corpus reader.

Vanilla's survival 001 recording carries it (`~/rustmc-entpvp/ent-logs/surv-p1/vanilla.json`); `damage` sent none. Ported as `crate::combat_tracker` on `entity-ports-pvp` f3fdc4139; 001/005 strict diverge -> match (p3).
* 2026-09-30 `diverge-burndown`: the chunk sender was a Chebyshev square of radius `viewDistance`; vanilla's sent set IS `ChunkTrackingView` (`ChunkMap.applyChunkTrackingView` -> `ChunkTrackingView.difference` -> `markChunkPendingToSend`/`dropChunk`), Euclidean with a pad of two over centre +-(vd+1): 117 chunks at vd 4 against 81, and a one-chunk crossing moves 11 not 9. `broadcast.rs` `chunk_is_tracked`'s doc had asserted the sender was "a different question". 548/547 diverge -> match (subset, 612f61c61).

No server change can make these match; they are the rig's to fix (all four already `[[unstable]]` in `ci/checks/gameplay-unstable.toml`). The same run flags `a-daylight-detector-inverts-when-right-clicked` and `a-flower-pot-empties-when-right-clicked` NEWLY UNSTABLE (4/10 pairs each).
* 2026-10-01 `train-e`: `ServerEntity.sendChanges` reaches `sendDirtyEntityData` at offset 205 only from the `ItemFrame`/map arm (jumps 99, 127, 151 land on 204; 69 and 84 skip to 208); every other entity sends it at 1093 inside the `tickCount % updateInterval == 0 || needsSync || isDirty()` gate (250..281). train-b's mob path sent `set_entity_data`/`update_attributes` before the gate on the 205 reading; entity-ports' `94e2becfe` (DirtySource) is the correct one and train-e keeps it (`javap -p -c -constants net/minecraft/server/level/ServerEntity.class`, 26.2 jar).
* 2026-10-01 `train-e`: diverge-burndown's T0 exit 101 (`gate-diverge-burndown-20261001-163430.log:366`, unused `BlockPos` at `wall.rs:41`) hid eleven more compile errors in four test targets, all its own: `mob::tick_all` gained `server_view_distance` with 9 callers in `tests/mob_in_world.rs`/`tests/mob_combat.rs` unchanged, and `placement::Replacing` gained `secondary_use` with 2 test literals unchanged. clippy stops at the first crate error, so a branch's first clippy red is a floor on its count, not the count.

## 2026-08-19

* `docs/FINDINGS.md` is wired from `docs/AGENT-BRIEF.md` as a paragraph and
  **not** as a numbered `## Read first` item, deliberately: `ci/reading-tax.py`
  cross-checks numbered items against `ci/checks/reading-tax.toml`, and this
  file is `action` tier, not `core`. **Whoever merges
  `origin/docs-waiter-t2-blindspot` still owes it a `tier = "action"` manifest
  entry and a row in `## Where everything else is`** — neither file exists on
  `main` yet, so this lane could not add them without duplicating that one.
* The brief gate's `EXPECTED COMMITS` floor no longer blocks. It is a sizing
  check; the arm that now refuses a bug-shaped brief tests the AREA against
  `ci/figures.toml` and the file/number shapes. `hooks/agent-brief-gate.sh` is
  tracked as of today, and `ci/install-hooks.sh --check` reds on drift.
* `MAX_AGENTS` is 4, derived rather than decided: the gate absorbs about 22
  branches a day and 63 remote branch tips moved in the 30 h to 2026-08-19 at 8
  lanes, so 6.3 branches per lane per day and 22/6.3 = 3.5. Use 6 only with
  batching on.
* Three published `det-gradient` figures had **byte-identical windows and
  identical provenance and numbers two points apart** — 97.474607%, 99.621506%,
  99.492021%. The separating conditions (`taskset -c <one core>`, SerialGC)
  were in `docs/DETERMINISM.md`'s table and in no field of `ci/figures.toml`.
  Now carried as `window.jvm`. Found by `ci/figure-units.py`'s
  QUESTION-COLLISION rule on the day it was written.
* `ow-r16-12345-census-n6` quotes a **floor taken at N=5 beside a residual
  taken at N=6**, deliberately and conservatively — and that was recorded only
  in a prose `reason`. It is now `floor_n = "5"` plus a `floor_basis`, because
  a condition written in a sentence is a condition no checker can read.
* A conflict vocabulary built from every noun a unit accepts fires **74 times
  on a ledger with no known defect in it**: "x", " s", "id", "test", "table",
  "target", "less" and "charge" all stand beside numbers in ordinary prose. Only
  a curated *diagnostic* subset can discriminate. Same lesson for proximity —
  "beside the number" has to mean same line, nearest number, or a caveat naming
  two instruments reds.
* **A null result and an empty result must be different words.** Now
  `docs/ORACLE.md` rule 31, with both defects it has produced.
* `ci/handoff.sh` published the literal words **`0 skips`** in its GREEN and
  MERGED verdicts from a counter that is **structurally always 0**. The tree's
  only two shell emitters of `skipping: no vanilla` are called by nothing, and
  the comment claiming `ci/dump-flat-preset.sh` was a live emitter was wrong
  twice: that script prints a different string and then exits 1. Guarantee
  removed; the counter kept as an arm that would fire if an emitter appears.
* `ci/check-comment-guards.py`'s GREP-PATTERN-ONLY-CAPTURED-PRODUCER rule
  needed a **second** narrowing on its first run over `main`: a grep whose
  target is a **source file** is not a grep of a log, and cargo's output
  capture is irrelevant to it (`ci/plugin-hook-cost.sh:70`). Both narrowings
  were found the same way — by the rule reporting something.
* A raw grep for `pkill -f` over `ci/` returns **twelve** hits and **four are
  comments warning against it**. Any class checker over this repo's own shell
  must blank comments and heredoc bodies first, or its ratchet rises every time
  somebody records the lesson. **String literals must NOT be blanked in shell**
  — `N="$(grep -c . f || echo 0)"` is a live instance inside double quotes.
  The Rust rule ("strip comments, strings and heredocs") does not translate
  literally; what translates is "decide what is inert in THIS language first".
* **CORRECTION to the `MAX_AGENTS` line above, same day.** The gate's drain
  rate is **9.4 to 11.6 branches a day**, re-measured 2026-08-19 from the
  gate's own logs — GREEN 107.9 min (n = 4), RED 26.1 min (n = 7) — against an
  `origin/main` that did not move during the measuring lane's run. The 22/day
  it replaces came from `docs/ASSESSMENT-2026-08-19.md` §4.1 pricing a gate at
  66.1 expected minutes, and was **inherited rather than measured** by the lane
  that used it. Rerunning the same arithmetic on 6.3 branches per lane-day:
  9.4/6.3 = 1.5 and 11.6/6.3 = 1.8, so **`MAX_AGENTS` is 2, not 4**.
* **Two lanes measured the gate's drain rate on the same day and got figures
  2x apart**, and the second landed after the first had already been built on.
  This is the shape this file exists for. The hook now carries the derivation
  with both inputs, their dates, and which of the two measurements it rests on,
  rather than the constant alone — because a number with no derivation beside
  it cannot be rechecked when the thing it came from moves, and this one has
  now been wrong twice for exactly that reason.
* **Batching was REFUSED 2026-08-19.** At today's gate costs no width beats
  serial, and the `p = 0.8217 at N = 157` that justified it is **not
  reproducible**: those gate logs are gone, 28 survive, giving 11 verdicts and
  p = 0.3636. Any advice to raise lane count "once batching is on" — including
  an earlier draft of `hooks/agent-brief-gate.sh` — is withdrawn.
* **Wiring a falsifier into `ci/t0.sh` silently disabled one of its own arms.**
  `ci/comment-guards-falsify.sh` arm 4 writes a stand-in emitter into a temp
  tree with `echo "zzfalsify sentinel absent"` inside a heredoc. Once `ci/t0.sh`
  called that script, `script_is_invoked` returned True for it, so the FIXTURE
  counted as a live shell emitter of the sentinel **in the real tree** — and
  arm 3, whose only job is to prove the rule fires when the sole producer is a
  captured Rust print, stopped being able to go red. A check switched off by
  another check citing it, which is that checker's own defect class committed
  against itself. Fixed by blanking heredoc bodies in the shell corpus
  (`ci/shellstrip.py`, now shared with `ci/shell-footguns.py`).
* **A falsifier that hardcodes a line number goes vacuous the first time the
  file it names is edited.** `ci/comment-guards-falsify.sh` arm 5 grepped
  `probe_placement_vs_servers.rs:62` out of the ratchet; on `main` the same
  instance is at `:63`, so it removed nothing and reported the checker as
  toothless — the arm testing itself rather than the checker. Match by
  filename.
* The `pgrep -f` self-match is not only a script defect: **a waiter written as
  `until ! pgrep -f 'x' ; do sleep 10; done` never exits**, because the shell
  running it carries `x` in its own command line. Hit while writing this lane's
  checks for that exact class. Wait on a log line, or on a pid, never on a
  pattern you are yourself named by.
# FINDINGS — one line per finding, append only

Cross-lane finding log. The same defect has been rediscovered independently by
five lanes; a line here is cheaper than a sixth rediscovery. Newest at the
bottom. One line each: what was measured, the number, and where the detail is.

## gate-throughput (2026-08-24)

- `origin/gate-stage-timing` (e8d0e515d, 2026-08-17) is UNLANDED and not an ancestor of main — the gate's own stage-timing instrument has never run in a real gate; zero `STAGE-TIME` lines exist across all 60 logs in `~/rustmc-private/gates`.
- Gate total wall time is recoverable with no new instrument at all: the log FILENAME carries the start (`gate-<branch>-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.log`) and the file MTIME carries the end. n=60.
- Of 60 gate logs only 27 reach stage 1, 21 reach stage 2, 27 reach stage 4 (T0), 13 reach stage 5 (T1) and 11 reach stage 6 (T2). 16 logs carry "stage 6 skipped: an earlier stage was red".
- 20 of the 60 logs are the SAME branch (`gameplay-block-classes`) re-gated 20 times, each dying in 11s — a wedged branch spinning in the chain, not 20 gate attempts on 20 branches. Any per-log average over this corpus is contaminated by it.
- `ci/hold-phases.sh` is an always-on per-phase instrument inside `ci/t2-gameplay.sh` (14 marks) writing `~/.rustmc-hold-phases/phases.tsv`. It already answers "where does stage 6 go" and predates this lane. 498 rows, 101 runs, 2026-08-18..2026-08-24.
- MEASURED, role=gate, n=8: corpus-lock HOLD mean 29.69 min; `reference` phase 35.7% and `subject` phase 77.4% of hold; `ports`/`vanilla-stop`/`rustmc-boot`/`compare`/`teardown` together under 1.2%. Vanilla BOOT is only 0.26 min — the vanilla cost is RECORDING, not booting.
- MEASURED like-for-like at corpus=108, role=gate: refcache miss hold = 43.90 min (n=2), refcache hit hold = 21.82 min (n=4). The reference and subject phases are near-identical (21.74/21.77 and 21.87/21.80) because the gate records the same corpus TWICE, once per side, at ~12.1 s/scenario.
- `RUSTMC_REFERENCE_CACHE` is OPT-IN and OFF BY DEFAULT (`ci/reference-cache.sh:263`). The gate therefore re-records a vanilla reference that is a pure function of (jar, corpus, recorder, substrate) on every default run, inside the box-wide corpus lock.

## stack-ci (2026-09-04)

- Of the 25 ci/docs/gate branches queued, 4 are patch-subsets of another (`boxcopy` ⊂ `land-boxcopy`, `lockwhy` ⊂ `excl2`, `t0-test-target-coverage` ⊂ `t0-assertion-floor`, `tables-refusal-ledger` ⊂ `probe-answering-environment`; `git cherry` + `git patch-id --stable`) and `docs-figures-ledger`'s 12 subjects are all on main already — 20 distinct bodies of work, 15 folded into `stack-ci` (272 patches above f4047ae1f by `git cherry origin/main stack-ci`).
- Every "T0 RED after rebase" in the queue was one of five classes, none a defect in the branch: a check needing a git repo in the rsynced gate copy (check-source-citations, check-cited-commits, claim-number --check, merge-appendonly case 30); a committed diff3 `||||||| base` marker from an earlier auto-rebase (lockwhy/excl2, docs-queue-order-race, end-biome-census, t1-positive-controls, PARITY-ROADMAP §73 duplicated); a ratchet measured on the branch's base that main had moved past (bare citations 87→97→100, unreachable shas 5→8→9, dangling section refs, comment-guards/shell-footguns ledgers); a figure registered without the field a later check requires (unit/question on 16 entries, provenance on 3, floor_n on main's own two spruce figures); and a pin main had already bumped (grit-plugin-api 0.2.1→0.3.0, cb358498c).
- `land-boxcopy` is the enabling branch for the git-less gate copy: it ships a bare object store (`.gitstore`, HEAD only, no refs) — after folding it check-cited-audits and figure-provenance ran for real on the box (stackci-t0-7.log) where they had SKIPPED; a check that needs REFS (reachability) still cannot run there, and check-cited-commits now says so instead of reporting 126 of 130 unreachable.
- `claim-number.py --check` in T0 (claims-allocator 4a58ff3bd) contradicts main's own ci/t0.sh:369 paragraph and cannot run where the gate runs; reverted on the stack, the paragraph stands.
- Two branches carried latent reds of their own: `work-method` made unit/question required in check-figures.py without giving them to check-figures-falsify's fixture (its own tree: "the unmutated fixture is not green"), and its setup-merge-drivers.sh no-git path died on an unbound `$DET_LINE` (found by merge-appendonly-falsify case 30 on the fold).
- Folding branches that each add a `[features]` table to crates/rustmc-server/Cargo.toml yields a manifest cargo refuses (`cargo metadata` exit 101, "duplicate key"); ci/check-t1-controls.py reported it as REFUSED, not RED (stackci-t0-10.log:300) — the instrument was right.

* 2026-09-05 **A control over `java.util.Random` seeded 0..64 cannot see a one-bit draw.** `nextInt(2)` is `(int)((2 * (long) next(31)) >> 31)` — the top bit of a value that moves almost linearly in the seed — and over 64 consecutive small seeds it is **0 every time**. The control asserting "a two-entry loot pool is seed-dependent" therefore failed against an implementation that draws correctly, reporting `produced one item over 64 seeds — it is not drawing`. Spreading the seeds by the golden-ratio odd multiplier fixes it. Any control that varies a seed over a small consecutive range is asking a question `LegacyRandomSource` cannot answer.

With a builtin (`printf '%s' "$OUT" | grep -q`) it is deterministic by size: 0 of 1,000 at 20 KB, 1,000 of 1,000 at 100 KB, so a check flips once its captured output passes the 64 KiB pipe buffer (`bash pf-rate.sh N`, sayer-desktop, GNU grep 3.11). * 2026-09-05 **`tests/golden_chunk_packet.rs`'s "117 of 117 captured chunk packets round-trip" cannot see the section light at all, so it is not the check that grades turning it on.** It decodes vanilla's bytes and re-encodes them; it constructs no `World`, reads no `RUSTMC_SECTION_LIGHT`, and is green whether this server's light is exact or absent. Re-taken and re-run under the new default it is still 117 of 117 and 5,616 containers re-packed, and that green says nothing about the flip. The check that does grade it had to be built: `tests/golden_chunk_light.rs` lights **vanilla's own blocks, decoded out of the same 117 packets**, and compares against the light vanilla shipped in those same bytes. **A round trip is green against any self-consistent encoding; only a comparison against a second, independent producer of the same quantity is a reading.**
* 2026-09-05 **Vanilla's chunk packet carries light that lags its own blocks, and the correction is the `light_update` this project has never recorded.** Two dumps from ONE vanilla server 120 s apart, same seed 31415926, same chunks, same client, graded by the same deterministic engine: the block-light disagreement falls from **5,261 nibbles to 55** while the sky half does not move at all (190 both times). Attribution (`RUSTMC_LIGHT_DUMP=1`): the first sample sits 14 blocks from `minecraft:lava[level=2]` at (-54,-27,-53) whose source `lava[level=0]` is one block further out — fluid ticks spread the lava after the chunk was lit, `LevelChunk.setBlockState` queued the repair on the light thread, and the chunk was serialised with the new block and the old light. **Ours was one brighter, which is exactly one lava block's worth, and ours was right.** Any instrument that reads light out of a chunk capture must settle the server first; `ci/dump-chunks.sh` takes a settle time and says why.
* 2026-09-05 **A capture-derived light figure moves between vanilla SERVER RUNS of the same seed, not only with settledness**: three captures at seed 31415926 carry 190, 190 and **299** sky nibbles of disagreement and 24, 26 and 27 sections vanilla materialised that we do not — which is vanilla's own non-deterministic worldgen showing through into a light comparison. So `golden_chunk_light` asserts a **gross** 0.1% ceiling (2,572 against a measured 245) plus the one invariant no lag can explain — that we never set a mask bit vanilla left unset — and reports everything else. A tight ceiling here would have been a number about one capture.
* 2026-09-05 **`LayerLightSectionStorage` has two sets and only one of them reaches the wire.** `changedSections` drives `swapSectionMap`'s visible-data copy and is added to by `markNewInconsistencies` when a section starts or stops being stored; `sectionsAffectedByLightUpdates` is fed only by `setStoredLevel` through `aroundAndAtBlockPos` and is what `ChunkHolder`'s filters, and therefore the `light_update` mask, are built from. **A block landing in a previously all-air section makes a whole 3x3 of chunks start storing that section** — `updateSectionStatus` walks a 3x3x3 cube — and vanilla names none of them in the packet. A first version of our wire check demanded the mask cover every section whose values moved and was demanding a defect: 9 sky section-5 pairs, one per chunk, from a single torch.
* 2026-09-05 **`ChunkHolder.sectionLightChanged` accepts one section past the last bit the packet reads.** `getMaxLightSection()` is `getMinLightSection() + getLightSectionCount()` and the range test is `sectionY > max`, so a write in section `max` sets bit `getLightSectionCount()` — 26 in the overworld — while `ClientboundLightUpdatePacketData`'s constructor loops `0..getLightSectionCount()`, i.e. bits 0..25, and never looks at it. Ported as written, with the filter one entry longer than the packet window; clamping would differ from vanilla only somewhere currently unreachable.
* 2026-09-05 **`ClientboundLightUpdatePacket` writes its chunk coordinates as VARINTS where `ClientboundLevelChunkWithLightPacket` writes them as fixed-width `i32`s**, and the payload after them is byte-for-byte the chunk packet's light tail (`ClientboundLightUpdatePacketData.write` is the same six writes). A port that reused the chunk packet's writer emits four bytes where vanilla emits one for `(1, 1)` and five for `-1`, and the client reads the sky mask out of the coordinate. Graded by asserting the actual bytes — `[1, 1]` and `[ff ff ff ff 0f]` — because a round trip is green against any self-consistent encoding.
* 2026-09-05 **A partial harvest of a light engine's storage must be keyed by (section, LAYER), and vanilla's own swap is keyed by neither because it copies the whole map.** `swapSectionMap` replaces `visibleSectionData` wholesale whenever `changedSections` is non-empty, which a world-sized region cannot afford per edit; the cheap equivalent is "affected sections plus every stored section never harvested". Both halves of that were wrong first and each cost the identical figure — **17 of 468 section/layer pairs, 69,632 nibbles**: harvesting only the affected set misses sky columns, because `propagateLightSources` *creates* data layers rather than writing them and a created layer is in no affected set; and testing "never harvested" against a section-keyed set lets the block layer's pass hide every sky layer of the same sections.
* 2026-09-05 **`World::light` is no longer a `OnceLock`: the live path repairs the light on every edit and agrees with the graded post-edit instrument at 0 of 1,916,928 nibbles** over 9 chunks and 3 edits (2 torches and a stone block put back where air was, so the decrease queue runs), seed 20260722. The comparison is against `light_incremental::light_region_with_edits` rather than a hand-derived expectation, because that function is the one measured at 2,340 of 2,340 post-edit sections against vanilla's own `LevelLightEngine` — **when a graded instrument already exists, the new plumbing should be required to reproduce it exactly rather than to satisfy a weaker oracle written beside it.**
* 2026-09-05 **The End's 5,609 sky nibbles are a layer created at the wrong MOMENT, not a light value computed wrongly, and the probe that says so is an assertion: 32,768 of 32,768 of vanilla's padding-section nibbles equal OUR converged value at the bottom y-slice of the section above.** `SkyLightSectionStorage.createDataLayer` for a section below the data start returns `repeatFirstLayer` of the nearest stored section above — `if (d.isDefinitelyHomogenous()) return d.copy(); else sixteen arraycopy(src, 0, out, i*128, 128)` — so vanilla's padding layer is a copy of a **lit** section 0 and ours is what propagation left in a layer created before section 0 had any light. The same rule explains why only 8 of 81 scored chunks save the array at all: `d.copy()` of a homogeneous zero layer keeps `data == null`, so `DataLayer.isEmpty()` holds and the serialiser writes nothing.
* 2026-09-05 **Lighting a region one chunk at a time makes the End WORSE, 5,609 -> 14,537 of 1,863,680, while the overworld and the nether stay at 0.** The hypothesis was that our batch driver declares every chunk before propagating any and a server does not; `light_incremental::light_region_per_chunk` (declare, enable, propagate, drain, per chunk, `(x, z)`-ascending) tests it and refutes it. **Do not re-run this as an order sweep** — one order moved the figure the wrong way, `docs/AGENT-BRIEF.md` already records what chasing decoration orders cost twice, and the remaining variable is a chunk *load* order that a saved region file does not record. Writing `repeat_first_layer` of the converged slice at harvest time would produce vanilla's exact bytes by a route vanilla does not take, and is refused on the fidelity rule rather than left unmentioned.
* 2026-09-05 **`ci/checks/gameplay-floor.toml`'s standing corpus floor is STALE PROJECTION on `main`, and has been since before this branch.** Measured by building `oracle-runner` on each tree and asking it: the file pins `proj:7cefde0fb56d9f3e`, `origin/main` computes `proj:1277e43a7e157183`, `stack-worldgen`+`light-engine` computes `proj:abf4647324dc8caf`, and un-dropping `light_update` from the recorder takes it to `proj:acdd89c274c6e587`. The stack's move came from `0b4cd736b trace: decode the block-entity list inside LEVEL_CHUNK_WITH_LIGHT` without the floor being re-derived. **A lane whose change moves `projection-id` should check whether the floor was already stale before reporting that it caused it** — the answer here was that the bill was outstanding.
* 2026-09-05 **The 190 sky nibbles `golden_chunk_light` charges are vanilla disagreeing with vanilla's own rule, not a port defect.** At (-62, 67, 15) vanilla's saved sky light is **15 at an `oak_leaves` block and 15 all the way down to y=63**, where the water starts; `ChunkSkyLightSources.isEdgeOccluded` opens `if (below.getLightDampening() != 0) return true;` and `BlockState.getLightDampening()` for that leaf state is **1** — vanilla's own method, called by `ci/block-props-golden.sh` to build the column. So vanilla's source table for that column stopped at the water four blocks lower, exactly as it would have **before the tree was placed**. Our transcription of `isEdgeOccluded` matches the bytecode line for line and our answer is the one the rule gives. Corroborated by shape: **143 of the 190 sit on a chunk face**, where only 23.4% of a section's columns do (3.2x), and a tree straddling a border is written into this chunk by the *neighbour's* FEATURES stage — `ProtoChunk.setBlockState` runs `ChunkSkyLightSources.update` only past `INITIALIZE_LIGHT` with a non-null engine, the same conditional that made vanilla's own golden record that an 8x8 stone lid darkens nothing. **Second instance in one day of "vanilla's saved light is stale with respect to vanilla's own blocks", by a different mechanism from the fluid-tick one; a light instrument scored against a real server has to expect it.**
- landing (23 Sep): `light-engine-2` showed 421 commits over main; 390 are the pre-landing stack (378 patch-id-equal to main, 12 more subject-equal with rewritten patches), its own are the 31 from `db4e3aed4` to `33e6b2111` (`git cherry origin/main origin/light-engine-2` plus subject match), and `light-engine` (403) is an ancestor of it, so its 13 own commits land with this branch and it needs no landing of its own. Rebased onto main 1049a12d3 with no conflict.
- chunk-streaming: vanilla's tracked set is a padded disc, not a Chebyshev square — `ChunkTrackingView.isWithinDistance` is `max(0,|dx|-2)^2 + max(0,|dz|-2)^2 < vd^2` over a box of `vd+1`; at view distance 10 that is 473 chunks against `world_send::ChunkView`'s 441, and neither set contains the other (`(10,10)` square-only, `(11,0)` disc-only). `crates/rustmc-server/src/trackingview.rs`; `broadcast.rs:114` carried the opposite claim in a doc comment and is corrected in the same commit.
- chunk-streaming: `ChunkLevel.MAX_LEVEL` is **44**, not 33 — `RADIUS_AROUND_FULL_CHUNK` = 11, dumped from a live JVM by `ci/chunklevel-golden.sh` (`FULL=33 BLOCK_TICKING=32 ENTITY_TICKING=31 RADIUS_AROUND_FULL_CHUNK=11 MAX_LEVEL=44`, 19 levels, identical across two runs). A port guessing 33 unloads an eleven-chunk ring vanilla keeps and no value test inside the guess would see it. Fixture `crates/rustmc-server/golden/chunk_level.json`.
- chunk-streaming: `set_chunk_cache_center` was sent on every chunk-border crossing; vanilla's `ChunkMap.applyChunkTrackingView` sends it only when the new view's centre differs from the old one's, so a view-distance change with the player still sends none. `world_send::ChunkView::update_tracking`.
- chunk-streaming: `chunk_batch_received` (serverbound 11) was defined in `rustmc-proto::ids` and handled nowhere — with `maxUnacknowledgedBatches` at 1 until it arrives, a faithful sender that ignored it would deliver one batch and stop. Now handled in `world_send::keep_alive_loop`; the chunk sender runs once per server tick, not once per read-loop iteration, because the quota arithmetic is per call.
- chunk-streaming: `ChunkMap.isChunkTracked` is `view.contains(x,z) && !chunkSender.isPending(pack(x,z))` and is the only filter on `ChunkHolder.broadcastChanges`' recipients — a block change in a queued chunk is withheld, not deferred. `ChunkView::is_chunk_tracked` ports it; the broadcast path in `crate::broadcast` still has no pending half.
- chunk-streaming: nothing in this workspace opened `server.properties` before 2026-09-05 — `view-distance` arrived only as the `--view-distance` argv flag and `simulation-distance` existed nowhere: `play::JoinInfo::default` carried a literal 10 into every `ClientboundLoginPacket`. `crates/rustmc-server/src/properties.rs` reads the two keys (argv > file > default); `status::StatusInfo::simulation_distance` carries it.
- chunk-streaming: `Mth.clamp(int,int,int)` is `min(max(v,min),max)` (javap, `net/minecraft/util/Mth`), so `getPlayerViewDistance(10, 1)` is 1 and not 2 — the server ceiling beats the floor of two. `status::player_view_distance` was already right; `tickets::player_view_distance` is now the single implementation and `status`' delegates to it.
- chunk-streaming: `ci/chunk-stream-probe.sh` GREEN — rustmc at `--view-distance 4 --radius 2 --level-type flat` sends a conforming client **117 chunks in 6 batches**, and 31 in 2 without `chunk_batch_received`. 117 is exactly what real vanilla at `view-distance=4` was measured sending on 2026-08-03 (`ci/chunk-batch-probe.sh` header); a Chebyshev square of radius 4 sends 81. Independent confirmation of the `ChunkTrackingView` port against a vanilla number taken a month before it existed.
- chunk-streaming: `keep_alive_loop` never called `updateChunkTracking` on join, so a player who joined and stood still was sent the 25-chunk join blob and nothing else for ever. Measured 25/1 by the probe before the fix, 117/6 after. `ChunkMap.updatePlayerStatus(player, true)` ends in `updateChunkTracking`.
- chunk-streaming: the gameplay corpus **cannot observe this area at all** — `tools/oracle-runner/src/trace::is_housekeeping` drops `level_chunk_with_light`, `chunk_batch_start`, `chunk_batch_finished`, `forget_level_chunk` and `set_chunk_cache_center` before any decoder arm, so no `ci/checks/gameplay/*.toml` scenario can reach tier 2 on chunk streaming. Refused rather than worked around: the projection is `tools/oracle-runner`'s and widening it changes every one of the 110 recordings. `ci/chunk-stream-probe.sh` is the instrument that could be built without it.
- chunk-streaming: the two simulation radii differ by exactly one and both are derived, not written down — `entity_ticking_radius(sim) = 31 - max(0, 31 - sim) = sim`, `block_ticking_radius(sim) = sim + 1`, saturating at 31/32 above simulation distance 31 (`crates/rustmc-server/src/tickets.rs`). Random ticks use the *entity*-ticking set, per `randomtick`'s own reading of `forEachBlockTickingChunk`.
- chunk-streaming: `randomtick::CHUNK_RADIUS` is 2 where vanilla's is the simulation distance — 25 chunks against 441 at the default, a deliberate narrowing on perf grounds. The setting it needed did not exist until this branch; what closes it is still not a bigger radius but `LevelChunkSection.isRandomlyTicking`'s two counters, which is what makes vanilla's 441 chunks cheap. Pinned by `randomtick::tests::the_random_tick_radius_is_two_where_vanillas_is_the_simulation_distance`.
- chunk-streaming: REFUSED `ChunkMap.processUnloads`/`scheduleUnload`. This server has no per-chunk `ChunkHolder` or `updatingChunkMap`; its unit of residency is a tile, retired on a timer by `Tiles::retain_around`, and the save-on-unload path is `origin/persistence-2`'s (its cold start records `RETIRE_AFTER_SECS` and the block-entity eviction). Porting the unload queue would mean inventing the holder map first. What is portable and is stated instead: the predicate is `ChunkLevel::is_loaded(loading_level)`, i.e. Chebyshev `view_distance + 13` from a player, where `status::retire_unwatched_tiles` uses `max(conn.view_distance, server_view_distance)` — 13 rings short.
- chunk-streaming: a real vanilla 26.2 superflat server sends a joining client **117 chunks and they are exactly `ChunkTrackingView$Positioned(centre, 4)`** — 0 predicted-and-absent, 0 sent-and-unpredicted, against 36 of them outside a Chebyshev square of radius 4. Captured live by `ci/chunk-order-golden.sh` (`ci/regdump.py` with `CHUNK_DUMP`), fixture `crates/rustmc-server/golden/chunk_send_order.json`, gated by `crates/rustmc-server/tests/golden_chunk_send_order.rs`. Red-when-broken control: dropping the pad from 2 to 1 predicts 77 and fails with 40 unpredicted.
- chunk-streaming: vanilla's join stream is **not globally monotone** in `distanceSquared` — it is a concatenation of ascending runs, one per batch, because `ChunkMap.onChunkReadyToSend` queues a chunk as it finishes generating, so a nearer chunk can enter the queue after a farther one has gone out. Two captures: 117 chunks in 11 runs and in 10 runs (`[35, 9, 2, 3, 13, 16, 3, 12, 14, 10]`), with different spawn centres. A sequence assertion would be a check that fails on vanilla's own variation; the run decomposition is asserted instead.
- chunk-streaming: a readiness proof's variable NAMES are load-bearing — `ci/readiness-falsify.sh` `eval`s the shipped source line supplying `WORK`, `OUR_PORT`, `RUSTMC_PID` and `RUSTMC_READY`, so a proof spelled with any other names is not merely untranscribed, it is unexercised, and `set -u` reports it as `PORT: unbound variable`. T0 RED on the first registration of `ci/chunk-stream-probe.sh`; the four-arm sweep now covers it (`readiness-falsify: GREEN`, 4 scripts).
- chunk-streaming: `golden_chunk_send_order` is registered in `t0-wiring`, not `golden-vectors` — its fixture is committed, so `golden-vectors`' binding reason (needs the 3.8 GB `oracle-work/` extract) does not apply. 0.00 s, 2 tests; `check-test-targets: 125 targets declared, 32 run in T0`.
- landing (23 Sep): main carried `crates/rustmc-server/src/chunk_pace.rs` (9fa7dd47b), a second port of `PlayerChunkSender`'s pacing referenced by nothing but its `pub mod` line; chunk-streaming's `chunksender.rs` is the wired port of the same class with the same four constants and the same `onChunkBatchReceivedByClient`/quota arithmetic line for line, and it closes `docs/cold-start/chunk-send-path.md` open item 1 (the per-tick pump). Two unreconciled copies of one class is the `mod ids` shape `player.rs` already tests against, so the unwired one is deleted on chunk-streaming: `grep -rn chunk_pace crates` → 0 hits after.
- landing (24 Sep): `PlayerChunkSender.collectChunksToSend` compares on `distanceSquared` alone (`Comparator.comparingInt`, offsets 66..74 and 105..110), so equal-distance chunks go out in `pendingChunks`' `LongOpenHashSet` spliterator order (zero key first, then slots 0..n ascending; the iterator walks the other way), throttled through Guava 33.6's `TopKSelector`; neither chunk-streaming's (x, z) nor main's (z, x) is vanilla's. Ported and replayed against 5,167 collects recorded from the jar's own private method (`ci/chunk-send-ties-golden.sh`): 0 differ; (x, z) 2,361, (z, x) 2,369, iterator-direction 2,561, sort-and-truncate for `TopKSelector` 1,863, no shrink-on-remove 775 of 5,167 differ. `cargo test -p rustmc-server --lib chunksender`, ~/rustmc-eng-b, threadripper.
- entity-tracker: `ChangedBlocks::flush` sent **every block change in the world to every connected player** — `Players::broadcast` has no filter of any kind, and `world_send::ChunkView::is_chunk_tracked` had been ported with a test and was called by nothing, because the view lived on the session thread's stack (`Session.view: &mut ChunkView`) and the tick's broadcast runs on the clock thread. Closed by moving the view onto `players::Connection` as a `Mutex<ChunkView>`, which is where vanilla keeps both its halves (`ServerPlayer.chunkTrackingView` and `ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.chunkSender`); four production call sites, all in the read loop and none under `broadcast::tick_lock`, so it stays a leaf lock. `broadcast::a_block_update_reaches_the_client_holding_its_chunk_and_no_other`: 1 frame to the near client, 0 bytes to a client 40 chunks away, where before both got 1.
- entity-tracker: `ChunkHolder.broadcastChanges` resolves its recipient list **once per chunk column**, at offsets 111-125, and re-uses it for every section — so `getPlayers` is a per-`ChunkHolder` question and not a per-packet one. Our `BTreeMap` key `(x, z, y)` already delivers a column's sections contiguously and ascending, so the grouping is free; `broadcast::two_columns_resolve_two_recipient_lists` is the control that the list is not resolved once for the whole flush.
- entity-tracker: `ChunkMap.getPlayers(pos, boundaryOnly)`'s `true` arm is `isChunkOnTrackedBorder` — tracked, **and** at least one of the eight neighbours not tracked (offsets 0-74, centre skipped at 30-40) — and its only caller in the jar is `broadcastChanges`' **light** update at offsets 33-47; the block half at 111-125 passes `false`. Ported as `Connection::is_chunk_on_tracked_border` and reached by nothing yet, because this server sends no `light_update` outside a chunk body. `players::only_the_rim_of_a_view_is_on_the_tracked_border` measures both arms: at view distance 4 the centre chunk is 1 block-update recipient and 0 light recipients.
- entity-tracker: `TrackedEntity.getEffectiveRange` ends in `scaledRange`, which is `MinecraftServer.getScaledTrackingDistance` — the **identity on the base class and `entityBroadcastRangePercentage() * range / 100` on `DedicatedServer`** (offsets 0-9). This server is a dedicated one and had no scaling at all. Ported as `tracker::scaled_range` with the property read live off `Tracker` (vanilla's is a mutable `Settings$MutableValue`). At the default 100 the two are identical, which is why it could be missing for a month with no number moving: `tracker::the_broadcast_percentage_changes_who_is_paired` puts the boundary at 208%/209% for a 96-block item range at 200 blocks.
- entity-tracker: `entity-broadcast-range-percentage` is clamped **where it is parsed**, not where it is used — `DedicatedServerProperties.<init>` registers it through `getMutable(String, Function, Integer)` at offsets 670-686 whose function is `lambda$new$1` = `Mth.clamp(parseInt(s), 10, 1000)`, default 100 (`bipush 100` at offset 678). `view-distance` is the other way round: unclamped at parse, clamped in `ChunkMap.setServerViewDistance`. The difference is observable — an operator who writes 5 gets 10 and nothing later corrects it. `properties::the_broadcast_percentage_is_clamped_at_parse_time`, with the two distances as the control that the clamp is this key's alone.
- entity-tracker: `ChunkMap.move(player)` runs `updatePlayer` for **every** tracked entity on every move, and `updatePlayers(level.players())` for the moving player's own tracker (offsets 0-64) — where `ChunkMap.tick()`'s `movedPlayers` sweep fires only on a **section** change. So vanilla re-pairs on the move packet and again on the tick; this server has only the tick half (`tracker::Tracker::tick`). Not a wrong packet, a later one: the pairing arrives at the next tick boundary rather than inside the handler. Left as a stated deviation, not fixed, because the shape it needs is a `ChunkMap.move` call from `keep_alive_loop`'s move arm and that is a change to the tracker's cadence rather than to its content.
- entity-tracker: `ChunkMap.tick()` opens with `for (ServerPlayer p : playerMap.getAllPlayers()) updateChunkTracking(p);` — an unconditional per-tick chunk-tracking update for every player, **before** the entity loop (offsets 0-40). This server drives `update_chunk_tracking` from each connection's own read loop instead, on a chunk-border crossing plus once at join. Same packets, different thread and a different point in the tick.
- entity-tracker: `TrackedEntity.updatePlayer`'s third limb (offsets 94-122) is `ChunkMap.isChunkTracked`, i.e. **`contains && !isPending`**, and this server had the geometry half only (`broadcast::chunk_is_tracked`) because the pending set lived on the session thread's stack. So an entity in a chunk the client had been promised and not sent was paired anyway. Closing it makes the join's order load-bearing: `ChunkMap.addEntity` runs `updatePlayers` (99-108), then `updatePlayerStatus(player, true)` (124-128), then the `entityMap` sweep (131-186), and this server left the middle step to `keep_alive_loop` — a sweep against `ChunkTrackingView.EMPTY` shows the newcomer nothing. `tracker::an_entity_in_a_chunk_the_client_has_not_been_sent_is_not_paired` asserts three states: queued 0, delivered 1, six chunks outside a delivered view 0.
- entity-tracker: `ChunkMap.addEntity` **throws** `IllegalStateException("Entity is already tracked!")` on a duplicate id (offsets 45-64, through `Util.pauseInIde`); `Tracker::add` uses `get_or_insert_with` and silently reuses the existing tracker, which would carry a stale `range` and a stale `seenBy`. Not a packet difference in normal operation — nothing here allocates an id twice — and left as it is rather than made a panic, but it is the one place our map disagrees with vanilla's about what a duplicate means.
- entity-tracker: `ServerEntity.sendChanges` has exactly **one** `sendToTrackingPlayersAndSelf`, the `hurtMarked` velocity packet at offsets 1203-1240; the `trackDeltas` velocity packet at 993-1041 is plain `sendToTrackingPlayers`. So the two `set_entity_motion` arms have **different audiences** — one reaches the entity's own client and the other cannot, because `seenBy` never contains it. This server implements the second and not the first (`hurtMarked` is in `ServerEntity`'s stated not-modelled list), so the distinction is unreachable today and is written down before something sets `hurtMarked` and quietly picks the wrong helper.
- entity-tracker: `ChunkMap.removeEntity`'s order is confirmed as ours — for a player, `updatePlayerStatus(p, false)` then `removePlayer` on **every** tracker (offsets 18-59), and only then the map removal and `broadcastRemoved` (62-87). `status.rs` does `forget_observer`, `remove`, `entities.remove`, `players.leave` in that order and it matches, including the reason `players.leave` is last: taking the connection out first would make `tracked_players` empty and leave a frozen copy of the departed player on every other client.

- train-c (30 Sep): `ci/unfalsifiable-falsify.py` `die()`d on an empty dead-field set, which main has had since `mob-in-world`; latent on main because no merge gate carried a recording pair. Now the named SKIP the file already gives a dead set with no widenable observer — so its case 2 does not run on today's corpus, and that is stated in its output, not hidden.
- train-c (1 Oct): `ci/unfalsifiable-falsify.py` held its controls to the tree's pinned `DEAD_FIELDS`, but a gate directory's recording pair is whatever its last gate recorded — on 2026-09-30 it swept to 4 dead against a pinned `()`, so 2b'/3d went red on a set they are not about (gate-train-c-20260930-210226). A tree whose own pair agrees with its pins cannot see this; reproduce on a copy of the gate dir's pair. Fixed by pinning the dead set from the baseline sweep of the pair present.
- land-batch-1 (1 Oct): main's `mobs_in_a_flat_world` (its silent-pig step, `crates/rustmc-server/tests/mob_in_world.rs::an_egg_carrying_silent_entity_data_spawns_a_pig_that_never_sounds` (`:240`), added by 0ae84efee) is flaky on main itself: **8 of 40** runs of main's release binary red, 4 of 20 on land-batch-1 (`a silent pig never sounds`, left 2). It told the silent pig's sounds from the rig's other pigs by window (x 5..8, z 1.5..3.5) on the premise that pig one walks along z = 0.5, but the walk runs on the unseeded level random; a failing run showed `entity.pig.step` at (5.75, -60, 2.875) from the other pig. Fixed by silencing every other mob through the same `DATA_SILENT` and asserting zero SOUND frames: 0 of 30 red; red with the egg's apply removed (its SILENT assertion) and with `crates/rustmc-server/src/mob/living.rs::play_sound` (`:165`)'s gate removed (29 sounds).
- land-batch-1 (1 Oct): `ci/unfalsifiable-falsify.py`'s 2b'/3d controls go red on a gate directory's recording pair whenever the branch adds a scenario — the pair is main's corpus, so `check` refuses the new file as unswept (531-a-block-broken-out-of-view-is-not-broadcast: 180 files, 179 recorded). The same trap as the DEAD_FIELDS pin one level up. The falsifier now cuts its corpus copy to the scenarios both recordings carry and prints the cut; tree completeness stays `corpus-falsifiable.py --check`'s job.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: the creative-slot stack's component patch is `DataComponentPatch.DELIMITED_STREAM_CODEC` (each value behind a varint byte count); `crate::player::apply_hand` read none of it, and the first reader written here read it without the prefix, so a harness pickaxe at damage 200 (`c8 01`, prefix `02`) became damage 2 — recording r1 of 821/822/823/830-832/851 diverged on it, r3 matched after the fix (`30f5d0e05`, `tests/workstation_menus.rs::a_creative_slot_reads_a_length_prefixed_damage`).
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: `Player.enchantmentSeed` is 0 for a player with no save file (`Player.<init>` offset 63; `readAdditionalSaveData` redraws only a saved `XpSeed` of 0) and every corpus run boots a fresh world, so enchanting-table offers are reproducible — 840/841 match vanilla on all ten data slots. After one enchant `onEnchantmentPerformed` redraws it from the unseeded `Player.random`, so the seed data slot (id 3) and every later offer are unrecordable, vanilla against itself included: 843 observes no `container_set_data` for that reason.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: `ItemEnchantments` is an `Object2IntOpenHashMap<Holder<Enchantment>>` and `Holder.Reference` has identity hashing, so the wire order of a stack with two or more enchantments is not reproducible between two vanilla runs; a check observing such a stack cannot match by construction. Every enchanted stack in 810-871 carries one.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: the stonecutter's button `n` is entry `n` of the stonecutting recipes for the input **in recipe-id order** — `RecipeManager.prepare` scans into a `java.util.TreeMap` and `RecipeMap.byKey` is an insertion-ordered `ImmutableMap`; 811/812 (buttons 9 and 5 over stone) match.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: `crate::click`'s PICKUP arm never called `Slot.onTake` (`lambda$doClick$0`/`$1`); it now does for workstation result slots only. A crafting table's `ResultSlot.onTake` — which consumes the grid — is still not called, so taking a crafted item leaves the ingredients in the grid on this server; no scenario takes a crafting result, so nothing measures it.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: `ResultSlot.onTake` (offsets 0-268) consumes the grid one `TransientCraftingContainer.removeItem` per cell, and each removal runs `slotsChanged` on the spot, so taking a crafted result sends one direct `container_set_slot` for slot 0 **per consumed cell** (two for a cell that also gets a remainder), all before `broadcastChanges`. Ported in `crate::click::crafting_result_on_take`; 800/801 are the checks. Closes the gap in the line above.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: `ResultContainer.removeItem(int, int)` is `ContainerHelper.takeItem(itemStacks, 0)` and never reads the count, so a right-click (`(n + 1) / 2`) or a single `THROW` on a crafting, stonecutter, anvil, smithing, grindstone or cartography result takes the **whole** stack; the loom's `LoomMenu$2` is a `SimpleContainer` and splits. This server split all of them. 803 is the check.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: `canTakeItemForPickAll` is overridden to refuse the result slot by `CraftingMenu`, `InventoryMenu`, `SmithingMenu`, `StonecutterMenu` and `CartographyTableMenu` (and `MerchantMenu` refuses everything) — but **not** by `AnvilMenu`, `GrindstoneMenu`, `LoomMenu` or `ItemCombinerMenu`, so a double-click sweeps an anvil's result through `safeTake` and its `onTake`. `crate::click` returned `true` for every slot ("no menu this server opens overrides it" — false since the crafting table). 804/805 are the checks.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: `Slot.safeTake` calls `onTake` (offsets 9-18); `crate::click::safe_take` did not, so a `THROW` from any result slot left its inputs. 802 is the check.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: `SmithingTrimRecipe.applyTrim` returns EMPTY when the base already carries the identical `ArmorTrim` (`Objects.equals`, offsets 38-51), so `hasRecipeError` reads 1 with a recipe matched; `TRIM` is two `ByteBufCodecs.holder` refs (id + 1) into the key-sorted `trim_material` (11) and `trim_pattern` (18) registries. 854/855 are the checks.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: cartography results are unreachable without map saved data — `CartographyTableMenu.lambda$setupResultSlot$0` returns at offset 13 when `MapItem.getSavedData` is null, and this server has no `MapItemSavedData`; refused as a menus item, it is a maps port. Survival XP costs (anvil `mayPickup`/`giveExperienceLevels`, enchanting levels) are refused too: the creative substrate cannot leave creative (no `gamemode` command on this server) and the survival substrate cannot `hold`, so no scenario could reach either branch.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: a crafting grid recomputes **per storage write**, not per click — `TransientCraftingContainer.setItem`/`removeItem` each run `slotsChanged`, and `Slot.tryRemove` that empties a cell does both (`remove`, then `setByPlayer(EMPTY)` at offsets 61-76), so picking a log back off the grid sends slot 0 **twice**. `crate::click`'s `grid_written` flag recomputed once after the click; it is gone, the recompute is inline in `Slots::set`, and `moveItemStackTo`'s merge phase (`setCount` + a no-op `setChanged`) writes without one. 808 is the check.
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: `CraftingMenu.quickMoveStack` (offsets 0-233) was `QuickMove::NotImplemented`: a shift-click on the result now crafts under `doClick`'s loop until the grid runs out (806), and a shift-clicked stack goes to the grid first. The SWAP arm's `slot.onTake` (offsets 1199, 1379) was missing too, so a number key over a result left its inputs (807).
* 2026-10-01 `menus-workstations`: `CartographyTableMenu.quickMoveStack` (offsets 0-297) routes a `MAP_ID` stack to slot 0 and paper / an empty map / a glass pane to slot 1, otherwise the hotbar/main shuffle; it was `QuickMove::NotImplemented`. 864 is the check. The cartography table's **results** stay unported (map saved data, see above).
* 2026-10-01 `fluids`: `Level.setBlock` guards `sendBlockUpdated`, `updateNeighborsAt` and all three `updateNeighbourShapes` calls on the position still holding the state just written (offsets 56-64, `if_acmpne 241`), so when `onPlace` rewrites its own position (lava beside water -> obsidian, a rail bending) the outer write does nothing further; `crate::shapes::set_block` ran all three with the stale state and its comment said vanilla does too. Fixed in `crates/rustmc-server/src/shapes.rs` (set_block, compared by state id); rail scenarios (300) can move.
* 2026-10-01 `fluids`: the 2026-09-05 line above saying `ServerLevel.tick` runs `LevelTicks.tick` (fluid) before (block) is reversed: `blockTicks.tick` is at offset 269 and `fluidTicks.tick` at 296, both after the one `getGameTime()` at 240 (`javap -c net/minecraft/server/level/ServerLevel.class`, method `tick(BooleanSupplier)`). `crate::ticks::ScheduledTicks` drains block then fluid.
* 2026-10-01 `fluids`: `canBeReplacedWith` is not symmetric between the liquids: `WaterFluid` yields only to `direction == DOWN && !fluid.is(WATER)`, `LavaFluid` yields to water from ANY side once `getHeight >= 0.44444445f` (amount 4+) and never reads the direction; the unlanded water-only port (`origin/fluid-tickqueue-bucket`) mirrored water's rule onto lava. `crates/rustmc-server/src/fluid.rs` `can_be_replaced_with`.
* 2026-10-01 `fluids`: every one of the 49 classes in `net/minecraft/world/level/block/` implementing `SimpleWaterloggedBlock` reaches an `updateShape` that schedules a `Fluids.WATER` tick (44 directly, 4 signs through `SignBlock.updateShape` after their survival test, `CrossCollisionBlock` via `FenceBlock`/`IronBarsBlock`), but `update_shape.json` cannot show it for rows whose answer is the block itself; the rule is keyed on `waterlogged=true` in `crate::shapes::shape_after`; the scan is one `javap -c` pass over the 326 top-level block classes, resolving each `updateShape` up the superclass chain.
* 2026-10-01 `fluids`: a bucket ray into a one-deep hole has to cross y = 0 inside the hole's own column at 1.62/2.62 of the way to the floor, so from a corpus anchor only columns |dx|, |dz| <= 1 can be filled; every fluid rig in 161-168 is dug at dx 0..1, dz 0..3 so all writes share one chunk section and the cross-chunk broadcast order that parked 160 never arises.
* 2026-10-01 `fluids`: `LavaFluid.getSpreadDelay`'s x4 (when a non-falling flow rises, p = 3/4) never reaches the queue: `FlowingFluid.tick` writes the new state with `setBlock(..., 3)` first, `LiquidBlock.onPlace` inside that write books `(pos, type)` at `getTickDelay`, and the tick's own `scheduleTick(pos, type, i)` two instructions later is dropped by `LevelChunkTicks.schedule`'s `ticksPerPosition.add` — only the `nextInt(4)` draw on the level random survives. Asserted in `crates/rustmc-server/src/fluid.rs` (`the_first_booking_wins_so_lavas_slow_delay_is_dead`).
* 2026-10-01 `fluids`: a `timeout 10800` around `ci/t2-gameplay.sh` is shorter than the corpus-lock queue on this box today — the run waited 177 min (03:52 -> 07:09, queue 9-10 deep with a merge gate jumping it) and was killed before recording a scenario, forfeiting its place; the timeout has to cover the wait, so the chained retry uses 43200 s. `~/.rustmc-detached/fluids-full.log` lines 1040-1096.

A T0 red on that one case is not evidence about a branch. `ci/t1-par-falsify.sh:88`.

Gate log `~/rustmc-private/gates/gate-fluids-20261001-171544.log` lines 901-913.

* 2026-10-01 `fluids`: the 17 BETTER rows in the fluids gate's T2 (6 redstone among them) are main's, not fluids': `gate-structures-live-20261001-175502.log`, gated at main 1b2dfe629 itself, prints the identical 17 (`diff` of the two `^BETTER` blocks is empty) at declared 147/179, and fluids is 156/188 = the same 147 plus its own nine. `ci/checks/gameplay-baseline.toml` on main still holds all 17 at diverge/absent; locking them in belongs to main, not this branch.
* 2026-09-30 `mining`: vanilla's `ServerPlayerGameMode.tick` never destroys a block a client is still digging — the `isDestroyingBlock` arm (offsets 81-152) only displays progress, with no cap: vanilla's observer stream for survival `002` reached stage **48** (`~/rustmc-damage/oracle-work/gameplay/survival/vanilla.json`, 2026-09-30). Only STOP (>= 0.7) or the `hasDelayedDestroy` arm destroys. So main's survival `002` (actor-only, no STOP) was inert and `003` (no STOP) vacuous by construction; both reworked on `mining` (8101a5f67).
* 2026-09-30 `mining`: survival `004`'s name is contradicted by vanilla — an early STOP is acked at once and the block breaks **later**, ~520 ms after the ack in the same recording (block_update at 901 ms, ack at 382 ms). Name kept (published), `about` corrected.
* 2026-09-30 `mining`: this server made survival mining instant — `crates/rustmc-server/src/world_send.rs:943` on main destroys on any survival `STOP_DESTROY_BLOCK` with no START, no clock and no reach check; replaced by `crate::mining`'s state machine (3f08df15d).
* 2026-09-30 `mining`: `world_send.rs:987` on main acked every `player_action` but DROP_ITEM/SWAP — including RELEASE_USE_ITEM, STAB and DROP_ALL_ITEMS, which `handlePlayerAction`'s tableswitch returns from without `ackBlockChangesUpTo`. Only the three digging arms ack now (3f08df15d).
* 2026-09-30 `mining`: a survival block placement never consumed the held item (`world_send.rs:2561` on main, no `consume(1, player)` after `place_sound`), so a rig that placed a block and then took a tool from the console dug bare-handed — the tool landed in slot 1. Fixed in 1793dfffc.
* 2026-09-30 `mining`: `crates/rustmc-server/src/attack.rs:151` computes `AABB.distanceToSqr` as `dx.mul_add(dx, dy.mul_add(dy, dz * dz))` — a fused multiply-add, which rounds differently in the last bit from vanilla's `dmul; dmul; dadd` and is exactly what `playerlist.rs:93-102` documents as wrong for a range test. Not touched (combat lane); `crate::mining::within_interaction_range` writes it unfused.
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: a harness `walk` with `on_ground = false` does NOT make vanilla's survival digger airborne — after a quarter-block walk with the flag clear, vanilla's crack overlay climbed at the ground rate (stage 14 by 1.2 s) and the block broke at STOP (`~/mining-rec/r2-1001-0105/survival-vanilla.json`, `an-airborne-digger-is-five-times-slower`). This server trusts the packet's flag (`player.rs` `apply_flags`), so `Player.getDestroySpeed`'s `/5` arm fired here and not there. Scenario 043 withdrawn; the `/5` stays unit-tested only. A movement-lane question: where vanilla's `onGround` comes from for a survival player.
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: `Hotbar::add` (`crates/rustmc-server/src/player.rs`, `self.set_stack(slot, ItemStack::new(&item, have + take))`) rebuilt every inventory cell from the item name, so any stack arriving through `Inventory.add` lost its components — survival 048/049/050 recorded a pickaxe that never broke, Efficiency that did nothing and Silk Touch that dropped dirt. Fixed on `mining` (06b113347).
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: vanilla sends a worn tool's `container_set_slot` at two different points depending on which path destroyed the block: a STOP-path destroy puts it after the block_update and the drop's second `set_entity_data` (end-of-tick sweep), a tick-path (delayed) destroy puts it right after the drop's pairing bundle and BEFORE the block_update, which lands a tick later (`~/mining-rec/r2-1001-0105/survival-vanilla.json`, 047 vs 048/050).
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: adding two creative checks early in the corpus (`011`/`012`) turned `other-player-is-an-entity` from `match` to `diverge` in 3 of 3 runs on this branch and 0 of 8 other trees' recordings: the actor's stream gained a forced `entity_position` for the standing observer inside 080's window — `ServerEntity`'s `teleportDelay > FORCED_TELEPORT_PERIOD` (`tracker.rs:263`) resync, whose phase is set by elapsed corpus time, so every scenario inserted ahead of 080 slides the 400-update boundary across later windows. Vanilla did not send one in the same window, so the two servers' phases differ; a recording of 080 alone, or after only 010-012 with no vanilla, shows none (probe, `~/rustmc-mining-chk`). The new mining checks moved to 990-992, after every existing check but `999`. The tracker phase is the open defect.
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: vanilla refuses a piston placed at `[-1, 0, 0]` from a player standing on the anchor (x = z = 0.0, the block corner): the player's box overlaps that block and `BlockItem.canPlace`'s `isUnobstructed` says no; this server, with no entity sweep (`world_send.rs`, named at the `cannot_survive` gate), placed it. Gameplay 992 places two blocks out.
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: a survival double plant's drop does not come from `playerDestroy` (which this server ran for every block): `DoublePlantBlock.playerDestroy` offsets 0-17 rolls `Blocks.AIR`'s table (nothing), and the drop is rolled in `playerWillDestroy` offsets 26-37 — before the dug block's 2001 and before `removeBlock`, and ungated by `hasCorrectToolForDrops`. Ported at `crates/rustmc-server/src/dig.rs:354`; unit-tested only (no survival recording yet).
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: four classes override `playerDestroy` in 26.2 (`data/vanilla/block_mining.json`: DoublePlant 10 blocks, Ice 2 incl. `frosted_ice`, Beehive 2, TurtleEgg 1); ice now melts to a water source over a block that `blocksMotion()` or is `liquid()` (water, lava, bubble_column only, `Blocks.<clinit>`), unless the tool carries a `#prevents_ice_melting` enchantment; a turtle-egg clutch loses one egg with a 3-arg 2001. Dispatch at `crates/rustmc-server/src/dig.rs:424`.
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: a block destroyed by a shape cascade (a torch whose support was dug, a double plant's other half, a piston base) dropped nothing on this server in survival AND creative — `shapes::destroy_block` never read `dropBlock` ("nothing to drop into"). Vanilla's `Level.destroyBlock` rolls `dropResources(state, level, pos, be, null, EMPTY)` between the 2001 (offset 45) and the air write (94), so a creative dig under a torch spawns a torch item too. Now a request on `Effects::drops` (`crates/rustmc-server/src/shapes.rs:2064`), spawned interleaved with level events at the session, scheduled-tick and piston drains (`crates/rustmc-server/src/dig.rs:683`). Unit-tested only.
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: `RemoveBinomial.process` (Unbreaking's only effect) is not a plain per-point loop: past 128 points with both `value*chance` and `value*(1-chance)` at least 20 it takes ONE `nextGaussian` (`round(floor(v*c) + g*sqrt(v*c*(1-c)))`, clamped to `[0, (int)v]`), offsets 15-131; and Unbreaking's armour arm is `(2+2(L-1))/(10+5(L-1))`, not `1/(L+1)` (`data/vanilla/enchantments.json`). Ported at `crates/rustmc-server/src/item_damage.rs:92`, draws from a fresh level random (named deviation).
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: a crafter placed next to a powered source came out `triggered=false` on this server; `CrafterBlock.getStateForPlacement` offsets 105-122 set `TRIGGERED = level.hasNeighborSignal(clickedPos)` at placement, before any neighbour update. Set at `crates/rustmc-server/src/world_send.rs:2370` through `power::Redstone::has_neighbor_signal`; gameplay 981 records it.
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: `playerWillDestroy` overrides run their own body BEFORE `super`'s `spawnDestroyParticles` 2001, so whatever they write or announce leaves first; this server sent the dug block's 2001 ahead of the creative door/bed/double-plant cascades. Reordered, and two more overrides ported at `crates/rustmc-server/src/dig.rs:417`: `BaseFireBlock` (level event 1009 to everyone, digger included) and `PistonHeadBlock` (creative: `destroyBlock(base, false)` — without it the cascade-drop port would have dropped a piston for a creative head dig). The class string is `piston.PistonHeadBlock` after the package strip, caught by `the_extract_names_fire_and_the_piston_head_as_will_destroy_classes`.
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: survival 054 (silk touch keeps ice) diverged in both c7 passes (`~/mining-rec/c7-1001-1524`): this server wrote water. `ItemStack` stores enchantment ids bare (`silk_touch`) while the registry and its tags are namespaced, so `#prevents_ice_melting` never matched — and Unbreaking's `registry.get` missed the same way, so it never applied in-game while its unit test, written with namespaced ids, passed. Both now go through `item_damage::namespaced`; the registry test uses a bare id.
* 2026-10-01 `mining`: survival 048 (worn-out pickaxe) diverged in c7 pass 1 only: vanilla picked up the dirt dropped at `[1, -1, 0]` (`container_set_slot` 36). Its rig now digs three blocks out like 046/047/049/050.
